#CH 1
#TITLE: Chapter 1: Web Novelist Hit by Online Hate
Morning sunlight pushed through the gap in the curtains, spilling a thin shaft of light across a twenty-second-floor bedroom in a high-rise apartment.
The curtains were purple. Sunlight from outside sharpened the hue, tinting the bedroom air with a pale violet glow.
The room was plain and neat. Qianli Xun lay fast asleep on the bed, messy hair splayed across the pillow, quilt pulled up to her neck, letting out soft snores.
A phone rang on the nightstand, playing a current pop hit.
Without opening her eyes, Qianli Xun reached toward the nightstand, groped around for a moment, and grabbed the phone. Still eyes shut, she asked in a thick, drowsy voice, "Hello? What is it?"
Her apprentice Er'gang's frantic voice burst through the speaker: "Master! How are you still asleep? Open your phone right now and watch the video I sent you—everyone online is tearing you apart!"
Qianli Xun snapped wide awake and sat up. "What? Everyone online is tearing me apart? Why?"
"See for yourself! I told you ages ago to stop writing web novels, and you wouldn't listen. Stay indoors for a while and don't go out. Hanging up!"
As she sat up, the quilt slid off her shoulders, revealing pale purple pajamas. She opened her chat app and tapped Er'gang's profile picture—the God of Wealth, since the guy dreamed of getting rich day and night. Sure enough, a video link sat there. She tapped it, and a woman's voice rang out:
"Numerous netizens have exposed female web novelist Qianli Xun for plagiarizing others, recycling her own plots, and padding her chapters with fluff. She has lost the spark of her early work and churns out formulaic stories that fail to hold readers' interest..."
Qianli Xun frowned and shut off the video, refusing to listen to another word. "I've ruled the web-novel world for ten years! Who are you to spout nonsense about how I write?"
She tossed the phone aside, climbed out of bed, slipped into her slippers, and walked to the kitchen. She pulled a bottle of milk from the fridge and poured half a glass.
Cold milk straight from the fridge was bad for the stomach—she knew that much. Too lazy to heat it on the stove, she picked up the thermos next to her and topped the glass off with hot water until it felt just right. She raised the glass, gulped it down, and slammed it onto the table in anger.
She crossed the living room to the balcony and looked down. Crowds of reporters, men and women alike, jammed the area below, craning their necks upward. Good thing she lived in an upscale complex with security guards stationed at every building; none of them could get up here.
A smirk tugged at Qianli Xun's lips. "Didn't realize I was this famous—look at all those reporters. Well, makes sense. You've got to be somebody to get canceled online."
Leaving the balcony, she went back to the bedroom, grabbed her phone off the bed, sat on the edge, and took a deep breath. "Time to kick back and see just how famous you really are."
She opened a short-video app. The very first clip roasting her featured a plain-looking female streamer speaking in an unhurried drawl:
"Hey, chat! The name Qianli Xun rings a bell, right? Yep! She's that big-name web novelist who wrote a bunch of hit stories—bet most of you have read them. Truth be told, I read them too, and I used to be a fan. Notice I said used to be. Why not anymore? Because I grew up as a reader, while she's still stuck in place as a writer! She keeps churning out stories aimed at eighteen- and nineteen-year-old girls. How could fans not roast her? Looking back at the novels I once loved, all I can tell my younger self is: you were so naive, gullible, and immature—how else could you have liked stuff like that?..."
Qianli Xun marveled at her own thick skin for listening that long, yet she could not help snapping back at the screen: "What do you know? That age group is my target audience! If you loved my books as a teenager, doesn't that prove I did my job right? Blame yourself for getting old!"
Mid-rant, a notification popped up. She minimized the video and tapped it—more than half the members of her fan group chat had quit.
"Big deal! Leave if you want! I'm a writer, not some pop idol. I hated setting up a fan group when the platform asked me to anyway. Good riddance—go ahead and clear out the whole chat!"
No sooner had she spoken than another alert flashed: her fan group was down to single digits. Too annoyed to care, she went back to the video app and swiped past the streamer. A bold headline caught her eye: "Literature Is Dead." Intrigued, she thought, This one definitely has nothing to do with me. Might as well listen.
An elderly man appeared on screen, looking every inch the scholar, his voice booming and fiery:
"Literature is dead! How tragic! How lamentable! Nobody reads the works of traditional authors anymore! Young people would rather scroll through mindless web fiction on their phones! Literature must serve reality—expose it, critique it, and convey the Way! Driven by profit, web novels pander to the market and amuse readers to death! Power fantasies and brainless reads flood every corner, and not a single one meets the standard of real literature! Take Qianli Xun's novels, for instance..."
All that buildup just to tear into her. She really had become notorious overnight. Fine—she would hear what this firebrand old man had to say.
"With the whole internet criticizing her, I went and flipped through Qianli Xun's novels. After one passage, I couldn't stomach another word—pure drivel with zero substance! Traditional literature has depth of language, richness of content, and layers worth savoring. Web fiction cannot hold a candle to it! I call on everyone: seize this wave of outrage against Qianli Xun's work, launch a campaign against web fiction, and revive traditional literature!..."
Qianli Xun tapped the screen, cutting the old man off mid-sentence, and pointed straight at her phone.
"Such a fiery speech—if you're so capable, try writing a web novel yourself! You wouldn't even know where to start! Let me tell you something: literature isn't dead! In the digital age, it just put on a new face to meet its readers. Do you even know literary history? Go read up on it if you don't! Every era has its own unique form of literature. Traditional writing doesn't fit this age, so it's bound to fade—reviving it won't work! Who knows, a few hundred years from now, my books might be classics too! And let me ask you this: what's wrong with my stories making readers happy? Even Cao Xueqin said Dream of the Red Chamber was just playful ink meant to lift the spirits. If the greatest of the Four Great Classical Novels existed to entertain people, web fiction is just carrying on literature's finest tradition!..."
Before she could finish venting, "Editor Youyou" flashed across her screen—a call from her platform editor.
Qianli Xun tapped the green call button. A gentle female voice came through. "Qianli Xun, upper management has made a decision. Given the massive backlash against you online, to protect the site's image, all of your works are being taken down, including your ongoing serialization. The platform will offer fair compensation for your losses."
"How can you do that? Besides, getting roasted just proves I have clout—that's all the more reason not to pull my books..."
The editor had no patience to listen and hung up at once. Helpless and fuming, Qianli Xun wanted to hurl the phone to the floor to blow off steam, yet she held back. She needed to think about her next move. Was her creative career really over just like that? Writing was the thing she loved most in the world.
#CH 3
#TITLE: Chapter 3: Dazed Transmigration, Domestic Violence
Dragged along by a mysterious force, Qianli Xun dared not open her eyes. The sheer speed and terror left her no room to process a thing; her mind went blank until the force flung her out with brutal strength...
A voice reached her ears: "Miss, wake up, please wake up! Sob... Son-in-law, did you beat my Miss to death? Sob..."
Qianli Xun wondered in silence, Miss? Son-in-law? Am I dreaming? No—I, Qianli Xun, died in that earthquake. Did I end up in another world? The afterlife? Is it going to be terrifying?
Her eyes stayed shut, yet through her eyelids she sensed bright light around her. Did the afterlife have daylight too? She might as well take a look.
Straining to lift her eyelids, Qianli Xun saw a maid in period clothing kneeling beside her in tears.
The moment the maid saw her open her eyes, grief flipped to joy. "Thank goodness! Miss, you're still alive!"
Qianli Xun asked, "Am I dead? Is this the afterlife? Who are you?"
The maid insisted, "Miss, you aren't dead, you're alive! I'm your maid, Kuqiao!"
"My maid? Kuqiao?" Qianli Xun had written several transmigration web novels, and this setup felt straight out of her own chapters. "Did I transmigrate? I didn't really die?"
Her spirits lifted at the thought, and only then did she notice she was sprawled on the floor. Kuqiao helped her up. A sharp throb shot through her forehead; she touched it and found blood on her fingertips. Since childhood, nobody had ever laid a violent hand on Qianli Xun. Anger flared—until she remembered her body lying in a pool of blood during the earthquake. Coming back to life was already a gift. Most of her fury drained away.
Steady on her feet, she saw a man and a woman standing before her. The man looked to be in his twenties—average features, broad shoulders, clearly a martial artist—with irritation written all over his stare. Beside him stood a flashy woman with slicked hair and a coy posture, sizing Qianli Xun up with open disdain.
Both wore period clothes whose exact names Qianli Xun had no idea about, coming as she did from another era. She glanced around. Far removed from modern decor, sure, yet the furnishings spoke of a well-off household.
She looked down at herself—period clothes too—and felt her head: coiled hair, gold hairpins. She really had transmigrated.
Qianli Xun pulled Kuqiao aside. "You just said you're my maid, and your name is Kuqiao?"
Eyes wide, Kuqiao nodded. "Miss, did Son-in-law hit you so hard you lost your wits? Why are you asking me that?"
"Kuqiao, tell me—who am I?"
"Miss, he really did beat the sense out of you. You don't even know who you are!"
Instead of answering right away, Kuqiao marched up to the man. "Son-in-law, Master Zhu is the Prefect of Lin'an—his rank is far above yours! How dare you lay a hand on my Miss! Just wait till I go back to Zhu Manor and tell Master to punish you!"
Unfazed by the threat, the man sneered, "Once she married into the Lin family, she became a Lin. I'm disciplining my wife. A married daughter is like water poured out—even the Prefect of Lin'an has no right to meddle."
Qianli Xun grew more bewildered and uneasy by the second. Married into the Lin family? Was the body she had landed in married to that guy? Setting everything else aside, she tugged Kuqiao back. "Hurry up and tell me who I am!"
Looking at her poor mistress, Kuqiao answered, "Your name is Zhu Shuzhen, daughter of Zhu Manor."
"Zhu Shuzhen? The Song-dynasty female ci poet Zhu Shuzhen?" Qianli Xun had read plenty of Zhu Shuzhen's poems, loved them, and knew most by heart. "What year is it?"
The woman beside the man piped up in a sugary voice, "Training Officer, looks like you really knocked her silly. She doesn't know who she is or what era she's in. Let me tell her."
She sashayed over to Qianli Xun. "This is the fourth year of the Xuanhe reign of the Great Song."
Qianli Xun thought to herself, The fourth year of Xuanhe of the Great Song—it really is the Song dynasty, and I really transmigrated into the poet Zhu Shuzhen. Back in my era, almost no records survived about her—unknown birth and death dates, people couldn't even agree whether she lived in the Northern or Southern Song. Since it's the fourth year of Xuanhe, Northern Song it is...
The woman drawled on, "Miss Zhu, since you married the Training Officer, you ought to do your wifely duty. Not to lecture you, mind you, but he's a red-blooded man. Keeping a beauty like you at home where he can look but not touch—of course he loses his temper and beats you!"
"What? Beats me?"
Qianli Xun stared at the man. Kuqiao called him Son-in-law, and this woman said Miss Zhu had married the Training Officer—so he was Zhu Shuzhen's husband. From what Qianli Xun remembered reading, Zhu Shuzhen had married at twenty. Had transmigration just shaved almost ten years off her age?
A spark of delight flashed, then soured. Qianli Xun had stayed single right up to thirty, only to land in a twenty-year-old married body—her hard-kept chastity gone down the drain, and stuck with a husband who beat her to boot!
She marched straight up to the man. "You're Zhu Shuzhen's husband? What's your name? What do you do?"
The man stared back. After he had punched, kicked, and shoved his wife into a pillar until she blacked out, she had woken up looking odd and speaking with far more backbone than before. Suspicion gnawed at him, yet he had watched the whole thing happen with his own eyes—nothing strange had occurred, and her face was unmistakably Zhu Shuzhen's. Keeping up his macho front, he ignored her and turned his head away.
The woman stepped in to explain. "He's your husband, Lin Jue, the famous Training Officer in charge of drilling every soldier in Lin'an. You should count yourself lucky you married Officer Lin!"
Training Officer? Qianli Xun mulled it over. The historical notes she knew said Zhu Shuzhen had married a minor clerk, hated the match, and pined away in misery. Looking at this cocky Training Officer who treated his wife like dirt, even Qianli Xun despised him—let alone Zhu Shuzhen, one of the Twin Jewels of Ci Poetry.
Qianli Xun decided to teach Lin Jue a lesson on Zhu Shuzhen's behalf, starting with the woman beside him. "Since I married him, I'm his primary wife. Where did you crawl out from? Are you a concubine he took?"
Lin Jue and the woman gaped. Fragile, quiet Zhu Shuzhen—since when did she talk so tough and blunt?
Kuqiao rushed to explain. "Miss, she's a commercial courtesan Son-in-law brings home from Chunxiang Brothel—Chun Jinqiao. He drags her here all the time just to spite you. Today he brought her back again, and when you still ignored him, he started hitting and kicking you."
Fury surged through Qianli Xun. She loathed men who hit women, and this bastard had the nerve to haul a courtesan right into the house.
"Lin Jue, call yourself a man? Beating your wife over a brothel girl? If you've got the guts, step into the courtyard and fight me!"
Doubt churned in Lin Jue's gut. How is she nothing like the old Zhu Shuzhen?
Even if her personality had flipped after fainting, he figured she could not have picked up new skills out of thin air. She must have lost her memory and forgotten how strong he was. Better give her another beating to jog her memory and remind her of a wife's duties.
"Fine, then. Your husband will spar with you. This time I won't knock you cold—just teach you a little lesson."
They moved out to the courtyard. Servants of Lin Manor soon gathered around, curious. Usually their master shut the doors before beating his wife; why had the fight spilled outside?
Lin Jue scoffed, "Zhu Shuzhen, I'll go easy on you. You strike first."
Qianli Xun clenched her fists. "You offered Zhu Shuzhen the first move—don't blame me for not holding back!" With that, she threw a punch.
Lin Jue brushed it off. Delicate as Zhu Shuzhen was, what force could her punch hold? One light shove would drop her.
He raised a palm to brush her fist aside. The instant skin met skin, crushing force slammed into his hand, jolting his heart. How did she get this strong out of nowhere?
Before he could recover, Qianli Xun's other fist smashed into his eye, blooming into an instant bruise. A swift kick swept him off his feet. She pinned his torso under one knee and locked both his arms behind him. "With skills this pathetic, you dare call yourself a Training Officer? Kuqiao!"
Startled yet thrilled to see her mistress turn fierce—no more getting bullied!—Kuqiao snapped back crisp and clear, "Here!"
"Bring me a rope!"
Kuqiao dashed off and returned moments later with thick hemp rope.
Qianli Xun took it, trussed Lin Jue up in seconds, and pointed at two male servants standing nearby. "You two, get over here. Haul him into a room and lock him up. Give him food and water, nothing else—nobody lets him out!"
Obediently, the two servants hauled Lin Jue to his feet.
Tied up tight, Lin Jue still ran his mouth. "Watch out, or I'll report you to the local yamen for assaulting your husband!"
"Go ahead—I'll report you for failing your duty as a husband!"
"You aren't Zhu Shuzhen!" Lin Jue burst out with the doubt eating at him.
Qianli Xun stepped close and murmured in his ear, "You'd better believe I'm Zhu Shuzhen. Otherwise that means you beat her to death. That's a human life—take it to the yamen and see who lands in a cell."
Lin Jue clamped his mouth shut as the servants dragged him off and locked him inside a room.
Qianli Xun turned her gaze on Chun Jinqiao, strode over, and cracked a heavy slap across her face. "That one's for Zhu Shuzhen. Now get out of here and crawl back to your brothel!"
Chun Jinqiao swung back to return the slap, only for Qianli Xun to block her arm cold. Overmatched by sheer strength, she knew better than to stay; clutching her stinging cheek, she swayed out of Lin Manor in tears.
#CH 4
#TITLE: Chapter 4: Playing Zhu Shuzhen for Real
With the mess settled, a sharp sting shot across Qianli Xun's forehead. "Kuqiao, which room is your Miss's? Take me there right now—I need proper rest."
Kuqiao stepped forward to steady her. "Come with me, Miss. Your quarters are right in the back."
Qianli Xun did not refuse the support; exhaustion weighed her down. The horrors of the earthquake still burned behind her eyes, dread from that uncanny force lingered, and waking up as Zhu Shuzhen took energy to process. Once those two nuisances were dealt with and her tension dropped, fatigue hit like a wave.
Kuqiao guided her around to the rear. Qianli Xun looked up at a garden gate inscribed "Zhulan Residence." Inside the courtyard, bamboo and orchids filled every corner, living up to the refined name.
Stepping inside the rooms brought an understated, classic grace. Every piece of furniture was carved from rosewood; a delicate vase and incense burner sat on the table, lending a fresh charm. Sheer woven curtains let light breathe through the room, their bird-and-flower patterns adding life. Calligraphy scrolls hung along the walls, and volumes stacked on the desk filled the air with a scholar's quiet fragrance.
"Zhu Shuzhen has quite a nice place!" Qianli Xun said, sinking onto the bed.
"What are you saying, Miss, talking as if you aren't yourself?" Doubt lingered in Kuqiao's mind, yet she refused to accept it. She fetched a small wooden box, opened it, dabbed ointment onto a clean cloth, and applied it to her mistress's forehead. "This salve works wonders. In two days your cut will heal."
Once done, Kuqiao put the box away and stood obediently to the side.
Qianli Xun eyed her. "Come closer. I have something to tell you."
Kuqiao stepped in with caution.
Lowering her voice, Qianli Xun said, "Truth is, I'm not your Miss. My real name is Qianli Xun. In my era, a massive earthquake struck—at least magnitude 7.5. I died in it, a terrifying force swept my soul away, and when I woke up, I was inside Zhu Shuzhen's body. I still can't believe it's real. My guess is Lin Jue beat your real Miss to death."
Refusing to accept it, Kuqiao dropped to her knees. "You are my Miss! Son-in-law just knocked you cold and made you lose your memory!"
"You saw me fight. Did your Miss know martial arts?"
"My Miss only knew poetry and lyrics—not a scrap of martial arts. Still, that face and figure are hers. Everyone will take you for the daughter of Zhu Manor."
Kuqiao had a point. Qianli Xun had died in the quake, and Heaven had handed her a second life as Zhu Shuzhen. Nobody in this era would ever see her as anyone else.
"Get up—why are you kneeling? Fine. Since I've stepped into her shoes, I'll live as Zhu Shuzhen. Problem is, I don't have a shred of her memories. Plus I know martial arts, and my way of talking is nothing like hers. People will spot it right away—didn't you hear Lin Jue already saying I'm not Zhu Shuzhen?"
Kuqiao thought for a moment. "Don't worry, Miss. Back in our village, strange things like this happen sometimes—someone falls ill, recovers, and acts like a whole new person. Country folk call it 'rebirth.' If anyone asks, we'll say Son-in-law knocked you unconscious, and when you woke up you were reborn—of course you're different now!"
"Done! We'll go with that—it's pretty much the truth anyway. By the way, why did Lin Jue beat your Miss in the first place?"
"There you go saying 'your Miss' again! You're my Miss!"
"Right, right—me. I'm Zhu Shuzhen." The words still felt awkward on her tongue, yet having chosen to live as Zhu Shuzhen, she resolved to tuck the name Qianli Xun deep inside her heart.
Kuqiao explained, "When Master married you off to Son-in-law, you didn't want it one bit. He's nothing like the man you dreamed of—all he knows is drilling troops, he's rough, and he comes home drunk all the time. You wanted someone to recite poetry with beneath flowers and moonlight. So after the wedding, you never consummated the marriage..."
Hearing that, Qianli Xun brightened at once. "Yes! My virtue is still intact!"
"Because you refused to consummate the marriage and even threatened to kill yourself if he came near, he started dragging courtesans home and doing shameless things right in front of you. When you didn't even blink, he got madder and started hitting you."
"So that's why. Then why did Master Zhu—no, my father—marry me to someone I didn't love?" Qianli Xun began accepting her role as Zhu Shuzhen bit by bit.
Pleased by the shift, Kuqiao went on, "You and Young Master Xu were innocently devoted childhood sweethearts. Sadly, the Xu family fell on hard times and moved away, nobody knows where. Before leaving, Young Master Xu promised he would come marry you once he passed the civil exams. You waited five whole years without a word from him. By then you were twenty—already an old maid—and Master and Madam feared nobody would want you if you waited longer. Right then Son-in-law came to propose. Master and Madam thought the Lin family was a decent match, so they agreed."
Qianli Xun sighed inwardly—an old maid in any era! Out loud, she refused to admit it. "How is twenty an old maid? Where I come from, I was thirty, still single, and still had plenty of guys chasing me!"
At that, Shen Jianchen flashed across her mind. That mysterious force had swept him away too. Had he landed in this era as well? She hoped he was safe.
Kuqiao's eyes popped wide. "What? Still unmarried at thirty? In this day and age, women are grandmothers by thirty!"
Qianli Xun laughed. "Grandmothers at thirty? You're pulling my leg!"
"Why would I lie to you, Miss? Besides, I like you better now."
"Why?"
"The old Miss was kind, sure, yet she kept quiet, spent all day writing poems I couldn't understand, and let Son-in-law push her around because she was frail. You're different—you chat with me, and best of all, you know martial arts! Sticking with you, nobody's going to bully us ever again!"
Qianli Xun chuckled. "Your Miss is starving and parched. Go grab me some food and drink so I can fill up and get a good sleep."
"Right away! I'll go fetch it for you."
After Kuqiao left, Qianli Xun walked over to the bronze mirror. The hazy surface couldn't compare to modern glass, yet it still showed clearly that the face staring back wasn't Qianli Xun—it belonged to Zhu Shuzhen.
Sitting down, she admired the refined, classic features. "Turns out Zhu Shuzhen's a real beauty."
She spoke to her reflection. "Listen up, Zhu Shuzhen. From now on, you're me and I'm you. I'll borrow your name and your life to live on in your era." With that, she removed her hairpins one by one.
Zhu Shuzhen's hair was thick, long, and black as ink—good enough to land a shampoo commercial back in Qianli Xun's time.
Just then, Kuqiao returned carrying a tray. "Miss, the kitchen happened to make your favorites—two-color lotus seed chicken soup and wild vegetable steamed buns. I brought some to tide you over."
Unable to wait, Qianli Xun hurried to the table. "Song food looks great! Only two dishes, yet so delicate—especially this chicken..."
"Two-color lotus seed chicken soup," Kuqiao prompted when her mistress stumbled over the name.
"Right, two-color lotus seed chicken soup. You can tell just from the name it takes a ton of steps and hours of simmering."
"You're right, Miss, it's real work. Go ahead and eat."
Qianli Xun dug in with big bites. Delicious.
Watching her eat with far more gusto than her former mistress ever had, Kuqiao remembered the prisoner and frowned. "Miss, Son-in-law is hollering his head off in that room, saying you assaulted your husband and threatening to haul you before the yamen. Maybe we should let him out before he raises the roof?"
Qianli Xun took a sip of soup and polished off a bun in three bites. "No problem. Once I finish eating, I'll gag him so nobody hears his nonsense."
"He's still the Training Officer, though! Locking up Lin'an's Training Officer—won't the local yamen charge you?"
"Charge me with what? It's a domestic matter—none of their business! And if the yamen wants to poke its nose in, they can start by asking why he beats his wife!"
Having cleaned the bowl and plate, Qianli Xun stood up. "Great soup, didn't quench my thirst though. Make me some tea—I'll go check on Lin Jue and drink it when I get back."
"Yes, Miss." Watching her step out, Kuqiao walked to the tea table stocked with utensils and set to work.
Outside the room holding Lin Jue, a servant emerged with a tray and an empty bowl—he had just fed the master. Seeing Qianli Xun, he bowed in haste. "Madam."
She hated the title, yet as Zhu Shuzhen she had to swallow it. "Is he behaving in there?" she asked in a flat voice.
Hardly daring to judge his master's behavior, the servant hedged, "Master is uncomfortable tied up like that, Madam."
"Leave us."
Once the servant left, Qianli Xun pushed the door open, stepped past the folding wooden screen, and spotted Lin Jue trussed up tight on the bed—quiet now, no longer yelling.
At her footsteps, he sat straight up—proof of real martial training, rising without even using his hands.
Watching Zhu Shuzhen walk toward him, hair bare of ornaments yet still striking, Lin Jue felt an eerie mix of familiar and strange. Before, this woman only scribbled poems he could not fathom, wore a constant frown, acted aloof and priggish, and looked down her nose at a military man like him. How had one beating knocked her out and turned her into someone else—brow relaxed, voice loud, and packing punches like iron?
Lin Jue chose to soften first. "Wife, we're still husband and wife. Word gets out you tied up your husband, people will laugh at us. Untie me."
Qianli Xun planted her hands on her hips. "Who's your wife? Who's married to you? Let me make this clear: the old Zhu Shuzhen died under your fists! I'm the reborn Zhu Shuzhen—not only am I done being your punching bag, you're going to sit right there and write me a bill of divorce!"
#CH 5
#TITLE: Chapter 5: Love at First Sight, No Easy Divorce
Lin Jue had gone to Zhu Manor to propose for one simple reason: at a Flower-Viewing and Fishing Banquet hosted by Prefect of Lin'an Zhu Xiyan—now his father-in-law—he had caught a single glimpse of Zhu Shuzhen. From that day on, her beauty etched itself into his heart and refused to fade.
That spring, every flower in the city had burst into bloom. Zhu Xiyan sent an invitation to his clan uncle Lin Zixiao for the Flower-Viewing and Fishing Banquet. Though Lin Zixiao's poetry and painting did not shake all of Lin'an, he held a respected place among the city's literati. And Prefect Zhu himself loved verse—how else could he have raised his daughter into a renowned female ci poet?
Lin Jue happened to be beside his clan uncle when the invitation arrived and begged to tag along for a taste of scholarly elegance. Lin Zixiao told him lesser-known scholars could not even get through the gate, let alone a military man. Still, Lin Zixiao had enough face with the Prefect to bring one guest along.
So Lin Jue attended the banquet. Aside from viewing blossoms, fishing, sipping tea, listening to music, and feasting, the main event was composing poetry and ci to showcase literary brilliance.
After amusing himself for a while, Lin Jue lost all interest once the scholars began writing poems on "spring"—until Zhu Shuzhen stepped forward. His eyes lit up, love struck on the spot, and this man who had zero head for affecting literary airs somehow memorized the entire ci she composed that day. Even now he could recite every word:
Slow spring daylight toys with gentle grace, along the flower path a secret fragrance drifts. Qingming has passed; I cannot bear to look back, where clouds lock in the crimson tower.
Waking by the noon window to clever oriole songs, where do they summon this spring sorrow? Amid green willow shadows, beside the crabapple pavilion, atop the red apricot boughs. ("Yan'er Mei: Slow Spring Daylight Toys with Gentle Grace") [TN: Translator's rendering]
It would likely remain the only ci he ever memorized in his life—such was the power of love.
What a pity that once he married her, every sweet daydream shattered against reality. On their wedding night, she refused to consummate the marriage and even held scissors to her own throat. Out of love, Lin Jue backed off, telling himself she only needed time to adjust—they were man and wife now.
Instead, his patience changed nothing. She refused to eat at the same table, wouldn't speak a word to him, and spent every day either shut inside writing verse or going on outings with friends—male scholars included.
At last Lin Jue snapped. One day he stormed into Zhulan Residence and tried to force himself on her. Frail as she was, she knew where to strike—one kick to his groin doubled him over in agony, and fury drove him to hit her.
Seeing her battered and helpless gave Lin Jue a twisted rush of relief and restored his wounded pride as a husband. Beating Zhu Shuzhen became a regular habit, especially whenever she refused his bed. Later he went further, hauling brothel courtesans home to fondle right before her eyes—yet her icy indifference only stoked his rage higher.
Today had started the exact same way, only he had used too much strength and shoved her into a pillar until she collapsed. Panic had gripped him for a long stretch—killing someone meant paying with his own life. Thank heaven she had woken up, though she seemed a whole new person: same face, same figure, everything else turned upside down.
Once before, after a beating, Zhu Shuzhen had mentioned heli—a divorce by mutual consent—only to get slapped down so hard she never breathed the word again. Now this woman demanded he unilaterally repudiate her? Shock and stubbornness knotted in Lin Jue's chest.
He repeated the same line: "You aren't Zhu Shuzhen."
Qianli Xun rolled her eyes. "Why do you keep harping on that? Want everyone to know you beat Zhu Shuzhen to death so the yamen locks you up?"
"The yamen won't arrest me. Everyone can swear you look identical to Zhu Shuzhen."
"Then that settles it! I am Zhu Shuzhen. What's left to doubt?"
"She would never ask me to write a bill of divorce."
Qianli Xun pulled up a chair and sat. "That was then. She changed her mind."
"Don't you know what happens to a woman cast out by her husband?"
"Whatever happens beats spending another day with you."
"Either way, I refuse!"
His stubbornness didn't faze Qianli Xun one bit. "Refuse all you want. I'll just lock you in here day after day—no food, no water, no latrine, no chamber pot. Ten days and nights of that, let's see if you still refuse!"
"Keep dreaming! I'm the Training Officer—I drill Lin'an's troops every day. Miss one shift and people come looking!"
"Easy fix. I'll send a servant down to your workplace..."
"Workplace?"
Qianli Xun corrected herself fast. "To your drill ground to call in sick for you—say you caught something nasty and need ten or fifteen days in bed. Your superior won't say no."
"Vicious woman! Even so, the servants of Lin Manor won't take orders from you!"
"Servants are easy—money talks. Even ghosts turn the millstone for silver. Besides, those two guys just now didn't even need a bribe to haul you in here and lock the door!"
Lin Jue seethed. "Those two fools! I'll throw them both out sooner or later!"
Seeing she was dead set on leaving him and knowing he couldn't beat her in a fight, he gave ground. "We've been man and wife—that counts as fate. Let's part on clean terms. Besides a bill of divorce, there's a second way: mutual divorce. Leave that way, and your reputation won't suffer as much."
She barely knew Lin Jue, yet any man who hit women was rotten to the core; his offer warranted suspicion. "Since when are you this nice?"
"You wake up from a faint turned into a she-demon—keeping you under my roof will cost me my sleep and my appetite. Send you packing sooner, and I can marry a new wife and live in peace. Still, mutual divorce isn't up to just the two of us. We have to summon your parents and respected elders from both sides, plus the matchmaker. Everybody has to agree in person, divide the property, sign and affix our thumbprints to the divorce papers, and have the matchmaker file them at the yamen. Only then is our marriage over."
Qianli Xun thought, Getting divorced in ancient times is such a hassle—even the elders have to sign off! Still, when in Rome...
"Deal. Mutual divorce it is."
"Seeing as I gave you such a good idea, how about untying me?"
Qianli Xun stood, grabbed a cloth nearby, stepped to the bed, and shoved it straight into Lin Jue's mouth.
No matter how he thrashed, he couldn't stop her.
She pointed a finger at him. "I was going to lock you up for days, so consider this your reward for the bright idea: just half a day and one night. I'll let you out tomorrow morning. Call it payback for beating Zhu Shuzhen."
With that, she turned and walked out.
Gagged and unable to curse her aloud, Lin Jue raged in his head: Just you wait! Think mutual divorce is that easy? The elders will drown you in lectures—and your parents won't necessarily agree either! Hmph! You'll soon learn walking out of the Lin family isn't that simple!
Back at Zhulan Residence, Kuqiao had just finished making tea, still steaming hot, and presented the tea bowl. "Tea, Miss."
Qianli Xun took it and blinked at the bright white tea liquor topped with fine foam—nothing like any tea she had ever drunk. "This is tea?"
"Whisked tea I just prepared for you, Miss."
"Whisked tea?" She had heard of Song whisked tea, never seen it, and knew it took skill. Lifting the bowl, she drained it in one go—creamy, smooth, sweet, and silky. "Not bad! Didn't know you had this skill."
Kuqiao took the empty bowl. "You taught me yourself, Miss! You love whisked tea, didn't feel like whisking it every time, so you trained me. Otherwise a servant like me would never get the chance to learn."
"I taught you? Guess we'll have to flip roles and let you teach me." Looking around for a bed, Qianli Xun felt exhaustion drag at her limbs. "Where's the bed? I need to lie down."
Kuqiao set the bowl aside. "Right this way, Miss."
Following Kuqiao around an inner partition, Qianli Xun spotted a curtained bed and flopped straight onto it without a word.
Kuqiao hurried over. "Miss, take off your outer robes before you sleep."
Qianli Xun sat up to undress herself—new to this era, she wasn't used to being waited on.
"Stand beside the bed, Miss, I'll help you."
Qianli Xun complied. As Kuqiao untied her robes, she asked, "Why wouldn't servants get a chance to learn whisked tea?"
"Whisking tea is a refined art expected of noble ladies like you and high-ranking families," Kuqiao explained as she folded the garments. "Only people of your standing have the leisure to fuss over a single bowl of tea. Common street folk and servants like us have neither the leisure nor the chance. I only learned because of your favor."
Down to her inner tunic, Qianli Xun slipped under the quilt with a sigh of relief. "I'm worn out. Going to bed before sundown—don't wake me after dark. I'm sleeping straight through till morning."
Moments later, she was fast asleep.
Kuqiao let down the bed curtains, stepped out on light feet, and pulled the door shut behind her.
#CH 6
#TITLE: Chapter 6: Reborn as Prince Yi, Zhao Pu
In the outskirts of Bianliang, the Eastern Capital, stood Prince Yi's Manor—home to Zhao Pu, thirteenth son of the reigning sovereign Zhao Ji. Zhao Pu's mother, Consort Qiao, had died right after giving birth to him. Raised by a wet nurse, sickly since infancy and unfit for state duties, he was the least regarded of all the imperial princes.
On this day, Zhao Pu's strength gave out at last. He closed his eyes and drew his final breath.
Qiao Mengrong, wet nurse Matron Zou, and Zhao Pu's personal maids wept in bitter grief.
The moment Shen Jianchen caught faint echoes of weeping, the Divine Willow's force vanished. Floating in midair, still shaken, he stared down at the people in period dress. I'm dead—why am I on a film set? Where's Qianli Xun?
A strange pull tugged at him from the body lying on the bed, weak enough that he held himself back from drifting into it.
Then a glowing golden shade peeled free from the corpse and floated up to him. "You share a bond with my flesh. I am leaving—you may dwell in it. Remember: love Mengrong well for me."
Infuriated at being saddled with a stranger's dying wish, Shen Jianchen snapped, "Who's Mengrong? Why should I love her for you? I already love someone—Qianli Xun!"
The shade couldn't hear a word he said.
A beam of golden light broke open higher overhead, accompanied by ethereal celestial music that filled the air with bliss. The shade drifted up toward the radiance.
Drawn by the breathtaking glow and music, Shen Jianchen tried to float upward too—only for a shaft of golden light to strike him down, dropping him dead-center into the corpse on the bed, plunging him as if down a bottomless abyss.
When the falling stopped, the weeping returned—no longer distant and hazy as it had been in midair, but crisp and loud right beside his ears, grating on his nerves. He shouted, "Stop crying!"
Silence fell at once. The weightless float was gone, replaced by a solid, grounded heaviness throughout his limbs. Did I really drop into that shade's body? Better open my eyes and see.
He forced his eyes open to a bright room—and three female heads hovering right over him. One wore a maid's garb; another, a woman around forty, had a kindly face; the third was pretty, poised, and refined, every inch a sheltered young lady.
Seeing Prince Yi wake up by some miracle, Qiao Mengrong threw herself onto him in joy. "Brother! I thought I'd never see you again! You came back to life! Sob...!"
Matron Zou cried in haste, "Fetch His Highness's medicinal pills right now! Give him one before anything else goes wrong!"
A maid dashed to the side table, grabbed a brocade box, popped it open, pinched a pill with a silk handkerchief, and handed it to Qiao Mengrong.
Drying her tears, Qiao Mengrong let go of Prince Yi, held the pill between slender fingers, and brought it to his lips. "Brother, swallow this pill—it's good for your health."
Before he had even figured out what was going on, someone was shoving medicine down his throat! Worse, he hated bitter pills—just smelling them turned his stomach, let alone swallowing one.
The foul medicinal reek hit his nose so hard he slapped the pill away and sat straight up. "I'm not sick! Why would I eat that junk?"
Rubbing the stinging back of her hand, Qiao Mengrong stared in shock—her brother had never once been rough with her. His voice rang like a bronze bell, and he sat upright without a trace of his old frailty. Forgetting her sting and surprise, she gasped, "Matron Zou! Did Brother walk to the Gates of Hell and come back cured of every illness?"
Knowing a bit of medicine, Matron Zou seized Prince Yi's wrist, checked his pulse, and burst out in excitement, "His Highness is cured! Cured! Really cured!"
The maids standing around cheered in delight.
Overjoyed, Qiao Mengrong threw her arms around him again. "Brother, Heaven must have been moved by my prayers—not only did you come back from the dead, your whole body is healthy!"
Held tight by a beauty, Shen Jianchen felt not a flicker of joy. He knew he had landed inside a stranger's body—this wasn't a movie set, it was another era. Right now, he needed answers.
He eased Qiao Mengrong off him and asked, "Who are you? Who am I? Where is this?"
Everyone froze at his questions—especially Qiao Mengrong, whose face fell at the realization he didn't know her.
Seasoned Matron Zou stepped in at once. "Don't worry, Princess. Coming back from the Gates of Hell, His Highness must have drunk Mengpo's Soup before being sent back. The downside is forgetting one's past life; the upside is that it cures all illness!"
Qiao Mengrong exhaled in relief. "Hearing you say that puts my heart at ease, Matron Zou. Brother's health matters most. As for his memories, there's no rush—we can help him remember bit by bit, and if not, his memories can start fresh from today..."
Tired of their chatter without answering him, Shen Jianchen cut in, "You still haven't answered my questions."
"Don't fret, Brother, I'll tell you everything," Qiao Mengrong said. "You are Prince Yi, Zhao Pu, and this is Prince Yi's Manor..."
Before she could finish, Shen Jianchen blurted out, "I'm a prince? The emperor's son? What's my father's name?"
"Yes, Brother, you are His Majesty's thirteenth son. People of our station dare not speak His Majesty's personal name aloud, though I can tell you it is the fourth year of the Xuanhe reign of the Great Song." Watching his expression as she spoke, she saw only blank bewilderment.
Dying and waking up as an imperial prince wasn't a bad deal at all. "You call me Brother—are you a princess?"
Matron Zou explained, "Your Highness, she is Princess Jiahe—a sister you found abandoned by the roadside when you were eight. Later His Majesty granted her the title Princess Jiahe. You two grew up together as childhood sweethearts, and His Majesty even issued an imperial betrothal..."
"What? An imperial betrothal?" Shen Jianchen took a closer look at this adopted sister—softer and gentler than Qianli Xun. Remembering the shade's parting plea, he asked, "Your name is Mengrong?"
Qiao Mengrong lit up. "Brother remembers my name!"
Her excitement did nothing for him. Pointing at Matron Zou, he asked, "And who is she?"
"That's Matron Zou, the wet nurse who raised you," Qiao Mengrong replied. "She and I are your closest family in this world."
"Wet nurse? What about my birth mother?" Surely a birth mother came first.
Matron Zou let out a sigh. "Ah, I served Consort Qiao as a maid in her maiden home and married after she entered the palace. Who knew she would pass away in her twenties right after giving birth to you? I had borne a child two months earlier—he only nursed from me for two months before a grave illness took him, and his father followed him out of grief. The Qiao family sent me into the palace to care for you in Consort Qiao's stead. Raising you helped heal the pain of losing my own son."
Moved by the sad tale, Shen Jianchen held back further questions lest he dig up more grief. He had the gist of who these people were; the rest could wait. Since he had borrowed the original owner's body to live again, he needed to figure out how to live this new life.
Done sitting in bed, he threw off the brocade quilt and swung his feet to the floor. Two maids rushed forward at once, kneeling to put on his shoes, while Mengrong and Matron Zou stepped aside in quiet deference.
Shen Jianchen relished the feeling. Even as a big-company CEO, he had never enjoyed royal treatment like this. Being a Song prince had its perks.
Mengrong had called it the fourth year of Xuanhe. Never big on history, Shen Jianchen hadn't a clue what Xuanhe meant.
Thoughts of Qianli Xun surged back. If she were here, she'd unpack "the fourth year of Xuanhe" in a heartbeat. They had died together in the quake and been swept off together by the Divine Willow. He had landed in the Song dynasty—had she landed here too?
Once his shoes were on, Shen Jianchen stepped outside, gazed up at the sky above Prince Yi's Manor, and asked in silence: Qianli Xun, where are you?
A servant hurried in from the front gate, spotted the prince standing right there, and went weak in the knees. "A ghost...!"
A maid snapped, "What ghost? That's Prince Yi!"
The servant dropped to his knees. "I just went to the palace to report His Highness's death! Now I walk back through the gate and His Highness is alive again—haven't I committed the Crime of Deceiving the Sovereign?"
Qiao Mengrong and Matron Zou stepped out behind him.
Qiao Mengrong instructed, "Go back to the palace and explain that His Highness walked to the Gates of Hell and returned—cured of every illness."
The servant kowtowed. "Congratulations on your new lease on life, Your Highness! I'll run back at once!" Jumping up, he bolted out the gate.
Qiao Mengrong stepped beside Prince Yi. "Brother, are you hungry? I'll tell the kitchen to prepare food."
Checking in with his new body, Shen Jianchen felt no hunger. "Not yet. I want to walk around the manor. I woke up remembering nothing, and the place feels foreign. Since I'll be living here, I ought to learn my way around."
"I'll walk with you, Brother."
Shen Jianchen didn't refuse, nor did he wait for her; he set off along the stone paths alone, taking in everything around him with curiosity.
Qiao Mengrong trailed behind with her maids, watching his back with an eager yet unfamiliar gaze.
Matron Zou turned to a maid beside her. "Tell the kitchen to get His Highness's meal ready so it's on the table once he's tired of walking. For so long His Highness stayed bedridden without ever setting foot in the grounds. What a blessing—Princess Jiahe has finally made it through the dark days."
#CH 7
#TITLE: Chapter 7: An Unfavored Prince
Prince Yi's Manor was huge—about the size of a city park Shen Jianchen used to stroll through.
Buildings took up only a small part; most of the grounds showcased pleasing landscapes, while half the land remained untouched, overrun with wildflowers, tall grass, and wild trees. Reaching this edge, Shen Jianchen stopped.
Trailing behind him, Qiao Mengrong felt the chill in her reborn brother's manner. Before, he would hold her hand, fuss over her every step, and gaze at her with deep affection. Even frail and rarely able to walk the grounds, those rare moments had wrapped her in warmth.
Now he remembered nothing—not even their love. Resentment toward Mengpo's Soup flickered in her heart, until another thought checked it: without that soup, how could he be healthy? Intense love meant little if his frail body left her a young widow.
She told herself not to ask for too much. Lean as his frame still was, he radiated vigor and masculine strength; even his back looked sturdy, just like the brother she had seen countless times in her dreams.
Drawn forward, she took two steps closer—only for him to turn around. Qiao Mengrong froze in place, cheeks flushing shy.
"Princess Jiahe, why is half the manor left wild?"
"You felt half was plenty to live in, Brother, so you left the rest untouched. Above all, you love natural scenery."
Then she ventured a plea. "You never called me Princess Jiahe before—only Mengrong. Could you still call me that?"
Faced with her shy look, Shen Jianchen hated to hurt her; she and Zhao Pu had loved each other and shared a betrothal. "Alright, Mengrong it is. Still, I don't remember much, so about that betrothal..."
Delighted to hear her name on his lips again, Qiao Mengrong beamed. "No rush on the betrothal, Brother. His Majesty's marriage decree sets the wedding for next year's Mid-Autumn Festival."
Studying the gentle, blushing girl—surely the type most men fell for—he asked, "If I hadn't woken up, wouldn't the betrothal be void?"
"How could an imperial decree spoken by His Majesty himself be void? Even if you hadn't woken up, I had made up my mind to stay and guard Prince Yi's Manor for you all my life."
Moved on Zhao Pu's behalf, Shen Jianchen still asked, "You look barely fifteen or sixteen—right at the age to enjoy life. Why marry so young?"
"I'm already sixteen—right at marrying age. Wait any longer and no one will want me."
Remembering people wed young in this era, Shen Jianchen grew curious about Zhao Pu's age. "Mengrong, tell me—how old am I?"
"You just turned eighteen, Brother."
Twelve years younger in one leap! Shen Jianchen couldn't help cheering, "Yes!"
Catching the puzzled stares of the princess and her maids, he covered fast. "Just thrilled to be this young—feels like getting my wish to start life over!"
Baffled, Qiao Mengrong opened her mouth to ask what he meant by starting life over, when a servant hurried up to report: "Your Highness, Princess, an Imperial Attendant from the palace has come to call on His Highness."
"Brother, an Imperial Attendant is here. We have to go greet him," Qiao Mengrong said.
"Imperial Attendant? What's that?" Shen Jianchen wasn't up on court titles.
"Someone who serves His Majesty inside the palace. His Majesty must have sent him—we have to receive him."
Catching the rank involved, Shen Jianchen hurried back with Qiao Mengrong.
Three men waited in the main hall: an older man in the center flanked by two young attendants in matching robes. Their outfits brought TV eunuchs straight to mind. So Imperial Attendants are eunuchs, Shen Jianchen realized.
The senior eunuch stepped forward. "Truly a marvel! Your Highness looks nothing like before. His Majesty was grieving upon hearing you had passed away, only for glad news to follow that you had revived with every ailment gone, so he sent me to see for myself. Just by your stride, Your Highness has cast off illness for good!"
Unfamiliar with Zhao Pu's past yet well versed in handling people, Shen Jianchen responded with ease. "My thanks for Imperial Father's concern, and thank you for making the trip in person. Though fortunate enough to revive and shed my illness, I have lost all memory of the past. If not for the Princess and Matron Zou, I wouldn't even know who I am."
"Such a thing happened?" Intrigued, the eunuch glanced over at Princess Jiahe.
Qiao Mengrong spoke up at once. "Brother speaks the truth—when he opened his eyes, he didn't even recognize me. Please report this to His Majesty, and ask him to be magnanimous and forgive Brother if he makes any slip in the future."
The eunuch chuckled. "Lost memory or not, what matters is that he lives and his ailments are gone—a joyous blessing indeed! Princess Jiahe has great fortune ahead of her."
Blushing, Qiao Mengrong took a money pouch from a maid and stepped forward. "Thank you for taking the trouble, Attendant. A small token of appreciation—nothing worthy of your rank."
The eunuch pocketed it with practiced ease. "Princess Jiahe knows proper etiquette as always. I will report Prince Yi's recovery to His Majesty in full. Farewell."
After bowing, he led his attendants out.
Watching them leave, Shen Jianchen asked, "Why did you give him money?"
Qiao Mengrong sighed. "Imperial Attendants stay at His Majesty's side, so their words carry weight. Slipping him some silver ensures he puts in a good word for you—or at least doesn't speak ill of you."
Shen Jianchen frowned. "Aren't I the emperor's son? Why do I need a eunuch to put in a good word for me?"
"You really don't remember a thing, Brother," Qiao Mengrong explained. "Though you are His Majesty's son, he has never favored you. Through all your years of illness, he never visited and never summoned you, only sending attendants to pass messages. Everything His Majesty hears about you comes through their mouths. A bit of silver at least buys peace."
So Zhao Pu was an unfavored prince. Fine by Shen Jianchen—less attention meant fewer headaches and more freedom.
"Next time an attendant comes, don't give him a single coin. Let him tell His Majesty whatever he pleases."
"Absolutely not!" Qiao Mengrong shook her head at once. "We have to give silver. I don't ask for His Majesty's favor—only for your safety, Brother."
Touched by the devotion in her eyes, Shen Jianchen relented. "Do as you like. I'm hungry—is there anything to eat?"
Matron Zou walked into the hall right then and caught his words. "The meal is already set for you, Your Highness—just waiting for you to sit down."
In the dining hall, Shen Jianchen eyed the spread—some dishes familiar, others total mysteries—amazed that even an unfavored prince ate this well.
Matron Zou and Qiao Mengrong sat down to dine with him, happier than they had been in years.
In the past, Prince Yi ate only in bed, his meals cooked separate and stewed soft to mush so they wouldn't upset his weak frame.
Now he sat right across from them, full of life, enjoying every bite like any healthy man.
"Eat up if you like it, Your Highness," Matron Zou said, placing food in his bowl. "You've been sick so long you're skin and bones—putting on some weight will do you good."
Qiao Mengrong just watched him eat as if seeing him for the first time—and in truth, she was.
When she was six, her birth parents had abandoned her by the roadside. Prince Yi's carriage happened past; hearing her sobs, he halted, pitied her, and brought her back to Prince Yi's Manor to raise as his own sister.
From the first day she met him, he had been ill and never ate at the same table—not even after their feelings grew into love. She had dreamed of this scene countless times, never expecting to live it today.
Uncomfortable under two pairs of staring eyes, Shen Jianchen asked, "Aren't you two eating?" He picked up food with his chopsticks and served each of them.
Matron Zou beamed. "Seeing Your Highness healthy in my lifetime, I could die this very instant and go happy."
Qiao Mengrong chided her with a smile. "Don't talk such bad luck, Matron Zou! You'll live to a hundred and enjoy Brother's good fortune for years to come."
With that, both women picked up their chopsticks and ate in high spirits.
Seeing how wholehearted their devotion to Zhao Pu was, Shen Jianchen felt he owed them the truth. He waved to the maids standing by. "All of you step outside. No need to wait on us."
The maids filed out one by one.
Puzzled, Qiao Mengrong asked, "Why send the maids away, Brother? They're here to serve you."
"I have something to tell you two, and I don't want outsiders listening."
Watching the last maid pull the door shut, Matron Zou said, "Everyone's gone, Your Highness. Speak freely."
Shen Jianchen set his chopsticks down with grave focus. "Truth is, I'm not Zhao Pu. My name is Shen Jianchen, and I come from another era. I died in an earthquake and ended up here. I watched a beam of golden light and celestial music carry Zhao Pu away, and then I fell into his body..."
Qiao Mengrong laughed. "Brother, I never knew you could spin such tall tales!"
"I'm not making it up. Every word is true."
Sensing from his steady gaze that he might actually mean it, Qiao Mengrong glanced at Matron Zou.
Matron Zou stayed calm as stone. "Whoever you were, you woke up in Prince Yi's Manor inside Prince Yi's body. You can only be Prince Yi—no one else. Please never speak of this to another soul, Your Highness."
Qiao Mengrong refused to accept he was anyone else—if he weren't, where would all her love go? Had her brother never woken up, so be it. Yet he had awakened, transformed into the healthy man of her dreams—how could she let one sentence, "I'm not Zhao Pu," shatter everything?
"No, you are my brother! You just lost your memory and can't recall the past."
Helpless, Shen Jianchen had to admit the truth of it: Shen Jianchen didn't exist in this world. He was Zhao Pu now, and nobody would ever see him otherwise.
"Forget it—no use talking when nobody believes me. Fine, I'm Zhao Pu, and from now on I'll be a proper unfavored Prince Yi."
Matron Zou and Qiao Mengrong exchanged a smile, picked up their chopsticks, and went back to eating in high spirits.
#CH 8
#TITLE: Chapter 8: Three Rules to Free Her Husband
Day broke, and morning air drifted through the window—crisp and sweet, untainted by concrete dust or car exhaust. Kicking off her quilt, Qianli Xun breathed it in through her sleep and murmured, "Shen Jianchen! Shen Jianchen!..."
Hearing her, Kuqiao parted the bed curtains and asked in a hushed voice, "Miss, what did you say?"
"Shen Jianchen!" Eyes still closed, Qianli Xun slept on.
Kuqiao muttered under her breath, "Who's Shen Jianchen? Never heard Miss mention that name before."
She tucked the quilt back over her mistress, dropped the curtains, and sat by the bed working on embroidery while waiting for her to wake.
Kuqiao had just finished stitching a single leaf when Qianli Xun opened her eyes, sat up, and stared at the bed frame and curtains in dazed disorientation. Right—I transmigrated. For a second I thought I was still Qianli Xun.
Hearing movement, Kuqiao put down her needlework, stowed everything in a woven basket, set it aside, and pulled back the curtains. "Awake, Miss?" She hooked the drapes up neatly.
Seeing Kuqiao drove home the fact that she was Zhu Shuzhen now. "What time is it?"
Kuqiao paused to think. "Are you asking what hour it is, Miss?"
Qianli Xun gave a wry smile—different eras, different terms, at least they still understood each other. "Yes."
"Not yet the Hour of the Snake," Kuqiao replied, fetching her clothes.
Having researched ancient timekeeping for her web novels, Qianli Xun knew that meant not yet nine in the morning. She climbed out of bed and let Kuqiao dress her.
"Miss, Son-in-law stayed real quiet last night—didn't hear a peep out of him."
"I stuffed a gag in his mouth. Of course he stayed quiet."
"Ah! Miss, how could you gag Son-in-law? Aren't you afraid he'll cast you out?"
"Hmph! Afraid of what? Yesterday I told him to his face to write me a bill of divorce! He wouldn't do it—and gave me a better idea instead," Qianli Xun bragged.
Finished dressing her, Kuqiao pressed her palms together at once. "Amitabha! Amitabha! Thank goodness Son-in-law refused, or you'd be in terrible trouble, Miss!"
Amused by Kuqiao's panicked face, Qianli Xun laughed. "Look how scared you are. What's the big deal about being cast out?"
Kuqiao sighed in worry. "Hearing that, you really aren't the old Miss. She would never ask Son-in-law for that."
"I told you I'm not her."
"Still, you're the only Miss I have. Consider this Kuqiao begging you—never ask Son-in-law to cast you out!"
Seeing her genuine distress, Qianli Xun asked, "Tell me then—what's so terrible about a woman getting repudiated? Why are you this worried?"
Kuqiao rattled off at once, "Everyone assumes a cast-out wife committed some disgraceful misconduct. Remarrying becomes next to impossible, and it shames her maiden family! If she has unmarried sisters at home, their reputations get ruined too and nobody dares propose. To protect the other daughters' honor, maiden families won't even let a cast-out daughter back through the door!"
Pretty harsh indeed. Qianli Xun asked, "Do I have any sisters?"
"No, you're the only daughter of Zhu Manor. You have an older brother."
"Then I'm in the clear—even if Lin Jue casts me out, I won't drag anyone else down."
"No, Miss! If Son-in-law casts you out, you probably won't even be allowed inside Zhu Manor! Master values family honor above everything—he'd never tolerate his children bringing disgrace to the household!"
Kuqiao guided her to sit before the bronze mirror and began fixing her hair.
Watching Kuqiao coil her bun in the mirror, Qianli Xun said, "Relax. Lin Jue isn't casting me out—he suggested mutual divorce instead."
"Then Son-in-law isn't half bad! Mutual divorce is the best path. Back in our village, Aunt Yao got a mutual divorce from her first husband. Her maiden family welcomed her right back, she remarried later, and folks say her whole family lives happy as can be!"
"Still, I doubt Lin Jue is acting out of kindness. He's probably cooking up some dirty trick."
Pinning ornaments into her hair, Kuqiao sighed. "Son-in-law truly treated you poorly before. You and he belong on two different roads—forcing you onto the same path makes you miserable and hurts to watch."
"Which is why I have to leave him. Better for him, better for me."
Qianli Xun's mind was made up: she was divorcing Lin Jue.
From Qianli Xun's side, she had never even had a proper boyfriend—transmigrating into the Song dynasty only to wake up married to a wife-beating scumbag who hauled courtesans into the house? Not divorcing him would defy Heaven itself!
From Zhu Shuzhen's side, she and Lin Jue shared zero ideals or tastes—like a fresh flower stuck in cow dung, mismatched to the bone, bound to wither from the stench. Since Qianli Xun had inherited her life, she owed it to Zhu Shuzhen to break free—and helping her meant helping herself.
Mid-thought, Kuqiao spoke up. "Miss, you called out a name in your sleep—sounded like Shen Jianchen. Who is he?"
Seeing no reason to hide it, Qianli Xun said, "A friend who died in the earthquake with me. I must have dreamed of the quake and called his name."
"Was he someone you loved back in your era?"
"Ha! Me love him? Only when pigs climb trees! Every time that guy saw me, he couldn't rest until he got a few digs in, and he had the nerve to call that pursuing me—hilarious!"
A sudden ache tightened her chest as she added, "Now I don't even have the chance to hear him tease me to my face. I ended up as Zhu Shuzhen—no telling what happened to him. I might never see him again."
While they talked, Kuqiao finished her hair. "Look, Miss—how is it? Do you like it?"
Qianli Xun glanced in the mirror. "Clever hands—you know just how to make your Miss shine. Very nice, I love it."
After washing up and eating breakfast, Qianli Xun went to check on Lin Jue. He must have had a rough night; time to turn him loose.
She pushed open the door of his room and stepped inside.
At the sound, Lin Jue sat straight up. Gagged and unable to speak, his glare practically ordered her to untie him on the spot.
"Stop glaring—I came to let you go." Stepping up, Qianli Xun yanked the rag from his mouth.
"You wicked woman!" Lin Jue burst out.
Untying the knots, Qianli Xun shot back, "If I were wicked, you wouldn't have lived to see sunrise. Smarten up. You can't beat me in a fight, so behave yourself, go through with a clean mutual divorce, and after that we stay out of each other's hair. You live your life, I live mine, and if we bump into each other later we can even say hello—isn't that nice? My father's a man of standing in Lin'an and outranks you. Treat me right and he won't shortchange you."
Just as her lecture wrapped up, the last knot came free. Half expecting Lin Jue to swing at her the second he got loose, she braced herself—only to find him staring at her with a far softer look.
"What are you staring at?"
"Since the day you married me, every word you spoke to me combined doesn't match what you've said since yesterday. If you'd talked to me like this before, I never would've hit or cursed you."
A pang of pity flickered in Qianli Xun—stuck with a wife who gave him nothing but cold violence. "All the more reason we're wrong for each other. Staying together makes both of us miserable."
Lin Jue climbed off the bed and stretched his stiff limbs. "I'll give you three days. Bring your parents and the Zhu elders here in three days, and I'll let you leave the Lin family."
Qianli Xun beamed. "It's a deal! Tell me one thing, though—how many elders are you bringing? My side can't be outnumbered. We need an even match so neither family bullies the other at the table. Only fair."
"Don't worry—however many you bring, I'll match. And if you can't bring a single one, forget about mutual divorce."
Suspicion flared. "You planning some scheme?"
Lin Jue gazed at her. Different soul or not, her face hadn't changed—one look and he couldn't bear handing her over to another man. "Mutual divorce was my idea. What scheme could I have?"
"Fair point!" Remembering she still had to stay under this roof for a few days, she needed ground rules. Calling after his retreating back, she yelled, "Hey!"
Lin Jue paused and turned. "Something else?"
"While I'm still staying in Lin Manor, I have three rules."
"I'm all ears."
Strange as it was, Lin Jue found himself enjoying the sound of her voice—even idle chatter—after starving for a single word from her for so long.
"First, stay out of Zhulan Residence. You've already tasted my fists."
Lin Jue held no fond memories of Zhulan Residence—only cold shoulders and humiliation; aside from hitting her, he couldn't recall why he ever went in. Fine by him. He nodded.
"Second, no bringing courtesans into Lin Manor. Want fun, go to a brothel."
"Agreed."
"Third, until the divorce is final, you give me silver to spend."
"You're still mistress of the house—spending silver is your right."
"Look at you, agreeing so fast. My opinion of you just went up a notch."
Once Lin Jue left, Qianli Xun set her mind to the mutual divorce. She needed to visit Zhu Shuzhen's maiden home—a place she knew nothing about. First, get the rundown from Kuqiao, then plan her move.
Three days was tight, yet for the sake of a fresh start in this unfamiliar era, she would give it everything she had.
#CH 9
#TITLE: Chapter 9: Whisked Tea, Talk, and the Road to Zhu Manor
When Qianli Xun returned to Zhulan Residence, Kuqiao sat at the tea table making tea. Intrigued, Qianli Xun pulled up a seat to watch, putting serious business on hold for a moment.
"Making tea again?"
Whisking away inside the tea bowl with her bamboo whisk, Kuqiao replied, "You drink a bowl after breakfast every morning, Miss."
"What's that gadget in your hand called?"
"A tea whisk."
Kuqiao lifted the purple clay teapot beside her, poured a splash of hot water along the rim of the bowl, and whisked again until fine foam began to rise.
Qianli Xun marveled, "That's magic!"
Kuqiao added hot water and whisked again—seven infusions in all—until thick, creamy foam surged to the very brim of the bowl. Setting the whisk down, she lifted the bowl and offered it. "Ready to taste, Miss."
This time Qianli Xun didn't gulp it down. She took a small sip, savored the lingering aroma, then took another. Refinement, she realized, had nothing to do with modern technology—back in her high-tech era, she had never felt half this refined.
Tea done, Kuqiao moved to the writing desk to tidy up, laid out paper and brushes, and began grinding ink.
Finishing her last sip, Qianli Xun set the bowl down. "What are you doing now, Kuqiao?"
"Every morning after tea, you sit here to read or write poetry and ci, Miss. I'm getting everything ready." Kuqiao kept grinding ink.
These were the daily rituals Kuqiao repeated without fail; she refused to drop them or ask Qianli Xun's preference, unable in her heart to admit her former mistress was truly gone.
Qianli Xun took a seat at the desk. She had never owned a real study desk before.
During school years, desks belonged to her parents or classrooms, and she had never cared for them.
Later, when she bought her own apartment, the designer asked if she wanted a study desk. She said no—a computer desk would do. Even that sat idle most days; she wrote her web novels propped up in bed against cushions, hugging a giant Hello Kitty plush while tapping away on her phone.
Only after transmigrating into Zhu Shuzhen did she both own and need a proper desk.
She eyed the Four Treasures of the Study laid out before her. Her muscles remembered keyboards and phone screens, whereas Zhu Shuzhen's hands knew only the brush.
Good thing Qianli Xun had studied brush calligraphy from middle school through college—seven or eight solid years of practice.
Out of the corner of her eye, she spotted a square wooden tub on the floor stuffed with rolled sheets of calligraphy. Pulling one out and unrolling it, she saw graceful brushstrokes spelling out a ci:
Spring night, my jade body is soft as a gold hairpin as, back to the lamp, I unfasten my silk skirt. But the quilt and pillow are cold. The night's fragrance is gone.
Spring is a deep courtyard of many locked doors. Petals falling in falling rain make the night seem forever. Regret comes to me in dream. There is no escape. ("Huanxisha: Spring Night") [TN: Translated by Tony Barnstone and Chou Ping]
Qianli Xun had read this lyric before. Opening it now in Zhu Shuzhen's own shoes, she felt far deeper the ache, frustration, and loneliness of a loveless marriage—and the poet's yearning for true love.
Picking up the brush, she dipped the tip full of ink and copied the lyric line for line, matching the original hand.
Once she set the brush down, Kuqiao leaned over to admire the page. "Your writing is already seven or eight parts like before, Miss! A bit more practice and nobody could tell them apart."
Remembering the mutual divorce, Qianli Xun said, "Kuqiao, I need to make a trip back to Zhu Manor. Tell me about the family so I know what I'm walking into."
Kuqiao scratched her head. "Zhu Manor? Asking all at once, I don't know where to begin. Could you ask something specific, Miss?"
Thirst crept back. "I'm thirsty—make me another bowl of tea, and we'll talk while you work."
"Right away!" Returning to the tea table, Kuqiao opened the small porcelain jar for tea powder. "Out of powder—I'll grind some fresh. Ask anything you want, Miss, I'll tell you all I know."
Watching Kuqiao put dried tea leaves into a stone mortar and pound them with a stone pestle, Qianli Xun asked, "First, who lives in Zhu Manor?"
"Master, Madam, Eldest Young Master, and Young Madam—your sister-in-law," Kuqiao said as she pounded. "When you married into the Lin house, Young Madam was eight months pregnant, so the baby must be born by now."
"I've been married over half a year and never went back once?"
Kuqiao transferred the crushed leaves into a tea roller and ground them back and forth. "Master agreed to the match without asking you. You hated the idea, yet the betrothal was sealed, so you had no choice but to go through with the wedding. On the third day, when it was time for the return visit to your maiden home, you were too furious to go."
"Did I leave things real tense with my parents?" Qianli Xun worried a bitter rift would complicate the divorce.
"Tense at the time, sure," Kuqiao said, keeping the roller moving. "Still, what parent holds an overnight grudge against their own child? Besides, Madam bumped into me on the street several times and asked after you every time."
"True enough—parents don't stay mad at their kids forever..."
Thoughts of her own parents surfaced. Growing up as wild as any boy, she had gotten scolded nonstop, once even running away from home—only to hug her mother in tears the moment they found her, every grievance melting away on the spot.
Later, when she was twelve, a car crash took her mother. Her father taught her every move of Qianli Fist, charged her with keeping the style alive, then shaved his head to become a wandering monk right around her college graduation—leaving her to run Qianli Fist Manor on her own.
Had Qianli Fist Manor even survived the earthquake?
Grief tightened Qianli Xun's chest, and a sigh slipped out.
Having ground the leaves into fine dust and brushed them into the tea sieve, Kuqiao heard the sigh and tried to cheer her up. "If you miss Master and Madam, I'll go back with you to visit them, Miss. They'll be thrilled to see you!"
Qianli Xun wondered aloud, "What I don't get is, if a daughter skips her return visit, her father holds high office—couldn't he check in with the son-in-law he handpicked? Don't tell me Lin Jue has the guts to snub the Prefect of Lin'an."
Sifting the powder, Kuqiao answered, "I'm only a maid, so I don't know what goes on between Master and Son-in-law. All I know is Master cares deeply about family honor."
A father obsessed with reputation—would he step forward to back a mutual divorce?
No point pumping Kuqiao for more guesses; better to meet Zhu Shuzhen's parents face to face and size them up herself.
"Kuqiao, finish that bowl of tea, then come with me to Zhu Manor."
"Yes, Miss."
Done sifting, Kuqiao spooned green powder into the tea bowl, brushed the rest into the porcelain jar for later, added hot water, and whisked through seven infusions before bringing the finished bowl over.
"Please drink, Miss."
Taking the bowl, Qianli Xun marveled, "Watching you pour so much care into every single step makes me almost hate to drink it."
"Only if you drink it is my work worthwhile, Miss."
"Good with tea and smooth with words too. Just looking at you tells me what your old mistress was like—meticulous about every detail, not sloppy like me..."
Kuqiao protested at once, "What talk is that, Miss? How many times do I have to say it—you are my one and only mistress!"
Ignoring the lecture, eager to reach Zhu Manor and cut loose from a marriage she had zero use for, Qianli Xun downed the tea in one gulp, set the bowl down, and stood up. "Kuqiao, let's go to Zhu Manor!"
They walked out of Lin Manor, had the Lin coachman hitch a carriage, and climbed aboard one after the other.
Her first ride in a horse-drawn carriage thrilled Qianli Xun—even more than her first ride in Shen Jianchen's Rolls-Royce. To her, that Rolls-Royce had just been a prop for showing off wealth, and she despised men who flaunted cash in front of girls like clueless fools.
Yet the moment the carriage lurched forward over bumpy stone streets, she had to admit the Rolls-Royce beat this for comfort, bracing herself to stay steady.
Soon lively street noise swelled around them. Lifting the curtain to peek out, she saw Lin'an's avenues packed with pedestrians and lined shoulder-to-shoulder with shops. Voices haggled and chatted as the carriage threaded through the crowd; teahouses and taverns overflowed with patrons, snatches of storytelling drifting from inside.
Letting the curtain drop, Qianli Xun remarked, "Lin'an's streets are livelier than I expected."
"This is nothing—Bianliang, the Eastern Capital, is way livelier!"
"You've been there?"
"No, you told me before, Miss. A Lady Wei from Bianliang sent a letter inviting you up there to make friends through poetry, and you promised to take me sightseeing in the capital someday."
"Lady Wei? Is her name Wei Wan?"
"No idea—you never told me her given name."
Must be Wei Wan, Qianli Xun thought. Later historical records noted Zhu Shuzhen corresponded with Wei Wan, backed up by surviving poems. Right now she had no head for tracing verse—winning over Zhu Shuzhen's parents came first.
Lost in thought, she felt the clamor fade as the carriage rolled to a stop.
"We're here, Miss," Kuqiao said.
Nerves fluttered in Qianli Xun's stomach. "Here already? I haven't even figured out what to say or what face to put on when I meet my parents!"
"Don't be nervous, Miss—you grew up here."
"Does it show that much? Even you noticed."
Kuqiao smiled without answering, hopped down first, and stood ready to assist her.
After a brief hesitation inside, Qianli Xun braced her nerve and stepped down, taking Kuqiao's steadying hand.
Looking up, she saw massive vermilion gates shut tight beneath a plaque reading "Zhu Manor," flanked by imposing stone lions.
"Kuqiao, go knock."
"We don't go in through the main gate, Miss."
"Not the main gate? Then the back gate?"
"The side gate. Follow me, Miss."
"Weird—why build such a grand gate if nobody walks through it? What a waste!"
"You wouldn't know, Miss—the main gate stays shut unless honored guests visit. Family members use the side gate. Even Master uses the side gate every day."
Following Kuqiao around the left wall, Qianli Xun soon came to a far humbler side gate framed by slender bamboo groves, quiet and tasteful.
#CH 10
#TITLE: Chapter 10: Home to Zhu Manor, Meeting Her Mother
Kuqiao knocked for a moment. Soon the gatekeeper on duty pulled the door open, recognized Kuqiao, realized the young mistress was home, and swung the gate wide. "Miss is back! Madam has been talking about you nonstop!"
Kuqiao asked, "Where are Master and Madam? Miss is here to pay her respects."
"Master's still at the prefecture office. Madam is feeding the fish in the garden," the servant answered, stepping aside to clear the way.
Qianli Xun stepped over the threshold, Kuqiao right behind her.
Once they were inside, the servant said, "Wait inside the house, Miss—I'll run report to Madam." He jogged off toward the garden.
"Where do we go now?" Qianli Xun asked.
"Madam's quarters. Just follow me, Miss."
Kuqiao led the way while Qianli Xun trailed behind. Zhu Shuzhen's childhood home felt like a labyrinth—though an awfully scenic one, pleasant at every turn.
With her hopeless sense of direction, Qianli Xun knew she'd get lost in minutes without a guide.
"Kuqiao, stick right beside me in Zhu Manor. This estate is huge and twists every which way—I'll lose my bearings for sure."
"Don't worry, Miss, I won't leave your side for a second."
After countless turns, a courtyard appeared ahead.
"Madam lives here, Miss. Come inside."
Stepping into the yard, they met a maid walking straight toward them.
"Miss is home! Quick, come sit inside—I'll send someone for Madam right away!"
"No need, Sister—the gatekeeper already went," Kuqiao replied, turning to introduce her. "Miss, this is Yinxing, a maid from Madam's rooms."
Before Qianli Xun could speak, Yinxing scolded, "Since when does Miss need you to tell her who I am?" Taking Qianli Xun by the arm, she led her indoors.
As they walked, she chattered on, "Miss, you haven't set foot home since marrying into the Lin house. If Master hadn't held her back, Madam would've marched over there herself!"
Rarely this quiet, Qianli Xun held her tongue lest one wrong word blow her cover. Inside, she picked a chair and sat while Yinxing ordered other maids to serve tea.
Once tea was set out, Yinxing pulled Kuqiao aside and whispered, "What's gotten into Miss? Her expression looks odd—nothing like before."
"Quit asking silly questions, Sister. I'm waiting on her here, go tend to your chores," Kuqiao brushed her off and resumed her post behind her mistress.
Yinxing turned back. "Sit tight, Miss—I'll step out to the doorway and watch for Madam."
Qianli Xun nodded. "Go ahead, no need to hover."
Relief washed over her once Yinxing left. About to meet Zhu Shuzhen's mother, she wondered if she ought to squeeze out a few tears to look sincere—then thought better of it. Faking the real Zhu Shuzhen would fall flat anyway. Better to act natural and play it by ear.
Just as she decided, a woman in her forties hurried in with reddened eyes, surrounded by four or five maids.
Guessing this was Zhu Shuzhen's mother, Qianli Xun rose to her feet.
Madam Xiang had been feeding the garden fish when a page boy ran up with word of her daughter's return; dropping everything, she had rushed straight back with her maids.
Seeing the daughter she had missed for over half a year, Madam Xiang felt a sting in her nose and tears well up. Seeing Shuzhen stand, she rushed over and seized her hands. "Shuzhen, Mother thought you'd never come back to see me..."
