Qianli Xun spoke straight. "You're Zhu Shuzhen's birth mother? When I woke up, Kuqiao told me I'm Zhu Shuzhen, daughter of Zhu Manor, so I had her bring me home today to meet my family."
That one speech stunned Madam Xiang and every maid in the room.
Blinking through eyes brimming with tears, Madam Xiang looked her daughter up and down. "Shuzhen, what on earth are you saying?"
Thump! Kuqiao dropped to her knees and burst into tears before uttering a word.
Qianli Xun applauded inside. What a teammate this girl is!
Letting go of her daughter and stepping back, Madam Xiang sensed trouble. Dabbing her eyes with a silk handkerchief, she ordered the other maids, "All of you out—and close the door."
Every maid except Kuqiao filed out. Once the door clicked shut, Madam Xiang took a seat. "What kind of scene are you two putting on?"
Knowing Kuqiao would lay it all out, Qianli Xun stayed seated and watched how Madam Xiang would react.
Sobbing, Kuqiao poured it out: "Madam, Miss hasn't had a single good day since marrying Son-in-law! He beats her over nothing and drags brothel courtesans right into the house! Just yesterday he brought one home again and beat Miss—shoved her straight into a pillar! She fainted dead away, and when she woke up, she couldn't remember a thing, like a whole different person! Sob..."
Fury climbed across Madam Xiang's face. "How dare he! Why didn't you run home and tell Master and me sooner?"
Wiping her tears, Kuqiao cried, "Miss forbade me! She said telling you wouldn't help—might as well let Son-in-law beat her to death! Sob..."
Madam Xiang stared at her daughter—sure enough, a wound marked her forehead. Overwhelmed at seeing her, she hadn't even noticed. Rising, she stepped over and touched the cut with her handkerchief. "Does it hurt?"
Starved for motherly warmth since losing her own mother in that crash, Qianli Xun felt heat bloom inside her chest. "Doesn't hurt. Are you really my mother?"
Madam Xiang nodded. "You are my daughter Shuzhen."
"Mother, I woke up remembering nothing. If I'm not like the daughter you knew, please don't disown me."
Fresh tears rolled down Madam Xiang's cheeks. "Had I known you'd suffer like this in the Lin house, I never would have agreed to marry you to Lin Jue!"
A strong opening—Qianli Xun had won Zhu Shuzhen's mother onto her side in one stroke.
"Get up and speak, Kuqiao," Madam Xiang ordered.
Drying her eyes, Kuqiao climbed to her feet.
Sitting back down, Madam Xiang asked, "Tell me, Kuqiao—how is Shuzhen different from before?"
Glancing at her mistress for permission and getting a nod, Kuqiao spoke up. "Aside from her face and figure, everything's different! She talks loud, talks lots, can't even whisk tea anymore—and best of all, she knows martial arts! Even Son-in-law couldn't beat her, and she demanded he write a bill of divorce! Madam, I think Son-in-law knocked her out and she was reborn!"
"What? Reborn?" Madam Xiang shot to her feet, then slumped back down, slapping the table as she wailed, "My poor daughter! Did Lin Jue beat you to death? Sob..."
Pained to see her sob so hard, Qianli Xun spoke up. "Mother, I'm alive, not dead—just a bit different from before."
Yes—same voice, same face, alive and breathing. Madam Xiang choked back her wails. "Yes, my Shuzhen is alive! Come here, let Mother get a good look at you."
Qianli Xun walked over, crouched down, and rested her head against her mother's knees. "Mother!"
Stroking her hair, Madam Xiang swore, "Mother almost lost you! No—I will never let you go back to that Lin house!"
Lifting her head, Qianli Xun said, "Mother, I may not remember the past, yet even after I woke up Lin Jue tried hitting me, and I saw that courtesan right there in the house. Out of anger I told him to cast me out, till I learned a cast-out wife loses her reputation. So I pushed for mutual divorce. Lin Jue agreed—told me to bring Zhu family elders in three days to settle it."
Madam Xiang helped her stand. "Rest easy—Mother won't leave you in that fiery pit! Good thing Lin Jue has the sense to agree to mutual divorce. My daughter did nothing wrong; he has zero grounds to cast you out anyway!"
"Mother, I want to stay home for a couple of days. I don't want to go back to Lin Manor."
"Silly girl, this is your home. Stay as long as you want!"
Madam Xiang called toward the door: "Yinxing!"
Yinxing pushed the door open. "Your orders, Madam?"
"Go to Lin Manor yourself and tell them Miss is staying at Zhu Manor. When she'll return, we don't know yet."
"Yes, Madam." Yinxing left on her errand.
A surge of happiness filled Qianli Xun. Having family backing her grounded her in this strange new world.
"Once your father gets home, I'll explain everything before you pay your respects. Mother believes he'll agree to the mutual divorce too."
"Is Father fierce?"
The question pierced Madam Xiang's heart—her daughter truly remembered nothing. "Not fierce, just stern—sometimes strict. Nothing to fear. Your old courtyard is still kept for you. Have Kuqiao take you there and walk you around the estate so you can reacquaint yourself; maybe your memories will come back bit by bit."
Bidding Madam Xiang farewell, Qianli Xun followed Kuqiao through winding path after winding path deeper into Zhu Manor. Impossible to tell how huge the place was—it felt endless.
Along the way, singing drifted through the air. Qianli Xun stopped and peered toward the sound: another walled courtyard rose ahead, hiding the buildings inside.
"Kuqiao, who lives in there? Why is someone singing?"
"Those are the singing girls and dancing girls Master keeps."
"What? Singing and dancing girls? Why does my father keep them?"
"Nearly every high official's house keeps entertainers to receive distinguished guests. Every year Master hosts a poetry banquet for all the noted scholars of Lin'an, and you need musicians and dancers to liven up the scene."
"Did I attend those banquets?"
"Every year since you were ten, Miss! Many of your finest lyrics were written right at those banquets and passed around by scholars—that's how you earned the title Poetess of Jiangnan."
"The Poetess of Jiangnan," Qianli Xun murmured. Novels she could write in her sleep, yet classical poetry wasn't her craft. Wearing Zhu Shuzhen's name meant upholding her literary crown—heavy pressure. Remembering she had Zhu Shuzhen's poems memorized eased her nerves. "Kuqiao, isn't there a single straight path in Zhu Manor? Everything twists and turns!"
"Not a single one! I heard Master built the estate around the poetic idea of winding paths leading to secluded spots. Isn't it poetic?"
"Poetic, sure—and it's making me dizzy! Where on earth is my courtyard?"
"Almost there, Miss—just two more turns."
Two bends later, a small lake opened before them, over half its surface blanketed with lotus leaves, dotted with standing lotus buds. A little wooden bridge spanned the water, leading at last into Zhu Shuzhen's own garden courtyard.
#CH 11
#TITLE: Chapter 11: Chrysanthemum Verse in the Quiet Courtyard
A maid stepped out of the garden and bowed the instant she saw Qianli Xun. "Miss, the sweeping maids and I have already cleaned the courtyard and rooms—all ready for you to move right in."
Kuqiao whispered in her ear, "That's Lutao."
Qianli Xun smiled. "Ah, Lutao! I just walked in the gate and you already got the place spotless—fast work!"
Sensing her mistress sounded off yet knowing her place as a servant, Lutao kept her surprise to herself. "Did you forget, Miss? When you married, you instructed me to have the courtyard and rooms cleaned every single day, whether you came back or not."
"Oh, right—my orders! Still, I'm married now, and a married daughter is like water poured out. Why did Father keep this garden for me? Did he miss me and save it on purpose?" Qianli Xun fished for clues about her unseen father's feelings toward his married daughter.
Lutao almost reached up to check her mistress's forehead for fever—why ask such a question? Spotting the wound there, she held back and answered plain, "Miss, did that cut on your forehead rattle your memory? This garden is so tucked away nobody wants it, and Master pays it no mind. Only someone who loves peace and quiet like you treats it like treasure."
Embarrassed, Qianli Xun covered fast. "Of course I know that! I just hoped Father cared about me a little, so I asked. Kuqiao, let's go in!"
Kuqiao told Lutao, "Almost noon, Sister—go see to lunch. I'm here with Miss, don't worry about us."
Lutao bowed and took her leave.
Stepping inside, Qianli Xun felt as if she had entered a rustic cottage yard, only far more refined. Three plots sat behind low fences: one brimming with lush purple perilla leaves; one holding tall sunflowers angled toward the sun; and one filled with several varieties of chrysanthemums, some already in bloom, others still waiting for their season.
Beside the chrysanthemum fence stood a fine round wooden table paired with a rattan chair.
"Back when you lived here, Miss, you loved brewing a pot of tea and sitting in that rattan chair to admire the chrysanthemums," Kuqiao said.
Picturing Zhu Shuzhen seated there with a book, sipping tea among the blossoms, Qianli Xun wanted to try it herself. She settled into the rattan chair, gazed at the open chrysanthemums, and felt a poetic spark stir—reciting a poem by Zhu Shuzhen:
"Wild blooms can show both white and red; in late-season virtue I prize such craft. Sooner grow old on the branch hugging my scent than dance with yellow leaves on the autumn wind." ("Chrysanthemum") [TN: Translator's rendering]
Kuqiao gasped in excitement. "Miss, your memory came back!"
Even after inventing the "rebirth" excuse, the girl still clung to the hope her mistress only had amnesia; hearing her recite her own verse made Kuqiao think the old Zhu Shuzhen had returned.
Instead of answering, Qianli Xun asked, "You just told Lutao to arrange lunch—am I eating here?"
Hope draining from her face—this mistress still didn't know basic house rules—Kuqiao replied in a flat tone, "Yes."
"I just got back to Zhu Manor. Why aren't I eating with Mother?"
"Zhu Manor is huge, Miss. Each courtyard dines on its own unless it's a special occasion or Master gives the word. You just arrived and Master isn't home yet, so Madam can't dine with you until Master returns and approves."
"What?" Qianli Xun jumped up. "I need my father's permission just to eat lunch with my own mother?"
"That's always been the rule in Zhu Manor."
Dread crept in. "Wait—everything Mother just said about backing my mutual divorce... does that need Father's nod too?"
Kuqiao nodded. "Master makes every decision in this house. Madam meant every word from the heart, yet none of it counts unless Master agrees."
Just when she thought half the battle was won, she hadn't even left the starting line.
"Why didn't you tell me earlier Father runs the whole show?"
"You didn't ask, Miss," Kuqiao mumbled, feeling wronged.
"Forget it, not your fault. You said Father guards family honor—long as mutual divorce doesn't stain the Zhu name, he shouldn't block it."
"Miss, mutual divorce sounds better than being cast out, sure, yet it's still no honor. I'm afraid Master won't agree."
Kuqiao's words hit like a cold slap. Steadying herself, Qianli Xun tried optimism. "Any real father puts his daughter's happiness first."
Then she asked the same question she had asked Madam Xiang: "Is Father fierce?"
"Master is a Prefect—learned and cultured. He rarely loses his temper, yet he's so stern he keeps everyone at arm's length. Servants barely dare breathe around him."
"That scary?"
"Yes! You were never close to Master growing up because he's so stiff. Aside from formal greetings, you two never once sat down for a heart-to-heart or a casual chat."
Back in her old life, Qianli Xun had been closest to her dad—he spoiled her, spun stories, and bantered about everything under the sun. She had assumed all fathers loved their daughters like that. Even after shaving his head to wander the land, he still came home every Mid-Autumn Festival to spend the holiday with her.
Treating a father-daughter bond like boss and subordinate sounded surreal.
"How does he even host poetry banquets acting like that? Doesn't he scare all the scholars off?"
"Master's only like that at home. Around scholars and colleagues, he smiles plenty."
"What a weird guy."
As they talked, Lutao arrived leading five maids and an older servant woman.
"Time for lunch, Miss. Madam ordered two extra dishes added for you."
Each maid carried a dish while the older woman carried a food carrier. They bowed, stepped inside, and laid the table.
Lutao led them in a farewell bow. "Enjoy your meal, Miss. With Kuqiao attending you, we won't disturb you."
Qianli Xun nodded—tactful girl, sensing she only wanted Kuqiao around.
Stepping inside, she found the interior matched Lin Manor's Zhulan Residence—clearly Zhu Shuzhen favored this refined, uncluttered style.
Appetite roused by the spread of delicacies, she sat down and dug right in. Two bites in, spotting Kuqiao standing by ready to serve, she ordered, "Sit down and eat with me."
"I couldn't! Servants never eat at the same table as masters—Zhu Manor has no such custom!"
"Forget custom! I can't finish all this food alone, and wasting it is a sin. Sit down, quit arguing."
Seeing her mistress wouldn't budge, Kuqiao gave in and took a seat. "Never dreamed I'd live to share a table with Miss."
"Eat up. From now on, you eat at my table every day."
Moved, Kuqiao said, "Miss, I really like you now. Even though I still miss the old Miss and sometimes wish she'd come back, deep down I like you even better."
"Old me, new me—I'm your Miss either way."
"You're right!"
With Kuqiao pitching in, the two of them polished off every plate. Afterward, Qianli Xun browsed through Zhu Shuzhen's books at the desk until drowsiness crept in, then shed her outer robes for a nap.
She woke about an hour later and had just pulled on her clothes when Yinxing arrived to call.
Kuqiao showed Yinxing in; Qianli Xun spotted a brocade box in her hands.
"Miss, Madam sent this over—she prepared it on your behalf for your nephew's full-month celebration. Since you weren't home then, now that you're back, you can deliver it yourself or send someone with it."
Kuqiao took the box and handed it to her mistress.
Opening the lid, Qianli Xun saw a solid-gold Longevity Gold Lock—worth a small fortune. What a thoughtful mother, looking out for every detail! Closing the box, she handed it back to Kuqiao. "Keep it safe. Come with me to see my little nephew in a bit."
Task done, Yinxing bowed to leave, only for Qianli Xun to stop her. "Hold on—I have a question."
Yinxing paused. "Yes, Miss?"
"Did you go to Lin Manor?"
"I did. Eldest Master Lin said you may stay at Zhu Manor as long as you wish, and he'll pass word to Son-in-law once he returns."
"Eldest Master Lin?"
Puzzled that her mistress didn't know who that was yet too polite to pry, Yinxing answered, "Son-in-law's eldest brother."
Afraid more questions would arouse suspicion, Qianli Xun swallowed the rest and said, "Good work. Go tend to your duties."
The second Yinxing stepped out, Qianli Xun pounced on Kuqiao. "I spent half a day, a night, and a morning at Lin Manor and never saw any big brother—where did an eldest brother pop out from?"
"Lin Manor isn't as vast as Zhu Manor, yet it's still big," Kuqiao explained. "Eldest Master Lin lives in the north wing, Second Master Lin in the east, and Son-in-law is the third son over in the west. They live far apart and rarely cross paths."
Hearing there was a second brother too made Qianli Xun's stomach sink. "He's got a whole crowd—two brothers plus his parents! Won't they outnumber me at the divorce meeting?"
"You don't know, Miss—Son-in-law's parents passed away years ago."
Two down already—Qianli Xun exhaled in relief. "Well, that's a break."
"Except the Lins are a huge clan," Kuqiao added. "Just the clan uncles I know of number seven—and there are more I don't know."
"What? That many?" Qianli Xun sprang up and paced the floor. "I haven't nailed down a single elder yet, and he has an army! With those odds, they'll steamroll me—I'll never get divorced! Kuqiao, any chance none of the Lin elders show up?"
"Not likely," Kuqiao said, sharing her worry.
Eyeing the brocade box in Kuqiao's hands, Qianli Xun decided, "Let's go see my brother first and get him locked in."
Mind made up, she headed straight out, Kuqiao hurrying after her. Right past the garden gate, Qianli Xun froze—she had zero clue which way to go. "Kuqiao, take the lead again."
Resuming her role as guide, Kuqiao led her toward the eastern side of Zhu Manor.
#CH 12
#TITLE: Chapter 12: Winning Over Brother and Sister-in-Law
They walked on through winding paths—only where Zhu Shuzhen's courtyard was tucked in the deepest seclusion, her brother and sister-in-law's quarters opened into wide, airy grounds. Arriving there, Qianli Xun felt her eyes brighten as the view expanded.
Zhu Shuzhen's older brother, Zhu Jingyuan, lacked his sister's brilliance yet could turn out decent essays. Being the sole son, his father Zhu Xiyan had pulled strings to land him a civil post in Lin'an and married him to Chen Wenxin, daughter of the Chen family, the wealthiest clan in Lin'an. The couple got along well. When Zhu Shuzhen married, Chen Wenxin had been eight months pregnant; their son was now over six months old.
Outside Zhu Jingyuan's courtyard gate, Qianli Xun halted. "Kuqiao, what are my brother and sister-in-law like?"
"Eldest Young Master may not match your talent, Miss, yet he's honest and treats you well," Kuqiao replied. "As for Young Madam—I've heard she has a sharp tongue, though she's decent at heart."
"Heard? You haven't met her?"
"Only a few times when following you, Miss. You barely spoke to her, so she never got the chance to say much—haven't seen her sharp tongue myself."
"Used to ignore her, huh? Well, today I'm the one asking a favor. Go knock."
Kuqiao stepped up and rapped on the garden gate.
The gate cracked open and a maid poked her head out. "Oh, Kuqiao! Heard you came back with Miss—didn't believe it till I saw you!"
"Good day, Sister," Kuqiao said. "Miss finally made it home and came to visit Young Master and Young Madam."
The maid called over her shoulder to a page boy inside, "Run tell Eldest Young Master that Miss is here!"
Swinging the gate wide, she curtsied. "Perfect timing, Miss—Eldest Young Master just got back from office duties. Come on in, I'll take you right to him."
Catching her mistress's questioning glance, Kuqiao leaned in and whispered, "Her name is Yuegui, the maid Young Madam brought from the Chen house."
Qianli Xun nodded, and they followed Yuegui inside. The yard was vast and lined with pavilions, almost a manor of its own—every courtyard in Zhu Manor had its own distinct style.
Walking along, they met a tall young gentleman approaching—clean-cut, handsome, his smile carrying half of Madam Xiang's warmth. Qianli Xun knew at once this was Zhu Shuzhen's brother.
Zhu Jingyuan beamed at the sight of his sister. "Just saw Mother when I got back and heard you were home. I was about to change clothes, play with Laibao a bit, and come visit you—never expected you to walk right over and save me the trip!"
Having grown up an only child, Qianli Xun loved gaining an older brother out of the blue. Copying the servants' bow, she said, "Been away over half a year—now that I'm home, of course I had to visit my brother, see my nephew, and chat with my sister-in-law."
Already briefed by Madam Xiang on Shuzhen's "rebirth" and amnesia, Zhu Jingyuan still gaped at seeing how drastic the change was. Before, she never bowed to him, barely uttered "Brother," and never wasted a syllable on small talk—quiet, buried in poetry, immune to chitchat. Today she walked up wanting to chat with her sister-in-law! Night and day.
Still, he reached out to steady her. "Wenxin is inside playing with Laibao. Come meet her."
Warmth swelled in Qianli Xun—maybe she had always wished for an older brother. Carried away with delight, she linked her arm right through Zhu Jingyuan's. "Having a brother feels awesome! Now if anybody bullies me outside, my brother's got my back!"
Usually Qianli Xun was the one knocking people flat, yet deep down she was still a woman who liked the idea of protection—especially from a big brother.
Thrown off by this unrecognizable sister, Zhu Jingyuan spotted the wound on her forehead—that injury must have caused the rebirth and amnesia. "Marriage sure changed you, sister. Does your forehead still hurt?"
Clinging to his arm as they walked inside, Qianli Xun said, "Kuqiao put salve on it, so the cut barely hurts—what hurts is my heart! Lin Jue beats me nonstop. I can't live with him another day—Brother, you have to stand up for me!"
"Mother told me everything, and I'm furious too! Marrying a gem like my sister, what more could he want? Raising a fist to her defies Heaven! Count on me—I'll stand up for you. Let's talk inside."
They stepped into the room together.
Little Laibao sat babbling and laughing on a small sandalwood crib while Chen Wenxin shook a pellet drum to amuse him, maids standing at attention beside her.
Seeing Shuzhen enter, Chen Wenxin handed the drum to a maid and rose to greet her. "Look what rare guest blew in—what brings you over to our side, Sister?"
Letting go of Zhu Jingyuan, Qianli Xun held out her hand; Kuqiao slipped the brocade box into it. Offering it to Chen Wenxin, Qianli Xun said, "Sister-in-law, a first-meeting gift for my nephew. I was sulking with Father during his full-month feast and missed it, so let me make it up today."
Never before had Zhu Shuzhen spoken this many words to her—let alone with a smile. Surprised, Chen Wenxin accepted the box and lifted the lid to see the gleaming gold lock. "Marriage really turned you into a new person! Lovely gift—let's put it right on your nephew."
Yuegui took the box from Chen Wenxin, lifted out the gold lock, and slipped it around Laibao's neck.
"Take a seat, Sister," Chen Wenxin said.
Then she barked at a nearby maid: "What are you standing there like a post for? We have guests—go fetch tea!"
Too scared to speak, the maid scurried off to prepare tea.
Zhu Jingyuan took Chen Wenxin's arm. "Don't snap at the servants. Come sit down—Shuzhen rarely visits, let's have a nice talk."
Taking a seat, Qianli Xun observed the couple. Her brother treated his wife with genuine affection—a solid man.
Once seated beside her husband, Chen Wenxin eyed Shuzhen. "You hardly ever look my way, Sister—dropping by out of the blue really surprises me. Aside from seeing Laibao, you must have something else on your mind?"
The maid returned, set the tea down, and stayed standing beside them with her tray.
Chen Wenxin flared up again. "Masters are talking—why are you still loitering here? Get out!"
Stung—Kuqiao, Yuegui, and the maid tending Laibao were all still in the room, so why single her out?—the maid dared not talk back and slunk out.
Qianli Xun spoke up smoothly. "Can't I just come visit my brother and sister-in-law? Shuzhen was immature before and neglected you."
To steer straight to the point, she put on an act: "Kuqiao, my cut is stinging—dab on some more ointment."
Kuqiao pulled out the wooden box, opened it, and dabbed ointment onto her mistress's forehead in plain view.
Chen Wenxin noticed at once. "Where did you bang your head, Sister? Is it bad?"
Qianli Xun kept silent, letting her brother carry the ball.
Right on cue, Zhu Jingyuan spoke up. "Haven't had a chance to tell you—Lin Jue has been beating Shuzhen ever since she married him. He gave her that cut, and the blow wiped her memory and changed her whole personality. Mother says she was reborn."
"What? Reborn?" Surprise flickered across Chen Wenxin's face before she recovered fast. "If you ask me, reborn and memory-less Shuzhen is way more down-to-earth and pleasant to be around! Reborn is reborn, amnesia is no big deal—what matters is she's alive. Still, Lin Jue laying a hand on a daughter of Zhu Manor? Outrageous! Father-in-law ought to teach that punk a real lesson!"
Delighted that both brother and sister-in-law backed her, Qianli Xun seized the moment. "Sister-in-law, woman to woman, you know how much marrying a good man matters. I didn't have your luck landing a gem like my brother—I got stuck with a wife-beating scumbag like Lin Jue. I can't bear another day with him, so I'm pushing for mutual divorce. When the day comes, please stand behind me!"
As she spoke, Chen Wenxin glanced at her husband. No towering genius, maybe, yet he cherished her, listened to her, and put her first—better than ten times the men out there. A warm glow lit her face.
"You really can't stay with a man like Lin Jue. Mutual divorce is the smart move—leave him, and you can still find a fine husband."
Qianli Xun pressed on, "So you and Brother agree to appear on my behalf at the divorce meeting?"
"Need you even ask? I agree," Chen Wenxin answered.
Zhu Jingyuan chimed right in: "If your sister-in-law agrees, I agree!"
Jumping up, Qianli Xun threw her arms around Chen Wenxin. "You're the best, Sister-in-law! I'm so lucky to have you!"
"Actually, I ought to thank Lin Jue," Chen Wenxin remarked.
Qianli Xun let go. "What do you mean?"
Chen Wenxin laughed. "Beating you is despicable, sure, yet without his beating, you never would've turned into someone I actually like! Before, you were frosty and aloof, barely spoke two words, kept everyone miles away—now you act like a real person!"
Qianli Xun pouted. "Sister-in-law, you make it sound like Lin Jue deserves credit for hitting me!"
"Alright, alright, just teasing! Lin Jue really is that word you used..."
"Scumbag."
"Right! A scumbag!"
Mid-laugh, Yinxing's voice floated from outside the door: "Young Master, Young Madam—Master is home! Hearing Miss was here, he sent me to summon her to dine with Master and Madam."
Zhu Jingyuan encouraged her, "Father's back and calling for you. Tell him everything about Lin Jue beating you—I'm sure Father will back you up."
Reassured by his words, Qianli Xun asked, "Aren't you and Sister-in-law coming to dinner too?"
Pitying his memory-less sister, Zhu Jingyuan explained, "Father only summoned you, not Wenxin and me. We can't intrude uninvited."
Qianli Xun had no choice but to take her leave.
Dusk had fallen by the time she stepped outside. One day gone, two left. Win over Zhu Shuzhen's father, and she was home free. Buoyed by hope, she followed Yinxing's dim lantern as they wound once more through the paths of Zhu Manor.
#CH 13
#TITLE: Chapter 13: Facing Her Father, Smashing the Table
By the time they reached the Main Courtyard of Zhu Manor, night had deepened.
Lanterns hung at regular intervals all around the courtyard, their warm glow cutting clean through the dark.
Yinxing raised her handheld lantern, blew out the flame, and guided Qianli Xun and Kuqiao down a long corridor into the main residence's yard. Vast and paved with dressed stone tiles, the yard stood bright in the lantern light, revealing three magnolia trees planted on each side in full bloom—white, purple, and yellow.
Drawn by the blossoms, Qianli Xun paused to take them in.
Yinxing nudged her along. "If you like them, Miss, come admire them in daylight tomorrow—they look even lovelier in the sun. Better hurry to dine with Master and Madam before Master gets displeased."
Resuming her stride, Qianli Xun weighed her strategy: act like the old Zhu Shuzhen in front of her father, or stay Qianli Xun? Better start in Zhu Shuzhen's mode, observe first, and adapt. She coached herself: bow first, then stay silent.
Soon they reached the main dining hall, where maids bustled in carrying platters.
Halting at the doorway, Yinxing announced, "Master, Madam, Miss has arrived."
A man's voice answered from within, cool and distant: "Let her in."
"Master says you may enter, Miss," Yinxing murmured.
Two steps in, Qianli Xun saw Kuqiao hanging back and glanced over her shoulder. "Why aren't you coming?"
"I'm not permitted inside, Miss. I'll wait out here."
Yinxing added, "Don't worry, Miss—I'll take Kuqiao to eat in the maids' quarters."
Qianli Xun sighed. "Fine—guess I'm walking into the lion's den solo."
Inside the main hall, a massive standing screen blocked the view beyond. She paused and reminded herself: Keep your cool, show manners, stay silent.
A maid stepped out and whispered, "This way, Miss."
Rounding the screen behind the maid, Qianli Xun took in the room at a glance.
Ahead sat a wide couch with a low square table across it. On one side sat Madam Xiang, greeting her daughter with a quiet smile and no words. Across from her sat Zhu Shuzhen's father, absorbed in a book without lifting his eyes once.
Qianli Xun studied him: forties, neatly trimmed beard, bold brows, clear eyes, fine bone structure, good skin—must have been quite a looker in his youth.
So Zhu Shuzhen got her looks from her dad, Qianli Xun thought.
Stepping forward, she bowed. "Father. Mother."
Madam Xiang nodded in silence.
Without raising his eyes from the book, Zhu Xiyan ordered, "Sit."
Qianli Xun took a seat, repeating like a mantra in her head: Stay quiet, stay quiet.
Minutes ticked by in dull silence as maids shuttled in and out setting dishes. All this food for three people? Talk about extravagant!
Madam Xiang hadn't found an opening to brief her husband—she never spoke out of turn in front of him. Watching Shuzhen enter with poised grace and sit still without a peep, she brightened with a relieved smile.
Then her daughter opened her mouth: "Father, Mother, the food's all set—let's go eat! Eating too late messes up your digestion and packs on weight..."
A single tear slid down Madam Xiang's cheek—this was no longer her old Shuzhen. She turned her head to blot it away with her handkerchief.
Startled, Zhu Xiyan raised his eyes and shot a sharp glance at his daughter.
A chill ran down Qianli Xun's spine under that severe stare. Cutting herself off, she ducked her head and clamped her mouth shut again.
A maid stepped forward and bowed. "Master, Madam, Miss—dinner is served."
Finally! Qianli Xun sprang up first—only to catch Madam Xiang shaking her head and motioning her back down. Sitting again, she sneaked a look at Zhu Xiyan as he laid down his book, rose from the couch, and walked past her.
Madam Xiang stepped down too, took her daughter's hand, trailed behind Zhu Xiyan to the dining table, and guided Shuzhen to her proper seat.
Never in her life had Qianli Xun endured such a stifling meal. Fed up with the tension, she ditched silence.
The instant Zhu Xiyan uttered "You may begin," she picked up a piece of duck and dropped it into Madam Xiang's bowl. "Mother, try this."
Madam Xiang shook her head again.
Ignoring the hint, Qianli Xun picked up another piece of duck and reached toward Zhu Xiyan's bowl—"Father..."—only to pull back at his thunderous scowl. "Don't want it? I'll eat it!" She popped it straight into her own mouth and chewed away.
A maid leaned down and whispered in her ear, "Miss—no talking at the table, and mind your posture."
Irritated, Qianli Xun grumbled inwardly: Can't even eat in peace in my own house—rules for this, rules for that! Fine, I need a favor from him. Look at him though—a grown man nibbling away without making a sound, zero manly gusto!
Glancing at Madam Xiang, she saw dainty chopsticks, tiny bites, delicate chewing. Qianli Xun tried mimicking it, then almost laughed at herself—this wasn't her at all. Raised among martial artists practicing Qianli Fist, she had always eaten hearty and fast. Refusing to torture herself, she dropped the act and dug in with big bites.
Madam Xiang froze mid-bite in shock. This wasn't her refined Shuzhen—she ate like a country bumpkin! Tears welled again as she dabbed her eyes, while the serving maids traded startled smirks.
Smack! Zhu Xiyan slammed his chopsticks down on the table, making everyone jump.
Mid-reach for a drumstick, Qianli Xun jerked her hand back, slapped her forehead, and put on a show. "Ow! My forehead cut hurts so bad!"
Alarmed, Madam Xiang cried, "Shuzhen, are you alright? Somebody bring wound salve right now!"
A maid rushed over with ointment and applied a fresh coat to her forehead.
"Does it still hurt?" Madam Xiang asked in anxiety.
"Much better," Qianli Xun said, hoping the stunt would stir a father's concern—or at least crack open the topic she needed.
To her surprise, it worked. Zhu Xiyan spoke at last, face still carved in stone. "I heard you want a mutual divorce from Lin Jue. Is that true?"
With the door cracked open, Qianli Xun pushed straight through.
"Father, you can see the cut on my forehead—Lin Jue gave me that! And you've noticed I'm nothing like your old daughter—that's because he beat her unconscious, and when she woke up, she became me with zero memory of the past! On top of that, he drags brothel courtesans right into the house! Isn't that outrageous? Keeping a man like that as your son-in-law is shoving your daughter into a fiery pit! Father, please step in and arrange a mutual divorce!"
"Are you still clinging to Young Master Xu?" Without even glancing at her, Zhu Xiyan spoke flat and cold. "I may as well tell you today: the Xu family moved north, and Young Master Xu married long ago—he already has a son and a daughter."
Remembering Kuqiao's story about that childhood sweetheart—a total stranger to Qianli Xun—she brushed it off. "Young Master who? Lin Jue beat the memory right out of me—I don't remember anybody!"
"Better that you don't. Then settle down and live your married life—drop this nonsense about mutual divorce."
Qianli Xun gaped. His daughter came home battered, and he didn't blink an eye? What kind of father was this?
"Father, he beats me and fools around with courtesans! How can I keep living with him?"
"Hmph! Think I'm in the dark? Lin Jue came to the prefecture office today, laid out the whole story, and knelt to admit his fault. He hurt you, yet you beat him up just as badly! As for bringing a courtesan home—were you blameless? Had you consummated the marriage when you should have, none of this would have happened."
So Lin Jue had beaten her to the punch with his own twisted tale! Fury boiled over as Qianli Xun shot to her feet. "Father, you can't just listen to his side of the story!"
"And I'm supposed to listen only to yours?"
Stung by his warped logic, Qianli Xun pressed harder: "Father, Lin Jue beat your real daughter to death! I'm a reborn Zhu Shuzhen borrowing her body—not your original daughter! I don't have a single shred of her memories—haven't you noticed? On that alone, you should charge Lin Jue with murder and throw him in jail!"
Zhu Xiyan lifted his eyes to examine her—different indeed, yet identical in face and frame. Dropping his gaze again, he scoffed, "Tales of rebirth are rustic superstition made up to fool simpletons. How could anyone take that seriously? If you aren't Shuzhen, who else could you be?"
Qianli Xun raised a clenched fist. "I know martial arts—did your old Zhu Shuzhen? I beat Lin Jue flat—the old Zhu Shuzhen could only take his blows! Do you still refuse to believe he killed her?"
Madam Xiang burst into loud sobs beside them. "Sob... Shuzhen! What a tragic death!"
Shocked by the exchange, the maids kept their heads low, not daring to whisper.
Zhu Xiyan's voice cut through, calm and steel-edged: "What are you crying for? Joining in the uproar without knowing a thing!"
Madam Xiang choked back her wails at once and dabbed her tears in silence.
Then Zhu Xiyan delivered the last words Qianli Xun ever expected to hear: "Since you claim you are not my daughter Shuzhen, I have even less reason to step forward for your divorce. Leave—your presence spoils my appetite."
Eyes wide, Qianli Xun couldn't believe how heartless Zhu Shuzhen's father was.
"And if I am your daughter?"
"Then even more reason not to divorce! Obey your parents' arrangement and live peaceably with Lin Jue. I married you to him precisely to teach you that life isn't all flowers and moonlight."
Rage surged straight into Qianli Xun's fists. Part of her itched to force him into backing the divorce right then, yet everyone saw him as her elder. All that fury coiled in her arm had to go somewhere.
Watching Zhu Xiyan pick up his chopsticks and keep eating without a flicker of emotion, Qianli Xun snapped—she drove her fist straight down onto the dining table! The wooden table cracked clean in two, sending bowls, plates, and food crashing to the floor. Zhu Xiyan and Madam Xiang scrambled aside while the maids gasped in terror at the reborn young mistress.
Pulling her fist back, Qianli Xun glared at Zhu Xiyan. "Like it or not, I still call you Father. If you won't stand up for me, I'll find my own way—I refuse to believe I can't get out of this marriage!" Turning on her heel, she stormed out.
Behind her stayed a weeping Madam Xiang and an outwardly composed Zhu Xiyan—who, deep inside, could no longer deny that this daughter was no longer the Shuzhen he had known.
#CH 14
#TITLE: Chapter 14: Turns Out I'm Rich
Outside waited Kuqiao, lantern in hand. Seeing her mistress emerge, she hurried over. "I figured it'd be a while longer—didn't expect you out so soon, Miss."
Silent, Qianli Xun marched straight out of the Main Courtyard—only to stop dead the moment she cleared the gate, clueless which way led back.
Sensing her dark mood, Kuqiao held back questions and trailed quiet behind. Seeing her halt, she stepped ahead. "Just follow me, Miss."
Another maze of winding paths.
Paying no mind to Zhu Manor's shadowed walkways, Qianli Xun racked her brain for a plan. With her father refusing to show, nobody in Zhu Manor would back her—did that mean the mutual divorce was dead on arrival? Was she really doomed to spend her transmigrated life stuck with Lin Jue?
Sparse as historical records on Zhu Shuzhen were, Qianli Xun knew the outline: Zhu Shuzhen never divorced Lin Jue, lived estranged from him for years, later fell in love with another man outside marriage, lost him to war, and died brokenhearted in her forties.
Live out that script? Never! She was rewriting this destiny—starting by ending this marriage!
Before she knew it, they stood back at Zhu Shuzhen's garden. Strange place, Zhu Manor—watch every turn and it felt endless; tune out and you arrived in half the time.
Kuqiao pushed the garden gate open, lighting the dark yard with her lantern. Not a single lantern hung outside—pitch black compared to Zhu Xiyan's blazing Main Courtyard. "Too dark in here," Qianli Xun said. "We need more lanterns."
Hearing them, Lutao stepped out to greet her. "Welcome back, Miss. Your bed is all made whenever you're ready to rest."
"Lutao, you're in charge of this courtyard?"
"Yes, Miss."
"Tomorrow grab some maids and hang lanterns all around this yard just like Father's Main Courtyard—light them up at night so it isn't so gloomy."
Lutao blinked in confusion. "We used to hang lanterns here, Miss, until you said you disliked them and ordered them all taken down. Are you... sure you want them back up?"
"Sure—one hundred percent sure!" Qianli Xun stepped inside.
Kuqiao passed the lantern to Lutao. "Just do as Miss says, Sister."
Taking the lantern, Lutao walked off muttering under her breath, "Heard the other maids whispering Miss isn't the same—guess they weren't kidding. Oh well, mistress's orders are orders. I'll have them hung soon."
With Kuqiao's help, Qianli Xun climbed into bed, still turning options over. The Zhu household looked like a bust—even her brother and sister-in-law probably dared not defy her father. A whole day of high hopes shattered flat by one stone-faced dad.
Still, worries or not, Qianli Xun had always been the type to knock out the second her head hit the pillow, and she slept solid through the night.
...
At the first rooster crow of dawn, her eyes blinked open—first time in her life an actual rooster had been her alarm clock. Kind of novel.
Pulling back the bed curtains, she slid out of bed, threw on an outer robe, and pushed open the window. Fresh morning air rushed in; she drew a deep breath. "Smells so clean—like fresh greens!"
Perched atop the courtyard wall stood a rooster with a bright red comb, sturdy neck, and glossy black feathers glinting red. Stretching its neck, it let out another crisp crow: "Cock-a-doodle-doo!"
Woken too, Kuqiao stepped out of the maids' room, snatched a pebble from the ground, and tossed it at the bird. "Quit making a racket here!" Startled, the rooster flapped over the wall and vanished.
"Chased it off, Miss. Go back to sleep for a bit."
"Wide awake already. Might as well get up."
Kuqiao came inside to help her dress and fix her hair.
"Why does Zhu Manor have a rooster?" Qianli Xun asked.
"Could belong to one of the servants—or maybe the Buddhist Shrine."
"Buddhist Shrine?"
Straightening her mistress's collar, Kuqiao explained, "Zhu Manor has a Buddhist Shrine where an Old Ancestor lives—over a hundred years old! Neither you nor I have ever seen her, though, because she never steps out of that shrine."
"Not even my father?"
"No idea—never once saw Master go near the shrine."
"This manor is full of mysteries. Wait—why guess the rooster belongs to the Buddhist Shrine?"
"Habit around here! Whenever something odd turns up in Zhu Manor, folks shrug and say it's probably from the Buddhist Shrine. Slipped right out."
"Got it."
Too busy to dwell on shrines, Qianli Xun waited until Kuqiao finished her hair and ordered, "Skip the fancy whisked tea this morning—grab me something simple to drink. I want to go see my brother and sister-in-law early and check if they'll still back me."
Worry flickered across Kuqiao's face. "Did Master refuse to appear at your divorce meeting last night?"
Qianli Xun nodded.
"Don't bother asking, Miss—Eldest Young Master and Young Madam won't dare step forward."
"Because of Father?" She already knew the answer, yet asked anyway.
Kuqiao nodded. "Always been that way in Zhu Manor. Once Master says no, nobody dares defy him—all they can do is fret."
"Why?"
"Zhu family discipline is ruthless—Master's word is the law."
"Hmph! Family discipline my foot—he's a straight-up Fascist!"
Kuqiao had no idea what a "Fascist" was; her changed mistress tossed out strange words all the time.
Qianli Xun thought of another angle. "Does the Zhu clan have outside relatives in town like the Lin family?"
"Yes! Five or six of your uncles live right here in Lin'an."
Hope flared bright again. "Just when the road looks blocked, another path opens! Today I'll visit every single uncle—no way I can't talk at least one into coming!"
Going empty-handed felt weak; if she wanted elders on her side, lavish gifts—or cold silver—would grease the wheels. Patting her sleeves, she came up empty; fresh off transmigrating, she hadn't needed cash yet and forgot to grab any before leaving Lin Manor.
"Kuqiao, run back to Lin Manor and get some silver from Lin Jue—he promised me spending money."
"If you need silver, Miss, I can fetch it from Lin Manor, no need to ask Son-in-law."
"Wait—if not from Lin Jue, then who?"
"You brought a huge dowry when you married, Miss—landed estates and shops galore! You can't even spend half the annual rent they bring in. Son-in-law isn't nearly as rich as you!"
"What?!" Qianli Xun shot to her feet. "I'm loaded?! Then what am I scared of?! People say women marry for clothes and a roof—I don't need Lin Jue for either! Why on earth would I stay married to him? This divorce is happening, and I'm taking every last bit of my dowry with me! What a fool I was making him promise me spending money—no wonder the sly bastard smiled and agreed! Kuqiao, right after breakfast, go fetch silver from Lin Manor—the more the better!"
"Yes, Miss!"
Right then Lutao led servants in with breakfast. After eating and watching Kuqiao tidy the study desk, just as Kuqiao prepared to leave for Lin Manor, Yinxing's voice rang from outside: "Madam is here to see Miss."
"Hold off on Lin Manor," Qianli Xun told Kuqiao. "Let's hear what Mother has to say first."
Madam Xiang walked in, saw her daughter waiting, and clasped her hands. "Shuzhen, Mother barely slept a wink last night. Even changed as you are, you're still my flesh and blood. Please don't hold a grudge against your father."
"If you, Brother, and Sister-in-law back me at the divorce meeting, I won't hold a grudge."
Letting go of her hands, Madam Xiang sighed in distress. "Once your father says no, nobody in this house dares step forward."
"Then don't ask me not to resent him! My father is cold-blooded—doesn't care a bit about his daughter's happiness, only his precious reputation! How much reputation can one mutual divorce even cost him?"
Madam Xiang sank into a chair. "Sit down and hear Mother out."
Qianli Xun sat. "What do you want to tell me?"
Madam Xiang turned to Kuqiao first. "Go whisk two bowls of tea for my daughter and me."
Kuqiao went to the tea table; fascinated by whisked tea, Yinxing wandered over to watch.
Madam Xiang let out a long sigh. "You were bright from the cradle, loved books, and taught yourself to write poetry and ci by age ten. Proud that you inherited his gifts, your father paraded you at banquet after banquet until your name as a female ci poet spread across all of Jiangnan. Back then we were close to the Xu family. You and Young Master Xu grew up childhood sweethearts, fond of each other, and both families hoped to seal a marital alliance..."
Uninterested in Zhu Shuzhen's old crush, Qianli Xun cut in, "Why tell me all this, Mother? What does it have to do with my divorce?"
"If you still call yourself Shuzhen, listen to the end."
Qianli Xun bit her tongue and listened.
"Out of nowhere, the Xu family announced they were moving north—claiming southern trade had soured and relocating every shop, without breathing a word about your betrothal. After they left, your father and I told you they had fallen on hard times so you wouldn't break down. Later, at a gathering of Lin'an officials' wives, I overheard gossip: the Xus moved because they didn't want their son marrying you! Some geomancer told them marrying a talented woman would ruin their family fortune and sink every venture..."
"Complete garbage!" Qianli Xun snapped on Zhu Shuzhen's behalf.
Finished with the tea, Kuqiao and Yinxing brought the two bowls over and set them down.
Too fired up to savor it, Qianli Xun drained her bowl in one gulp and slammed it down. "Mother, you and Father should thank your lucky stars you didn't marry me into that house! Narrow-minded fools like the Xus aren't worth being in-laws with!"
Madam Xiang took a small sip. "Your father is Prefect of Lin'an—the Xus dared not offend him openly, so they made up an excuse to leave. They still run businesses here, just live up north now, and their son married someone else. My poor girl waited five or six years for nothing!" Madam Xiang dabbed at fresh tears.
"Why didn't you and Father tell me the truth?"
Composing herself, Madam Xiang went on, "We didn't want to crush your heart! Your father acts cold, yet he cares in his own way. He regretted pushing you into poetry and banquets, yet by then your fame stretched across Jiangnan and you kept company with scholars and young gentlemen. Ugly rumors—even crude slander—reached his ears, making him believe 'a woman's virtue is having no talent.' Right then the Lin family proposed. Your father weighed it and figured someone different like Lin Jue would bring you down to earth to raise a family instead of chasing dreams. Besides, you weren't getting any younger—he feared waiting longer would ruin your chances."
"Ridiculous! Utter nonsense!" Qianli Xun burst out. "You can see for yourself, Mother—marrying Lin Jue brought Zhu Shuzhen nothing but misery! Even I want out of this marriage!"
Her phrasing stung Madam Xiang again. "You... still don't consider yourself Shuzhen?"
Catching her slip at once, Qianli Xun backpedaled. "Of course I'm your daughter, Mother!"
Rising, Madam Xiang stepped close and cupped her face. "Let Mother look at you... yes, you are Shuzhen. My Shuzhen is still alive."
Touched, Qianli Xun stood too. "No matter how much I change, you're still my mother."
"I know." Tucking a stray lock behind her daughter's ear, Madam Xiang said, "I told you all this so you understand your father meant well—don't hate him. Now here's an idea: since your father won't appear, go to the Zhu clan elders. Six of your uncles live right here in Lin'an. I already sent word to your brother to take you visiting today—he's waiting outside the gate right now."
Overjoyed, Qianli Xun hugged Madam Xiang tight. "Mother, you're the best mother in the whole world!"
Patting her daughter's back, Madam Xiang laughed through her tears. "Getting divorced and grinning ear to ear!"
Pulling back, Qianli Xun asked, "Should I bring silver or gifts for the uncles?"
Madam Xiang shook her head with a smile. "We're all one family—gifts or money would feel stiff."
Eager to move, Qianli Xun grabbed her skirt. "I'm going right now, Mother! Kuqiao, what are you staring at? Let's roll!"
Watching her daughter dash out—both familiar and foreign—Madam Xiang murmured a sigh. "Heaven grant those uncles don't care what your father thinks..."
#CH 15
#TITLE: Chapter 15: Calling on the Clan Elders
Outside Zhu Manor's side gate waited Zhu Jingyuan beside a hitched carriage.
The second Qianli Xun stepped out, she threw her arms around him in glee. "Having a brother is the best!"
Still adjusting to his bold new sister, Zhu Jingyuan couldn't help grinning anyway. "Took leave from office today just to make the rounds of the clan elders with you. Let's talk inside the carriage."
Climbing aboard, Kuqiao took the seat opposite and watched her mistress cozy up to Eldest Young Master, thinking to herself: The old Miss never acted this close with Eldest Young Master—now they're thick as thieves!
The carriage rolled down quiet lanes into bustling Lin'an streets, lively chatter spilling past the curtains.
With his sister tucked against his arm, Zhu Jingyuan looked right content. "Mother told me what happened last night. Once Father says no, nobody under his roof dares step out for you. Don't blame Mother, or Wenxin and me."
"Why would I blame you? Father's the heartless one!" Leaning her head against his shoulder, Qianli Xun asked, "Heard if you cross him, he brings out family discipline—what does that even look like?"
"Twenty blows of the cane for the men, palm strikes for the women, kneeling in the ancestral hall for two days on water alone—and confined inside Zhu Manor for a full year."
Sitting bolt upright, Qianli Xun fumed, "Total Fascist!"
"Fascist? What's that?" Zhu Jingyuan blinked.
"Means Father is a ruthless tyrant!"
Recalling Madam Xiang's tale of how Shuzhen had smashed a dining table clean in half with one fist—something the old Shuzhen lacked both nerve and muscle to do—Zhu Jingyuan knew his sister had changed for good.
"Shuzhen, gaining all that strength after your rebirth... I know you aren't the same as before, yet in my heart you'll always be my little sister."
Warmed to the core, Qianli Xun leaned back on his shoulder. "And you'll always be my big brother."
"At least with you this strong, I won't worry about anyone pushing you around," Zhu Jingyuan went on. "You probably don't remember, yet His Majesty prizes scholars above all, lifting literary figures to the skies across the empire. You're a celebrated poet here in Lin'an—tell me you didn't lose your gift for poetry and ci?"
Unwilling to disappoint him—with Zhu Shuzhen's works locked in her head anyway—Qianli Xun sat straight and met his eyes. "Relax, Brother! Your sister's poetic genius is right where it belongs—didn't lose a scrap!"
"I can vouch for that, Young Master!" Kuqiao chimed in, remembering how her mistress had recited "Chrysanthemum" just yesterday—a poem the old Miss had written one tipsy evening after rare cups of wine. If she remembered her own verse, her talent lived on—a blessing amid disaster.
"Good, good! As long as your talent remains, the title Poetess of Jiangnan won't slip away."
The carriage halted outside the first clan uncle's gate. Zhu Jingyuan left his sister and Kuqiao inside while he went in alone.
Minutes later he stepped back into the carriage and shook his head—no luck.
Five houses in a row, exact same result.
"Why won't any of our uncles back me, Brother?" Qianli Xun asked.
Zhu Jingyuan sighed. "Because our father is Prefect of Lin'an—none of them dare cross him! First question out of every mouth was, 'Does the Prefect approve?' The instant they heard Father said no, every last one pulled his head into his shell like a turtle."
"Brother, you're too honest! Why didn't you lie and tell them Father sent you to invite them?"
"Can't lie about something this big! Mutual divorce decides your whole future. Drag them there under false pretenses, they spot Father missing and walk out—you'd lose face and ruin your reputation!"
"Good thinking, Brother. One uncle left—let me go in with you so he sees I'm sincere."
The last elder, an older cousin-uncle senior to Zhu Xiyan, received the pair politely—only to ask the Prefect's stance first, refuse outright, and launch into a lecture about preserving a woman's good name, warning that even mutual divorce scarred a lady's reputation and made remarriage hard.
Squeezing out tears on command, Qianli Xun wailed, "Uncle! If your own daughter married a husband who beat her and dragged courtesans home, would you leave her trapped in that fiery pit? Sob...!"
Moved by her logic and tear-streaked beauty, the elder softened. "Were you my own daughter, I'd step forward in a heartbeat. Yet your father is Prefect of Lin'an—who dares cross him? Still, I'll give you a hint: there is one person in all of Lin'an whom your father dares not say a single word against."
Perking up through half-fake tears, Qianli Xun asked, "Who?!"
Before the uncle could answer, light dawned on Zhu Jingyuan. Jumping up with cupped hands, he bowed. "Thank you for pointing the way, Uncle! I'll take Shuzhen to see the Old Ancestor right now!"
The elder chuckled. "Go on! If you two can move her, she alone outweighs every elder in the Zhu clan."
Still fuzzy on who this Old Ancestor was, Qianli Xun beamed at having a way out, bade the uncle farewell, and climbed back into the carriage behind her brother.
Inside, bursting with curiosity, she asked, "Brother, who on earth is this Old Ancestor that even the Prefect of Lin'an can't defy?"
Calling out "Home!" to the driver, Zhu Jingyuan explained, "She lives inside Zhu Manor's Buddhist Shrine—a hundred and twenty years old! She's our grandfather's grandfather's mother. Grandparents, great-grandparents, even great-great-grandparents have all passed on, yet she's still alive and well—amazing, right? With her age and seniority, she doesn't give Father a second glance."
The tongue-twister family tree made Qianli Xun's head spin; forget tracking generations, all that mattered was she outranked Zhu Xiyan!
"Brother, if you and Mother had thought of her sooner, we wouldn't have wasted all day running around Lin'an!"
"Can't blame Mother or me—she stays shut inside the Buddhist Shrine year after year, forbids visitors, even Father rarely lays eyes on her. Over time everybody forgets she's there."
"A hundred and twenty years old... can she even walk?"
"No idea—heard of her all my life, never met her once."
Fresh worry crept in. At one hundred and twenty, could she even leave the shrine, let alone trek out of Zhu Manor for a divorce? Still, dead horse or not, Qianli Xun had to try doctoring it—no other play left.
#CH 16
#TITLE: Chapter 16: Appealing to the Old Ancestor
By the layout of Zhu Manor, the Buddhist Shrine sat tucked even deeper than Zhu Shuzhen's garden.
Following Zhu Jingyuan into the farthest reaches of the estate, Qianli Xun spotted a small hillock ahead—and nestled at its base, a walled compound shaped like a temple, low enough that the rooftops showed clear above.
"Brother, did Zhu Manor haul an actual hill inside the walls?"
"Not a real hill—just a massive earth mound. When they built the manor, they hauled tons of soil in from outside Lin'an just to raise that mound."
Next came a clear, shallow stream spanned by stepping-stones leading straight toward the shrine.
Hopping across the stones, the three stepped onto a pebble path that poked through their soles like a foot massage, then crossed a patch of wild grass before reaching the shrine gate. Closed tight beneath a modest plaque reading "Buddhist Shrine," it vibrated with the steady clack of a wooden fish inside.
"Wait here, Shuzhen—I'll knock," Zhu Jingyuan said.
Qianli Xun nodded as her brother stepped up and rapped three times. Silence. Three more knocks—still nothing. On the third round of knocks, footsteps sounded inside, lifting all three spirits.
A bolt slid back and the wooden door creaked open to reveal a silver-haired elderly woman. "Who are you?"
Kuqiao stepped forward with a polite bow. "Good day, Matron. This is Eldest Young Master and Miss from the manor, come to pay respects to the Old Ancestor."
"Ah, the young master and miss," the matron said. "Being young, you may not know—the Old Ancestor devotes herself solely to the Buddha and never sees worldly guests. Please go back." She started shutting the door.
Zhu Jingyuan wedged a hand up to stop her. "We know well, Matron, and wouldn't dare disturb her peace lightly—yet my sister faces a crisis of a lifetime and begs an audience. Please grant us this favor."
"The Old Ancestor has severed all worldly ties and purified her senses—how could she meddle in mortal affairs? Be good and leave."
This was her last shot—no elder, no divorce. Even one ally improved her odds, and Qianli Xun refused to let go of a single thread of hope. Raising her voice, she called straight over the matron's shoulder: "The Old Ancestor cultivates the Buddha's Dharma—doesn't she know the bodhisattvas vow to deliver all sentient beings from suffering? Right now one of those beings—her own descendant however many generations down—is in dire trouble and needs a bodhisattva to step out and save her! Hiding inside a shrine chasing private peace—what kind of Buddhist practice is that?!"
The matron bristled. "Impertinent child! How dare you lecture the Old Ancestor!"
Inside, the wooden fish stopped dead. An aged yet resonant voice rang out: "Xiuzhen, let her in."
Delighted, all three moved forward, only for Matron Xiuzhen to bar the doorway. "Didn't you hear the Old Ancestor say her alone? You two wait outside."
Zhu Jingyuan and Kuqiao halted outside the gate.
Heart racing, Qianli Xun followed Matron Xiuzhen in.
The tiny courtyard lay spare and spotless, its centerpiece three wintersweet trees—long past bloom, yet thick with lush green leaves.
Inside the main shrine hall towered a grand statue of the Buddha that inspired instant awe. Three incense sticks smoldered in a censer on the altar table before it, with a woven prayer cushion laid on the floor.
"Since you've entered, young lady, pay respects first," Matron Xiuzhen instructed.
With a favor to beg, Qianli Xun obeyed without fuss—palms pressed together, kneeling on the cushion for three bows, thinking: Haven't even met the Old Ancestor yet, already bowing to Buddha!
Rising, before she could take in the rest of the hall, she heard Matron Xiuzhen announce: "Old Ancestor, the young Zhu descendant is here—please give her a good scolding."
Qianli Xun turned. Left of the altar sat a low table holding a wooden fish and mallet, and behind it sat a completely white-haired elder—deep-set eyes, high nose bridge, face etched top to bottom with wrinkles. Yet neither wrinkles nor scattered age spots dimmed the luminous pallor of her skin; she must have been a radiant beauty in her youth. Aged as that face was, kindness radiated from it, wrapping Qianli Xun in an unexpected glow of warmth at first sight.
Eyes half-closed, legs crossed, the Old Ancestor rolled prayer beads through her fingers while murmuring sutras. Hearing Xiuzhen, she spoke: "Have her come over."
Knowing she was summoned, Qianli Xun stepped forward.
Matron Xiuzhen gestured toward a prayer cushion; awkward as sitting cross-legged felt, Qianli Xun lowered herself obediently.
Still rolling her beads with eyes nearly shut, the Old Ancestor said, "Your speech outside showed some spirit, which is why I invited you in. Tell me why you came."
Qianli Xun assembled her pitch fast and plunged in: "As a woman yourself, Old Ancestor, you know best how hard women's lives are. Obeying parents' orders and matchmakers' words, I married a husband unmatched in mind or character. I could have endured even that if he had a decent heart—instead, he beats me and brings brothel courtesans into the house! Unable to bear another day, I sought mutual divorce. Yet Father refuses to step in, and without his nod, not a single elder in the family or clan dares back me. With nowhere left to turn, I came to beg your mercy—please step forward and preside over my mutual divorce."
At last the Old Ancestor opened her eyes. Surrounded by folds of age, her gaze shone clear and sharp—holding Qianli Xun transfixed.
"A woman's life is indeed hard. Yet why should I take your word alone and preside over a divorce? If your father opposes it, perhaps you're simply acting rash."
"With respect, Old Ancestor, I know full well how grave marriage is for a woman—how could I act rash with my own future? Living in a loveless marriage feels like sinking in a swamp with no footing. Even so, I will struggle with all I have; dying in the fight beats living trapped in misery."
"Your resolve runs deep, I see. Tell me then—why does your father refuse to back you?"
"He believes a woman's virtue lies in having no talent. He regrets raising me as a ci poet and thinks marrying my current husband will force me to serve my spouse and raise children instead of writing verse and chasing airy dreams. Above all, he guards the Zhu family's face and fears even mutual divorce will tarnish its honor."
"Your father isn't wrong—he simply sees things differently from you. Still, if your husband beats you and brings courtesans home, even I agree no woman should endure that. Doesn't your father know? Why would he still oppose the divorce?"
Unwilling to hold anything back, Qianli Xun spoke plain: "Father knows everything. He still opposes it because he thinks I'm at fault."
"Oh? Tell me how."
"On our wedding night, I refused to consummate the marriage and threatened suicide. Day after day I ignored my husband and treated him like ice. So Father blames me, refuses divorce, and orders me back to behave."
The Old Ancestor let her eyelids droop once more and fell silent. Anxiety gripped Qianli Xun—would she turn her down too? The air seemed frozen solid, broken only by the dry click of prayer beads.
At length the Old Ancestor spoke again: "Since you are a ci poet, compose a lyric before me right now. Move my heart, and I will stand up for you. Xiuzhen—brush and paper."
At a desk to the right of the Buddha, Matron Xiuzhen laid out paper and ground fresh ink. "Everything is ready, young lady. Come write."
The instant the Old Ancestor asked for a lyric, Qianli Xun began ransacking her memory of Zhu Shuzhen's poems—which one would strike home?
Even as Matron Xiuzhen beckoned, she drew a blank and stalled, walking slow toward the desk. Every poem of Zhu Shuzhen's carried power, yet picking the exact one to move a century-old Buddhist elder into backing a divorce wasn't easy.
Seated at the desk, dipping her brush into ink, she happened to glance up—and saw a single massive calligraphy character hanging behind the Old Ancestor: "Riddle" (谜). Curiosity struck. "Matron, most Buddhists hang 'Zen' or 'Serenity' on their wall—why does the Old Ancestor hang 'Riddle'?"
"The Old Ancestor holds that mortals stay ignorant because they dwell inside a riddle," Matron Xiuzhen replied. "She keeps that character there as a constant reminder to break past illusion and grasp life's truth—only then can one cast off greed, anger, delusion, and pride to reach inner peace."
Half the Buddhist philosophy sailed right over Qianli Xun's head—yet that single word "riddle" unlocked the perfect lyric in her mind! Her brush flew across the paper:
Coming downstairs, golden coins drop for divination; I ask High Heaven, where has he gone? Resenting the noble lord who walked straight away; cursing the heartless one—speaking of parting, hard to hold back.
Regretting how I first misspoke; there is upper union, no lower tie. Why ask right from wrong? Parting needs no knife; from this day on lean no more on a foe—a thousand kinds of longing wiped away with one stroke. ("The Heartbreak Riddle") [TN: Translator's rendering]
Setting down the brush, Qianli Xun lifted the sheet, blew across the wet ink, and handed it to Matron Xiuzhen, who spread it on the table before the Old Ancestor.
The Old Ancestor halted her beads, raised her eyes to the page, and read every character with deep attention before declaring: "Go back. I agree to step forward for you."
Joy surged through Qianli Xun. "Your descendant thanks you, Old Ancestor!"
Walking her to the door, Matron Xiuzhen instructed, "Since the Old Ancestor has consented, send someone tomorrow with a note stating the time and place of the divorce meeting and slip it under the gate."
"Count on it, Matron! Only—I'm worried the Old Ancestor might not know the way. Should I send a carriage to fetch her?"
"No need. At a hundred and twenty, what hasn't the Old Ancestor seen? Wait where you are—I will bring her myself. Farewell, young lady."
Gesturing her out, Matron Xiuzhen shut the shrine gate firm behind her.
Zhu Jingyuan and Kuqiao hurried up.
"Well, Sister? Did the Old Ancestor agree?"
Qianli Xun nodded with a breezy smile. "She agreed!"
Thrilled for her mistress, Kuqiao asked, "What's our next move, Miss?"
Qianli Xun answered crisp and clear: "Back to Lin Manor!"
#CH 17
#TITLE: Chapter 17: Buying Medicine at Jixiang Pharmacy
Impatient by nature, once Qianli Xun decided to return to Lin Manor, she moved at once—not because she missed the place, but to feel out Lin Jue's hand and lock down every detail for the divorce. After going through hoops to summon a heavy hitter like the Old Ancestor, she wasn't leaving anything to chance.
She had Kuqiao order a carriage. The Lin coachman had long since gone back, so Zhu Manor's driver had to make the run. Soon they were rolling back toward Lin Manor.
Halfway there, Qianli Xun felt her forehead cut itch—nearly healed. That reminded her of the punch she had landed on Lin Jue; his black eye couldn't have faded yet, and he had already tattled to Zhu Xiyan about it. What if he waved that bruise around at the divorce meeting to muddy the waters?
"Kuqiao, got silver on you?"
"Not much—just five taels I packed for you, Miss."
"Enough for medicine?"
"Depends what kind."
"Lin Jue's eye is still bruised from my punch. I want to buy ointment to clear it up."
"Plenty for that! Wait—why are you being nice to Son-in-law all of a sudden?"
"Remember this, Kuqiao: sometimes being nice to someone is just a way to get what you want."
Lifting the window curtain, Qianli Xun scanned the street for a pharmacy.
Reading her mind, Kuqiao said, "No need to look, Miss—just up ahead is Jixiang Pharmacy. Best trauma salves in all of Lin'an. The ointment I put on your forehead came from there."
At Jixiang Pharmacy, Kuqiao called a halt and helped her mistress step down.
Stepping inside, a rich scent of dried herbs washed over them. Wooden shelving lined every wall, row after row stacked with jars and vials of every shape.
An apothecary clerk stepped out from behind the counter with a welcoming grin. "What medicine can I fetch for you today, young lady?"
Glancing around the shop, Qianli Xun asked, "Someone took a punch to the eye and has a nasty bruise—got anything that clears it up fast?"
The clerk beamed. "Came to the right place! Can't brag about everything, yet for bruises like that, dab this on tonight and by tomorrow it's gone without a trace!"
He reached up and pulled down a small porcelain vial. "Right here."
Qianli Xun took it. "We'll take it. Hold this, Kuqiao." Kuqiao stepped up to tuck it away.
"One hundred wen for this vial," the clerk said.
"Hold on, you'll get your money. I need a second medicine—see this cut on my forehead..."
Before she could finish, the clerk cut in, "Your cut is practically healed, miss! Don't waste coin on more salve—we run an honest shop and never trick customers into buying what they don't need."
Qianli Xun raised her voice. "Who said anything about healing it?! I want to make this wound look worse—without leaving a scar! Got anything like that?"
Never having met a buyer asking to harm herself, the clerk frowned. "We sell medicine to heal and save lives, never potions to hurt people! Don't put me in a bind, miss."
Qianli Xun held out her hand. "Kuqiao—the silver!"
Kuqiao pulled out the money pouch. Qianli Xun fished out one tael of silver and set it on the counter. "Got any?"
Eyeballing the silver, the clerk stood firm. "No."
Qianli Xun laid down a second tael. "Got any?"
Unmoved: "No!"
A third tael hit the wood. "Got any?"
Before the clerk could refuse again, the shopkeeper brushed aside the back curtain, grinned ear to ear, and scooped up all three taels. "Yes! Of course we do!"
Pocketing the silver, the shopkeeper pulled a tiny porcelain vial from beneath the counter and handed it over. "Miss, this is Corrosive Paste. Just a tiny dab on your cut will make it flare up looking fierce. Whenever you want it healed fast, just dab on the first salve you bought."
"Thanks, boss!" Turning to the clerk, Qianli Xun teased, "Take notes from your boss—gotta stay flexible in business!"
Handing the vials and remaining change to Kuqiao, she stepped back out to the carriage for Lin Manor.
Kuqiao frowned down at the two vials. "Three whole taels—highway robbery! You shouldn't have dropped silver one tael at a time, Miss—both vials together aren't worth a single string of cash!"
"If I hadn't, would the boss have pulled out Corrosive Paste?"
"Still don't get what you're thinking—spending all that silver just to buy ointment to hurt yourself!"
"Sometimes hurting yourself a little is the smartest move you can make. You'll see soon enough."
Back inside Lin Manor, Qianli Xun glanced around hoping to catch sight of Lin Jue's two brothers, yet only busy servants crossed the paths.
Heading west back to her shared courtyard, she learned from servants that Lin Jue wasn't back yet and went straight to Zhulan Residence. With divorce looming, she needed a full accounting of her own assets before Lin Jue could pull a fast one.
Barely seated—not even waiting for tea—she asked, "Kuqiao, who manages my dowry?"
Setting the two new vials into a round bamboo medicine basket, Kuqiao answered, "You poured your whole heart into poetry and never bothered with accounts, Miss—Matron Wei handles all of it for you."
"Matron Wei?"
"Your wet nurse! When Madam gave birth to you, she lacked enough milk and hired Matron Wei to nurse you. Afterward Matron Wei stayed to raise you, trusted completely by Madam, and came with you when you married to manage your dowry. Whenever you need cash, just ask Matron Wei—never mind Son-in-law."
"Why haven't I seen her around?"
"She took servants out to the countryside these past few days to collect rent. Once she's done, she'll be back and you'll meet her."
One of her own people holding the purse strings—zero worry of Lin Jue skimming her assets. "I'm beat from running around all day—going to grab a nap. Tell me the second Lin Jue gets back."
After helping her mistress lie down, Kuqiao closed the door and went to check on Son-in-law.
Lin Jue's page boy promised to notify her the moment his master returned. Reassured, Kuqiao returned to Zhulan Residence and watered the bamboo and orchids—normally sweeping maids' work, just something to pass the time.
She had just finished watering when the page boy dashed over. "Sister Kuqiao, Master is back!"
Thanking him, Kuqiao slipped inside to the bedside and whispered, "Miss, Son-in-law's back."
Too wired over the Old Ancestor agreeing to appear, Qianli Xun hadn't fallen asleep, only resting with shut eyes. Hearing Kuqiao, she sat right up and parted the drapes. "Right on time! Hand me the bruise ointment."
Slipping on her clothes, smoothing her hair without pinning ornaments, and pocketing the vial Kuqiao handed her, she strode straight out of Zhulan Residence to Lin Jue's quarters and shoved the door open.
Having just changed clothes with a servant's help, Lin Jue spun around at the noise.
Spotting the bruise still circling his left eye, Qianli Xun smiled inwardly—good thing she had bought this ointment so he couldn't weaponize that punch at the divorce meeting.
"Back from Zhu Manor so soon, Wife? Couldn't get a single elder to show up, huh? If not, settle down in Lin Manor—I won't mistreat you."
"Ha, hate to burst your bubble—if I hadn't lined someone up, why would I come back early? You tattled to my dad first, fine, I won't hold it against you. I'm a softhearted person, unlike you. Figuring we were husband and wife once and I clocked you pretty hard, I felt bad—so I picked up some top-grade ointment passing Jixiang Pharmacy."
She tossed the vial straight into his chest. "Put it on right away, before your troops at the drill ground find out your wife gave you a shiner and cost you your face."
Still smitten with Zhu Shuzhen, seeing her talk nonstop and even buy him medicine sent Lin Jue's imagination racing.
"Well? Put it on!" Eager to make that bruise vanish fast, Qianli Xun frowned as he just clutched the vial in a daze.
Impatient, she stepped up, snatched the vial back, popped the lid, scooped thick salve onto her finger, and smeared it right over the bruise around his left eye.
Snickering at the sight, the servants slipped out the door on quiet feet.
As Qianli Xun finished dabbing and pulled her hand back, Lin Jue caught her wrist. "Why are you suddenly so nice to me?"
Yanking her wrist free, she snorted. "Didn't I just tell you? Soft heart. Don't read into it."
Setting the vial on the table, she added, "Keep applying it tomorrow—don't waste my goodwill."
Turning to go, she remembered the real reason she came and spun back to find him still staring dumbstruck. "Hey! Let's nail down the time and place for the mutual divorce right now."
"Mutual divorce" snapped him cold awake. "Day after tomorrow, the Hour of the Snake, North Courtyard reception hall."
"You know I'm not the old Zhu Shuzhen—remember nothing, don't even know who our matchmaker was. You're in charge of summoning her."
"No problem."
"How many elders are coming on your side?"
"Eight. More wanted to come, had to hold them back."
"What? Eight?!" Qianli Xun's heart dipped—one elder against eight! "I've only got one, you're bringing eight—how's that fair? Can't you bring just one too?"
"Can't help it—Lin clan elders are huge in number and eager to help." Stepping closer to study her face, Lin Jue murmured, "Why bother divorcing me? Even if you aren't the old Zhu Shuzhen, I like you even better now. Why not stay and build a life with me?"
Qianli Xun looked him up and down. Definitely not her type—even if she wasn't sure what her type was, it sure wasn't Lin Jue, especially after their rocky start.
"Save your breath—this divorce is locked in. Only one elder on my side or not, I'm fighting this through!"
With that, she marched out.
Lin Jue let out a cold laugh. "You're the one who should save your breath!"
#CH 18
#TITLE: Chapter 18: Invited to a Poetry Salon
The next day, Qianli Xun penned a note and sent Kuqiao back to Zhu Manor to slip it beneath the Buddhist Shrine's gate.
Mission complete, Kuqiao hurried back to Lin Manor and ran right into Huayu, Dongfang Chenze's page boy, outside the front gate.
One of Lin'an's premier young scholars—handsome, wealthy, and fond of hosting literary gatherings—Dongfang Chenze threw a salon almost every month, with Zhu Shuzhen always an honored guest.
Spotting Kuqiao, Huayu presented an invitation card at once. "Sister Kuqiao, my young master invites your mistress to a poetry salon today at Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake."
Taking the card, Kuqiao remarked, "Your master throws salons every single month—doesn't he ever get tired? My Miss is swamped these days, might not make it."
"My job's just delivering the card; whether she comes is up to her. Task done—farewell!"
After Huayu left, Kuqiao returned to Zhulan Residence, confirmed the note was delivered, and handed over the invitation.
Opening it for a quick glance, Qianli Xun asked, "Who's Dongfang Chenze?"
"A famed young scholar in Lin'an who loves gathering poets," Kuqiao explained. "Master always invites him to our annual banquets—that's how he met you. Whenever he hosts salons of his own, he always invites you too."
"And I usually like going?"
Kuqiao nodded. "Love it! Still, the divorce meeting is tomorrow—are you even in the mood?"
Cooped up between Zhu Manor and Lin Manor for days chasing elders, Qianli Xun felt rusty. Getting fresh air and meeting people sounded great.
"Plenty in the mood! If I always go, I'm going today. Help me fix up—can't show up looking messy."
Once dressed and groomed by Kuqiao, she boarded a carriage bound for West Lake.
Qianli Xun had always meant to vacation at West Lake back in her own era and never found the time—now transmigration handed her West Lake a thousand years in the past! She intended to soak in every view.
Stepping out by the shore, she found an ink-wash painting come to life: calm, crystal waters mirroring rolling hills and lush trees, little bridges over clear streams, ancient willows swaying in a cool breeze, wild ducks gliding across ripples.
Transfixed, Qianli Xun stopped in her tracks—until a woman's voice broke her reverie.
"Youqi Jushi, this is hardly your first trip to West Lake—why so captivated by the scenery today?"
Unfamiliar with the pen name Youqi Jushi, Qianli Xun didn't realize she was being addressed and kept gazing at the water.
Not until Kuqiao whispered in her ear—"Miss, Youqi Jushi is you! That's Miao Zhiyu, daughter of the Vice-Prefect of Lin'an"—did it click.
Right: Zhu Shuzhen, styled Youqi Jushi. Turning with a smile, Qianli Xun beheld a slender classical beauty radiating scholarly poise—even the maid trailing behind her looked refined by association.
Knowing Miao Zhiyu's name yet unsure how Shuzhen addressed her, Qianli Xun played it safe and skipped titles. "I come here often, yet scenery this fine never grows old. Today's view is extra lovely—couldn't help pausing a while."
"What happened to your forehead, Youqi Jushi?"
"Nothing much—just a clumsy bump."
Miao Zhiyu smirked inwardly. Everybody knows the Training Officer beats you—why bother covering it up?
"Heading to Young Master Dongfang's poetry salon?"
"Yes." Keeping answers short lowered the odds of giving herself away.
"Shall we walk together?"
Nodding, Qianli Xun fell into step beside her, holding her tongue. Miao Zhiyu kept quiet too. Soon a vast spread of lotus leaves carpeted the water ahead, dotted with lotus buds nodding in the light breeze. If only I had a phone, Qianli Xun thought—I'd snap fifty photos right here!
Noticing Miao Zhiyu didn't spare the view a glance, she mused how living next to beauty dulled the eye. To look like a local, she tore her eyes away and walked on.
Turning down a narrow path into a lakeside grove, they soon came upon a two-story teahouse. No trees blocked its front door—only flowerbeds opening straight onto tranquil blue ripples.
"After you, Youqi Jushi," Miao Zhiyu said with a graceful gesture worthy of a noble daughter.
Refusing to let Zhu Shuzhen lose on manners, Qianli Xun mirrored the bow. "After you."
