Yielding back and forth, they stepped inside side by side.
The teahouse dripped with taste: flower arrangements on every table, famous calligraphy and paintings lining the walls while guests sipped tea and studied the brushwork. At some tables female tea performers displayed whisking art; at others crowds gathered to watch two tea performers face off in a tea competition.
A waiter hurried up, recognizing them at once. "Welcome, young ladies! Young Master Dongfang is waiting upstairs."
"Any new calligraphy or paintings hung?" Miao Zhiyu asked.
"Same as before, miss—new pieces go up next month."
Miao Zhiyu turned to Qianli Xun. "We've seen all of these already, Youqi Jushi. Let's head right upstairs—I heard Young Master Dongfang obtained a new poem by Yi'an Jushi and invited us to study it."
Yi'an Jushi? Li Qingzhao?! Qianli Xun grinned inside. Wonder which "new" poem he got—all classics to me, hehe!
"If it's Yi'an Jushi's poem, we definitely must examine it."
Following the waiter up to the second floor, they passed private booths each named after famous lines of verse: "Autumn Moonlight Bright on Cassia," "Yan Grass Like Green Silk," "Moistening Things Without a Sound," "Bright Moon Shines Between Pines," and more.
The waiter pushed open a booth labeled "Thick Dew, Slender Blossoms."
That phrase comes from a Li Qingzhao lyric, Qianli Xun thought, though the exact title slipped her mind.
Inside sat three poised, handsome young gentlemen sipping tea at separate low tables. Rising with warm smiles as Zhu Shuzhen and Miao Zhiyu entered, one called out, "Youqi Jushi, Lady Miao—at last! You kept us waiting!"
Aha—everyone called her "Lady Miao," and this host must be Dongfang Chenze. She'd ask Kuqiao about the other two in a second.
Qianli Xun bowed. "Apologies for keeping you three gentlemen waiting."
Miao Zhiyu bowed beside her.
"No need for ceremony, ladies—please sit," Dongfang Chenze urged.
All three gentlemen spotted the healing cut on Zhu Shuzhen's forehead, traded silent glances, and resumed their seats.
Just then a female tea performer entered with an assistant, took her place at the tea table, and began whisking tea. Everyone fell silent to watch.
Compared to Kuqiao's workmanlike moves, this performer's hands flowed with unhurried elegance—a feast for the eyes.
After watching a moment, Qianli Xun crooked a finger at Kuqiao. Kuqiao leaned in close as her mistress whispered, "Center one's Dongfang Chenze—who are the other two?"
A veteran of these salons, Kuqiao whispered back, "Left is Cao Ban, son of Military Commissioner Cao; right is An Mingxuan, son of Intendant An."
Locking both names into memory, Qianli Xun murmured, "How do I usually address them?"
"Always as Young Master." Answer given, Kuqiao stepped back into place.
Relaxed now, Qianli Xun watched the performer draw delicate lines across the foam with a slender bamboo slip. Once done, her assistant lifted the bowl and set it before Qianli Xun. "Please enjoy, Youqi Jushi."
Even the teahouse assistant knows her—Shuzhen really is a regular here, Qianli Xun mused, nodding thanks.
Glancing down, she caught her breath: delicate strokes on the tea foam formed willow branches curving around a bright moon. Too lovely to drink! People a thousand years ago lived with far more elegance than modern folks. Willows and moon brought back the earthquake—and Shen Jianchen. Where had the Divine Willow carried him?
The assistant set the next bowl before Miao Zhiyu. "Your tea, Lady Miao."
Bowing to the company, the tea performer withdrew with her assistant.
"Please drink, ladies," Dongfang Chenze invited.
Miao Zhiyu took a dainty sip, set her bowl down, and spoke: "I hear Young Master Dongfang obtained a new poem by Yi'an Jushi—please bring it out for us to admire!"
"Let Youqi Jushi taste her tea first before we turn to poetry," Dongfang Chenze suggested.
On cue, Qianli Xun lifted the bowl and gulped that poetic masterpiece straight down sip after big sip.
Setting down the empty bowl, she met four bewildered stares. Realizing she had drunk way too fast, she covered with a sheepish smile. "Parched from the ride over."
Dongfang Chenze chuckled. "I'll have the performer whisk you another bowl."
"No, no, plenty already!" Flushing slightly, she waved him off—no holding up the poetry session on her account.
Miao Zhiyu urged again, "Then let's see Yi'an Jushi's poem right away!"
Dongfang Chenze waved to his page boy Huayu. "Hang Yi'an Jushi's poem on the wall."
Huayu unrolled two scrolls and hung them side by side.
Every pair of eyes turned to the wall.
Qianli Xun cheered inwardly: Li Qingzhao's poem "Reflections"! With the prose preface on the right scroll! I know this one—no chance of embarrassing myself!
Dongfang Chenze introduced it: "Friends, Yi'an Jushi wrote this last year—not brand-new, yet hard to come by. Thanks to Brother Qiao Wenxian knowing a close friend of hers, he secured a copy and sent it to me by letter so we could all study it together."
At the name Qiao Wenxian, rosy color bloomed across Miao Zhiyu's cheeks. "Is Young Master Qiao in Lin'an, or still in Bianliang?"
"Were Brother Qiao in Lin'an, I'd certainly have invited him today," Dongfang Chenze replied. "Ever since Youqi Jushi married the Training Officer, Brother Qiao left for Bianliang and hasn't come back—word is he's staying at his nephew Prince Yi's Manor."
All three young gentlemen turned straight toward Zhu Shuzhen to gauge her reaction.
Flustered under three probing stares, Qianli Xun offered an awkward smile. Why did Dongfang Chenze go out of his way to link Qiao Wenxian leaving town with Zhu Shuzhen's wedding? What was between those two? Those looks felt awfully loaded.
#CH 19
#TITLE: Chapter 19: Displaying Literary Wit at the Teahouse
Noticing Zhu Shuzhen's discomfort and realizing their staring crossed a line, Cao Ban broke the awkward hush.
"Arriving with an injury again, Youqi Jushi—looks like the Training Officer laid hands on you once more! If it comes to it, report him straight to the Prefect—see if he dares pull that again!"
Realizing her friends already knew about the domestic abuse took a load off Qianli Xun's mind. "Don't worry, everyone—there won't be a next time. Tomorrow I'm getting a mutual divorce from Lin Jue."
Miao Zhiyu nodded. "High time! We all grieved for you the day you married that Training Officer."
An Mingxuan agreed, "If you break free of him, Youqi Jushi, we'll all celebrate for you!"
A sly grin curved Dongfang Chenze's lips. "And one person out there will celebrate harder than all of us combined."
The three men traded knowing smiles.
Clueless about the inside joke—having zero of Shuzhen's memories—Qianli Xun couldn't care less and steered them back: "Yi'an Jushi's poem is right in front of us—let's get to discussing it."
Because Qiao Wenxian had gone out of his way to obtain this poem, Miao Zhiyu had eyes for the verse alone, half imagining his presence between the lines, oblivious to the gentlemen's knowing looks.
Prompted by Zhu Shuzhen, Miao Zhiyu spoke first: "From the preface we see Yi'an Jushi is in Laizhou, reunited with Master Defu—which ought to bring joy. Yet every line radiates solitude and chill. Has strain crept into their marriage? If even they grow estranged, how heartbreaking for women like us!"
Curious, An Mingxuan asked, "Why heartbreaking for women?"
"Every woman envies Yi'an Jushi—marrying the man she loves and sharing his mind and soul," Miao Zhiyu replied. "If even she feels lonely, unmarried daughters like me have no idea what sort of husband to hope for! Compared to this, I still prefer: 'Don't say she's not heartbroken, / The west wind lifts the blinds, / She's more wasted than the yellow flowers.' [TN: Translated by Ronald Egan] At least those lines carry the sweetness of longing." A wistful shadow lingered on her face.
Cao Ban spoke next: "Nothing delights me more than historical allusions—where else can I show off my reading? Yi'an Jushi's poem suits me down to the ground! Allow me to embarrass myself by unpacking her allusions."
Dongfang Chenze laughed. "Always itching to prove how many volumes you've read! Fine—we'll be generous and yield the floor."
Cao Ban launched right in: "Three allusions anchor this poem. First, 'Pity Gonglu brought to such a pass'—Yuan Shu's courtesy name was Gonglu, drawn from the Book of Wu cited in the Records of the Three Kingdoms: after defeat, racked by hunger and thirst, Yuan Shu sighed, 'Has Yuan Shu truly come to this?' Second, 'Qingzhou Inspector and Brother Kong Fang'—'Qingzhou Inspector' means fine wine; A New Account of the Tales of the World records that Duke Huan had a clerk skilled at judging wine who called fine vintages 'Qingzhou Inspector' and poor ones 'Pingyuan Postmaster.' 'Brother Kong Fang' means money, from Lu Bao's Discourse on the God of Money: 'Cherished as a brother, styled Kong Fang.' Third, 'Sir No-such and Sir Fantasy' are fictional figures from Sima Xiangru's Rhapsody on Sir Fantasy."
Everyone listened in open admiration.
Qianli Xun felt blown away—like an ignorant schoolkid next to a walking encyclopedia, marveling both at Cao Ban's memory and Li Qingzhao's vast erudition. Better play it smart, hear everyone out first, then speak.
"I've embarrassed myself!" Cao Ban bowed in mock modesty.
An Mingxuan praised him: "Nobody matches Brother Cao at unpacking classical allusions!"
Seeing Miao Zhiyu still steeped in gloom despite Cao Ban's erudition, An Mingxuan offered comfort: "Don't be downcast, Lady Miao. Master Defu serves as Prefect of Laizhou—busy with official duties, he's bound to neglect Yi'an Jushi at times. In my view, reading this solely as a wife's lonely lament underestimates her scope. Beneath the surface loneliness and frustration with her husband lies a veiled jab at court officials—look at 'Qingzhou Inspector and Brother Kong Fang, bustling all day stirring trouble.' Isn't that the spitting image of officialdom's daily life?"
An Mingxuan's reading lifted much of the cloud from Miao Zhiyu's brow.
Dongfang Chenze warned with a laugh, "Watch your tongue, Brother An! Every father of the five of us holds an imperial post—let them hear that reading and you'll catch a beating! Worse, spreading such an interpretation would turn Yi'an Jushi into a target. Careful, careful!"
An Mingxuan bowed in quick humility. "Well spoken, Brother Dongfang. Just a personal fancy—strictly between the five of us. Please keep it inside these walls!"
"We debate poetry together all the time, and you don't trust us—least of all our two talented ladies?" Dongfang Chenze teased. "Penalty! Whisk a bowl of tea for Youqi Jushi!"
An Mingxuan stood and cupped his hands. "Guilty as charged! I'll whisk tea for Youqi Jushi at once." Stepping to the tea table, he set to work.
Cao Ban downed his own tea fast and chimed in, "Penalty includes a bowl for me too!"
"Relax, one bowl each! Don't complain if my whisking falls short of the teahouse master's." Focusing on his utensils, An Mingxuan kept listening closely to the discussion.
Dongfang Chenze gave his view: "The genius of Yi'an Jushi's poem lies in contrasting two worlds: 'Qingzhou Inspector and Brother Kong Fang, bustling all day stirring trouble,' set against 'Declining guests with verse behind closed doors, fragrant quiet breeds fine thoughts.' The former leaves only 'cold window, broken desk, stripped of histories and books'; the latter yields 'in stillness I gain true friends—Sir No-such and Sir Fantasy.' Placed side by side, they challenge the world to choose what life is worth living. Whatever others chase, Yi'an Jushi chooses quiet study behind closed doors—revealing her true spirit."
All three men and Miao Zhiyu had spoken; every gaze now turned toward Qianli Xun. Impressed by their insights, she scrambled to assemble her own take—how to speak without shaming Zhu Shuzhen's name?
Dongfang Chenze prompted, "What are your thoughts, Youqi Jushi? You're Yi'an Jushi's most devoted admirer."
A devoted Li Qingzhao fangirl, huh? Even more reason not to drop the ball! Heart pounding, she forced a calm, poised smile. "Every one of your insights is sharp and brilliant—Shuzhen stands in awe. You've already uncovered every subtle layer of the poem, leaving me hard-pressed to add a word..."
Flattery and modesty worked like magic; all four beamed and relaxed. Even if her commentary fell slightly short now, nobody would nitpick.
"Still, invited to a salon, I owe a few humble thoughts built upon yours."
Rising, Qianli Xun strolled over to the hanging scrolls—buying herself precious seconds to think—stood before them a beat, and turned back to face the room.
"Most poets hide the poetic core inside the verse itself; Yi'an Jushi placed hers in the preface: 'Sitting alone in one room, all I have known in life lies absent before my eyes.' That single word 'alone' holds everything—both a woman's quiet ache without her husband beside her, and the lofty solitude of Wang Wei's 'Alone I sit in dark bamboo.' [TN: Translated by Burton Watson] What is 'all I have known in life'? Past marital bliss, cherished people, finer days—all longed for, all 'absent before my eyes,' breeding sorrow and loss..."
Before she finished, a soft sigh escaped Miao Zhiyu.
Score! Qianli Xun celebrated inside. Hit her right in the heart—she's hooked!
Her nerves melted away, words flowing free.
"Grasp that core, and the lines unlock themselves. 'Cold window, broken desk, stripped of histories and books; pity Gonglu brought to such a pass' paints her surroundings—'cold' evokes autumn chill and a setting mismatched to her spirit. What does 'pity' mourn? Read the next lines introduced by 'such a pass' and it clicks: she isn't mourning material poverty, she's mourning spiritual emptiness! That echoes 'all I have known in life lies absent before my eyes,' proving the 'alone' in her preface is the solitude of a noble mind. Her poem brings to mind the Buddhist saying: 'like dew, or again like lightning—in this way should you view them.' [TN: Translated by Burton Watson] Worldly love, fame, and wealth vanish like morning dew the moment the sun rises; only spiritual pursuits and unblemished character endure."
Finished at last, Qianli Xun exhaled in quiet relief—she hadn't disgraced Zhu Shuzhen's crown—and strolled back to her seat.
Dongfang Chenze spoke first: "Brilliant critique, Youqi Jushi! I fall far short—far short!"
Cao Ban echoed, "Far short indeed!"
Lost in her own wistful thoughts, Miao Zhiyu sat silent.
An Mingxuan finished whisking and set the first bowl before Zhu Shuzhen. "This bowl carries my admiration—please drink, Youqi Jushi." Then he served the others one by one.
Everyone lifted their bowls for a single sip—when a rough shout erupted from downstairs: "Today I say she's keeping me company! Let's see what any of you can do about it!" Chaos crashed out below.
#CH 20
#TITLE: Chapter 20: Running Into the Chen Dandy at the Teahouse
Back at his seat, An Mingxuan set down his bowl. "That Chen family dandy can't go two weeks without causing a scene—odd he dragged it into Yongtai Teahouse today."
Cao Ban chimed in, "Right—he practically lives in Goulan Wasi, gambling dens, and brothels. Since when does he have the taste for a teahouse?"
Miao Zhiyu sneered in disgust. "Hmph! A dandy like that belongs locked up in the yamen jail for a spell to teach him manners."
Confused, Qianli Xun asked, "Who is this dandy you're talking about?"
All four stared at her in astonishment.
Oops—she'd just given herself away! Zhu Shuzhen clearly knew this guy.
"Don't you recognize him, Youqi Jushi?" Dongfang Chenze asked.
Qianli Xun tried to bluff: "Plenty of dandies roam the streets—how should I know which one you mean?" The words barely left her lips before she realized how hollow the excuse sounded.
Sensing danger, Kuqiao jumped in fast: "Young masters, Lady Miao—you saw the cut on Miss's forehead. That blow left her forgetting quite a few faces and events. Please forgive her."
Fresh astonishment rippled across all four faces.
Speaking for the group, Dongfang Chenze asked, "Do you still remember us, Youqi Jushi?"
"Bits and pieces—why else would I show up for the salon?"
"Good, good! Since you don't recall Young Master Chen, let me fill you in. The Chens are the richest family in Lin'an. Their son Chen Haoyu is an idle good-for-nothing who haunts brothels and gambling houses, throws his wealth around, and drags thugs along to bully folks—now bringing his mess into a teahouse."
People like that made Qianli Xun's blood boil. "Let me go teach him a lesson!"
Dongfang Chenze stopped her at once. "The Chens are your in-laws—Chen Haoyu's older sister is your sister-in-law! Cross him and he'll tattle to your brother and sister-in-law. Worse, he and his cronies know rough brawling—a delicate lady like you would only get hurt!"
Right then the teahouse waiter rushed in, wringing his hands. "Young Master Dongfang, Young Master Cao, Young Master An—you're respected gentlemen of Lin'an, please save our teahouse! Young Master Chen took a fancy to our female tea performer, tried forcing himself on her, and when she resisted, started smashing the place downstairs!"
Before the three men could open their mouths, Qianli Xun shot to her feet. "I'm going!"
The three scholars and Miao Zhiyu had no choice but to trail out of the booth and peer down over the second-floor railing.
Every customer downstairs had fled, leaving utter wreckage behind. Flower arrangements lay smashed across the floor; wall scrolls hung ripped or trampled under thugs' boots; the tea table lay overturned, tea powder and utensils scattered everywhere.
Seated on a chair, Chen Haoyu had pinned a female tea performer across his lap, gripping her tight.
Qianli Xun sized him up—handsome enough on the outside, rotten to the core inside.
Chen Haoyu leered at the struggling woman. "The whole teahouse is paying for your stubbornness! Give in already—spend one night with me and I'll treat you right."
"In your dreams!" the tea performer spat. "Everyone knows girls you toy with end up sold to brothels!"
"Keep smashing!" Chen Haoyu barked. Seven or eight cronies resumed wrecking tables and chairs.
Unable to stomach another second, Qianli Xun shouted from above: "Let her go!"
Every head snapped upstairs.
Chen Haoyu broke into a mocking grin. "Well, well—who do we have here? My dear in-law! What was your name again... oh right, Zhu—Zhu Shuzhen! Go back upstairs and write your little poems, don't meddle! Out of respect for our families being in-laws, I'll let you off."
"Too bad I'm not letting you off!" Qianli Xun snapped back.
Chen Haoyu laughed cold. "And how do you plan to take me on? A poetry duel? Spare me—I have zero use for that junk! Only those three softies beside you treat verse like treasure because they can't do anything else—affecting literary airs and reciting lyrics like a bunch of sissies!"
Stung, Cao Ban cursed back: "Chen Haoyu! Preying on respectable women every day—you're lower than pigs and dogs!"
Chen Haoyu smirked. "Calls himself a famed scholar of Lin'an and talks of pigs and dogs—not very refined! Mind your precious reputation!"
An Mingxuan stepped forward. "Chen Haoyu, this is a teahouse, not your usual brothel haunt! This tea performer is a skilled artisan, not a courtesan. Release her now, or I report this to the yamen and watch you pay!"
"Hmph! Your dad's just an Intendant—who gave you leave to bark at me?" Chen Haoyu sneered. "Go ahead and report me! Think I'm scared?"
Dongfang Chenze spoke cool and steady: "Not scared of the local yamen—what about His Majesty?"
"His Majesty sits way off in Bianliang—think he has time to care if I fancy a girl or two? Don't make me laugh, Dongfang Chenze!" Chen Haoyu leaned his face toward the tea performer.
She twisted away in revulsion.
"You just sneered that men who compose poetry aren't real men," Dongfang Chenze pressed on. "That insults His Majesty himself—our sovereign excels at calligraphy, poetry, and lyrics alike! One letter from me to my friend in Bianliang, passed to Prince Yi and straight to His Majesty, and your entire Chen clan pays for your loose tongue!"
"Talking about Qiao Wenxian? His nephew Prince Yi is a sickly prince who has zero pull before His Majesty! Think I don't know? Quit trying to bluff me!" Chen Haoyu swaggered even harder.
Done listening, Qianli Xun snapped: "Chen Haoyu! Eat my fist!" Using light-foot skill, she vaulted clean over the second-floor railing, landed below, and drove a punch square into Chen Haoyu's face!
Yelping in pain, he loosened his grip; the tea performer tore free and bolted. Clutching half his face, Chen Haoyu stared. "Whoa! Since when can you hit like that?!"
Upstairs, every friend who knew Zhu Shuzhen stood with jaws on the floor—was that really Youqi Jushi?!
"Get her!" Chen Haoyu barked at his thugs. "Beat her flat right now!"
Seven or eight toughs closed in around Qianli Xun. Calm and sharp, she tracked every stance. The instant fists flew from every angle, she slipped outside their ring with agile footwork, dropped one man flat, and before the rest could pivot, unleashed a blur of rapid punches and kicks that put every last thug on the floor groaning, "Ow! It hurts!"
Chen Haoyu jumped in himself—two moves later, Qianli Xun twisted his arm behind his back. "Catch you bullying anyone again, and I beat you every single time!"
Still talking tough, Chen Haoyu hissed, "Dare touch me? Watch me tell Zhu Jingyuan and my sister to deal with you!"
"Hmph! My brother and sister-in-law are fair people—they'll probably help me thrash you!" She wrenched his arm higher.
Agony shot up his shoulder. "Ow! Mercy, Granny, mercy!"
"Easy! First, apologize to the tea performer. Second, pay for the wrecked teahouse. Third, never set foot in Yongtai Teahouse again!"
Outmatched by her iron grip and knowing better than to fight a losing battle, Chen Haoyu folded. "Fine, fine—I agree!"
Letting go, Qianli Xun shoved him straight toward the tea performer. "Apologize now—or my fist talks next!"
Cowed, Chen Haoyu muttered, "I'm sorry. I won't harass you again."
Disgusted, the tea performer turned her head away without a glance.
Pulling a pouch of silver from his belt, Chen Haoyu tossed it onto an upright table. "See that, Zhu Shuzhen? Payment for the teahouse right there." He turned to leave.
"Hold it!" Qianli Xun halted him. "Haven't checked if it's enough yet—stay put till they count!"
A waiter opened the pouch and did a quick count. "Enough—more than enough!"
Chen Haoyu forced a cocky laugh. "The Chens are the richest clan in Lin'an—silver's the one thing we're never short on! Doubting my word is an insult. Seeing how tough your fists are, I'll let it slide."
Glaring at his bruised thugs, he barked, "What are you lying around for? Move! Never coming back to this dump anyway!"
At the doorway he looked back over his shoulder. "Watch yourself, Zhu Shuzhen—don't ever fall into my hands!" With that, he swaggered off.
The tea performer, shopkeeper, and waiters crowded around pouring out gratitude.
Upstairs, the three scholars and Miao Zhiyu came down the steps, staring at Zhu Shuzhen as if meeting a stranger.
Miao Zhiyu asked first, "Youqi Jushi, when on earth did you learn martial arts? Never saw anything like that before! If you're this skilled, why did you always come to salons covered in bruises—is the Training Officer even stronger?"
At a loss for an excuse, Qianli Xun kept silent behind an awkward grin.
Dongfang Chenze stepped in with a smile. "Youqi Jushi hides her depths well—gifted in verse and deadly in combat, putting us men to shame! No wonder Brother Qiao..."
His trailing words puzzled Qianli Xun, yet Miao Zhiyu caught fire at once: "What about Young Master Qiao?"
Choosing discretion, Dongfang Chenze waved it off. "Nothing. Today's salon was thrilling enough, and the teahouse needs time to tidy up—let's call it a day."
Leading their maids and pages out of the teahouse, they strolled a bit longer along scenic West Lake before parting ways outside the lakeside path.
#CH 21
#TITLE: Chapter 21: Questioned After the Poetry Salon
Riding home, Qianli Xun felt pleased with how today went. Zhu Shuzhen's four friends saw differences, sure, yet none suspected she wasn't the real Zhu Shuzhen—credit to her Li Qingzhao critique living up to the poet's fame. A satisfied smile tugged at her lips.
Noticing her bright mood, Kuqiao remarked, "You look real happy, Miss."
"Course I am!" Curious how the real Zhu Shuzhen behaved among peers, Qianli Xun asked, "Kuqiao, did I talk much around those three gentlemen and Lady Miao before? Or did I play the ice queen there too?"
Kuqiao chuckled. "You only acted frosty around folks who didn't understand poetry—and stayed quiet around family. Around Young Master Dongfang and the others, you beamed and talked nonstop!"
"Aha—so that's how I was! No wonder they didn't bat an eye."
"Didn't bat an eye? When you jumped off the second floor and beat up Young Master Chen, their eyes popped out of their skulls! Lucky they just figured you were hiding your talents."
"Meeting friends today taught me one thing: I am who I am. Done bending over backward pretending to be the old Zhu Shuzhen—from now on I show the world the real me!"
She made up her mind: stop squeezing herself into Zhu Shuzhen's mold, and let Zhu Shuzhen become Qianli Xun instead.
Pulling up outside Lin Manor, Qianli Xun remembered the Corrosive Paste—time to apply it back at Zhulan Residence.
The moment she stepped into the West Courtyard, Lin Jue blocked her path.
"Where have you been, Wife?"
The bruise around his left eye had vanished clean. Qianli Xun smiled inside: That Jixiang Pharmacy salve really delivers!
"Why aren't you answering?" Lin Jue pressed.
"Went to a poetry salon to catch up with friends," she replied breezy as air.
"Hmph! Probably using 'poetry salon' as a cover to carry on illicit affairs with those fancy scholars!" Bitter resentment edged his voice.
"Lin Jue! Nothing decent ever comes out of your filthy mouth—your head is full of gutter trash! Not wasting breath on you!" Fuming, she sidestepped toward Zhulan Residence.
Lin Jue darted ahead to bar her way. "I already warned you to stay away from poetry salons—why didn't you listen?"
Exasperation flared. "Lin Jue, you're a grown man—quit nagging like an old busybody! We're getting divorced anyway, why do you care? Even if we weren't, you don't own me—I go where I please!"
Stung in his pride, Lin Jue swung a hand to strike her.
Qianli Xun caught his blow cold with one fist. "What, trying domestic violence again? Didn't even weigh yourself before swinging at me?"
Stopped dead by her force, knowing full well he stood no chance against this reborn wife, Lin Jue lowered his hand and softened his tone.
"Once during a drill break, I overheard my soldiers whispering about you—saying you and Dongfang Chenze had something going on, that you and Qiao Wenxian exchanged flirtatious glances, saying all kinds of ugly things! I'm your husband, a man—how could I not lose my temper hearing folks talk like that? After I warned you off Dongfang Chenze's salons, you stayed home once, and now you go right back! Trying to make me the laughingstock of all Lin'an?"
Seeing him cool down, Qianli Xun let her anger ebb too—no point souring things right before tomorrow's divorce. Speaking earnest and plain, she said, "Your biggest problem is having zero basic trust in your own wife. When you remarry, drop that habit. Just to clear my old name, next time they invite me I'll bring you along so you see with your own eyes what we actually do. Granted, by then we'll be divorced—no big deal, we can still be friends, and I'll bring you along as a friend."
Lin Jue stood stunned. Whenever this came up before, Zhu Shuzhen never offered one word of explanation, never invited him along—down deep she despised him as an uncultured brute who knew nothing of verse or romance. Now she offered to take him herself! Had she acted like this from day one, might everything have turned out different?
"Who wants to sit through that boring stuff? Qiao Wenxian and his crowd are pompous snobs—we'd never mix."
Sizing him up from head to toe, Qianli Xun remarked, "First time I saw you, I noticed you've got solid bones for martial arts. Tell you what—after we divorce, become my apprentice and I'll teach you Qianli Fist!"
That froze Lin Jue colder still—clinching his dread that the old Zhu Shuzhen really had died by his hand. A chill crawled up his spine; taking two steps back, he spun around and stalked off with heavy strides.
Watching him leave, Qianli Xun blinked. "Hey! Where you going? Don't want to be my apprentice?"
Without answering, Lin Jue cleared the yard and vanished.
"Weird guy," Qianli Xun muttered.
"You're the weird one, Miss," Kuqiao piped up. "Offering to take Son-in-law as your apprentice—even my jaw dropped, let alone his!"
One name had echoed all day: Qiao Wenxian. What exactly was his tie to Zhu Shuzhen? Time to find out.
"Back to Zhulan Residence, Kuqiao—got questions for you."
Kuqiao trailed obedient behind her.
Inside, after changing into lounge clothes, Qianli Xun asked straight out, "Everyone keeps mentioning Qiao Wenxian. Who is he?"
Folding the outer robes neatly, Kuqiao answered, "Young Master of the Qiao family—born to Master Qiao's concubine, yet as the sole male heir slated to carry on the Qiao estate, he was raised by Madam Qiao. He has four older sisters; the eldest entered the palace and became Consort Qiao—pity her fate was short, she passed away right after giving birth to a son."
"How do you know all that?" Qianli Xun wondered how a maid had the full scoop.
"Nothing secret about Qiao family business in Lin'an—everyone knows because they produced Consort Qiao, so folks talk about them nonstop. I heard it chatting with other maids back at Zhu Manor."
"Does Young Master Dongfang invite Qiao Wenxian to salons often?"
"All the time! Though he never writes poems himself—just loves critiquing yours, and nobody reads your verse deeper than him, so you considered him a true confidant."
"Confidant? Did I love him?"
"Back then your heart belonged to Young Master Xu—nobody else could fit."
Qianli Xun sighed. "Blame Mother and Father for hiding the truth and letting you pine for nothing! Put Young Master Xu in front of me today, I wouldn't take him for free! Wonder what your old self saw in him."
"Young Master Xu wasn't bad, really—handsome, brilliant, spoke your language, plus childhood sweethearts. Only flaw was obeying his parents blindly—filial to them, cruel to you."
"Enough about him—ancient history! Time to focus on tomorrow's divorce. Hand me the Corrosive Paste."
Fetching the vial, Kuqiao frowned. "Still can't fathom why you're hurting yourself on purpose, Miss."
Taking a seat at the bronze mirror, Qianli Xun tipped salve onto her healing cut. "You'll see tomorrow. Now go fetch the bruise ointment back from Lin Jue."
"Yes, Miss."
Outside Lin Jue's quarters, Kuqiao called in politely: "Son-in-law, Miss sent me to retrieve the bruise medicine."
Moments later a servant stepped out. "Master says come inside."
Stepping in, Kuqiao saw Lin Jue sitting at the table reading a book—Wait, Son-in-law reading?!
Spotting her, Lin Jue flipped the book facedown. Growing up beside Zhu Shuzhen, Kuqiao recognized characters well enough to read "Collected Poems of Wang Youcheng" on the cover—her mistress's beloved Tang poet! Never dreamed Son-in-law would crack it open!
"Vial's on the side table. Grab it."
Retrieving the bottle, Kuqiao bowed to leave, only for Lin Jue to halt her: "Hold on—a question."
She paused. "Yes, Son-in-law?"
"With your Miss acting nothing like before, doesn't the Zhu family resent me?"
Kuqiao thought, The nerve of him asking! Watch me jab back: "Of course they resent you! Only tales of rebirth sound too wild for folks to believe, and since Miss still wears the exact same face despite changing inside, even if they dragged you to the yamen for beating her to death, there's zero proof—so the Zhus have to swallow it in silence."
Unease pricked Lin Jue—his father-in-law was Prefect of Lin'an, after all. Lucky he had moved first, visiting Zhu Xiyan ahead of Shuzhen to kneel and weep out his side: claiming he only hit her because she flirted with Lin'an scholars and dragged his name through barracks gossip, and only saw courtesans because she refused his bed. Convinced by the tears and excuses, Prefect Zhu hadn't blamed him.
"Do you like your Miss better now, or how she was before?"
Refusing to hand him an inch, Kuqiao snapped, "Past or present, she's my Miss—I love her either way."
"Well, I like her better now. Knows martial arts like one of my own kind, and actually talks to me instead of freezing me out. Does she still care for those fancy word games?"
Kuqiao answered with sharp relish: "Hate to disappoint you, Son-in-law—Miss's poetic gift hasn't faded a bit! At today's salon, her reading of Yi'an Jushi's poem won raves from everyone!"
Disappointment crossed Lin Jue's face as he glanced down at the poetry book on his desk; staring at it half the night hadn't made heads or tails of it or sparked an ounce of interest. "Don't see the point of that stuff—can't eat it, can't drink it. Go on, and tell your Miss: mutual divorce won't come easy."
Stepping outside, Kuqiao muttered back toward the closed door: "My Miss is tough now—nobody pushes her around!"
Back at Zhulan Residence, she relayed every word to Qianli Xun.
Qianli Xun zeroed in on one line—"mutual divorce won't come easy." She had done everything she could; tomorrow was up to Heaven.
#CH 22
#TITLE: Chapter 22: The Elders Gather to Discuss Mutual Divorce
All night long, the wound on Qianli Xun's forehead burned like fire—Corrosive Paste packed a real punch. Just to catch some sleep, she had to dab on a bit of the healing salve before dozing off.
First thing in the morning, checking the mirror showed the cut much calmer—so she dabbed fresh Corrosive Paste right back on! After breakfast she picked out a fine dress, tidied up, and set off with Kuqiao toward the North Courtyard.
Halfway there she halted again, lost as ever; Kuqiao stepped ahead to guide.
Lin Manor paled beside Zhu Manor's landscaped beauty—barely designed at all, with dirt paths worn down purely by servants' feet, bound to turn to mud in the first rain.
Where the lane widened into neat stone paving, rows of buildings rose ahead—the North Courtyard.
Near the gate, a servant hurried up. "Young Madam, the Lin family has all arrived and awaits inside."
Detesting the title "Young Madam," Qianli Xun brushed right past him into the yard, heading straight toward the reception hall where figures already sat visible inside.
Stepping across the courtyard flagstones, excitement and nerves churned in her gut—excitement at winning freedom from a meaningless marriage today, nerves over what she'd do if it fell through.
Crossing the threshold, she took in the spacious hall. A painting of clustered peonies dominated the facing wall above a narrow long table holding porcelain vases and an empty incense burner kept purely for show.
Before it stood a square table flanked by seats of honor. On the left sat an elder stroking his beard with refined poise.
Shifting her gaze to the left side-seats, she counted two rows of four—eight seats in all, with Lin Jue sitting front row closest to the head seat.
Spotting how much worse Zhu Shuzhen's forehead cut looked than yesterday, Lin Jue knew at once she had tampered with it. Willing to hurt herself just to get away from him—a bitter twist tightened his chest.
Qianli Xun looked to the right side: the Old Ancestor hadn't arrived yet. Four chairs—three empty, one occupied by a middle-aged woman. Thinking some sympathetic Zhu elder had shown up on her own, Qianli Xun hurried over with a grateful bow. "Thank you for coming to support me, Elder! How should I address you?"
The woman chuckled. "Don't recognize me, Young Madam? I'm the matchmaker who arranged your marriage to the Training Officer—Matchmaker Yan! All the Zhu seats were empty, so I borrowed one."
Speechless, Qianli Xun sank awkwardly into an empty chair as sharp stings pulsed across her forehead.
"Youqi Jushi, what happened to your forehead?"
Someone among the Lin elders called her Youqi Jushi—must be a poetry lover!
Dabbing around the cut with her silk handkerchief and putting on a fragile, pained look, she turned toward the head seat. "Elder, this wound is Lin Jue's handiwork! You don't know—he beats me all the time, and this blow wiped my memory clean. Forgive me for not knowing your name."
The elder flared up at once: "Nephew Lin Jue! Marrying Youqi Jushi took blessings built across lifetimes, and instead of cherishing her you raise your hand and beat the memory out of her? No wonder she wants a mutual divorce!"
"Second Uncle, you don't know the whole story—don't listen only to her side!" Lin Jue protested fast.
This Second Uncle was Lin Zixiao, frequent guest at Zhu Xiyan's poetry banquets and ardent admirer of Zhu Shuzhen's talent—to him, his nephew landing such a poetess was sheer fortune.
Dead set against letting the marriage crumble, he barked, "Lin Jue! Kneel right now before your clan uncles and two brothers, admit your fault to Youqi Jushi, beg her forgiveness, and swear never to lay a finger on her again!"
Neither willing to divorce nor willing to kneel in public—he felt blame ran both ways—Lin Jue rose instead.
"Calm your anger, Second Uncle—I'll admit my faults when the time is right, not now. Look around: every Lin elder turned out today, yet not a single soul from the Zhu family showed up! Uncles, eldest brother, second brother—what does that tell you?"
One man spoke up: "Does the Prefect oppose the divorce?"
"Hit the nail on the head, Eldest Brother—that's it!" Lin Jue confirmed.
A clan uncle chimed in: "Nephew's wife, since your own father disagrees and no Zhu elder came, quit making a scene and head back to the West Courtyard to live in peace."
Refusing to get steamrolled, Qianli Xun braced through the burn of Corrosive Paste. "My parents aren't coming—doesn't mean other Zhu elders aren't! It's not even the appointed hour yet—what's the rush? Can't you wait?"
"Let's grant Youqi Jushi some time and wait in patience," Lin Zixiao agreed.
The other elders nodded. Lin Jue sank back into his seat, grumbling, "If nobody shows by the Hour of the Snake, the divorce is dead—and you never bring it up again."
Ignoring him, Qianli Xun played up her wound—she hadn't paid three taels and endured this stinging for nothing.
"Elders, look what Lin Jue did to me! Stay with him and I'll lose my life! I have to divorce him—please back me up!"
Another elder spoke up: "Nephew's wife, sure Lin Jue shouldn't hit you—yet when a man strikes his wife, the wife shares blame too. Look inside yourself and ask what you did wrong."
Fury exploded in Qianli Xun; she shot to her feet ready to rip into him—when a clear voice floated in from outside.
"Nonsense! Whatever a woman's flaws, the instant a man raises a hand to hit a woman, the fault is his! Doubly so when a daughter of our Zhu house is educated and refined—what fault could she possibly have?"
Every ounce of anger melted into pure relief—nothing beat having heavy backup! Every head turned toward the doorway as the Old Ancestor strolled in, rolling prayer beads, Matron Xiuzhen walking behind without even needing to steady her—a hundred and twenty years old and striding firm as iron!
Qianli Xun rushed forward. "Thank you for coming on my behalf, Old Ancestor!"
Hearing "Old Ancestor," every Lin in the room scrambled to their feet.
Lin Zixiao led the bow: "Greetings to the Old Ancestor! Beholding your esteemed presence today honors all of us juniors!"
Matchmaker Yan jumped up too; everybody in Lin'an knew of the Zhu clan's century-old matriarch, and meeting her face to face commanded deep awe.
Stepping into the hall, the Old Ancestor swept a cool gaze over the Lin men. "Ganging up on my granddaughter? I'm here representing the Zhu family to back her—and demand answers."
Rising with the rest yet scoffing inside, Lin Jue pushed back: "Crowds of Lin elders came today, while the Zhu house sent only you—proof enough Father-in-law opposes this divorce! Since you're here, Old Ancestor, please help persuade my wife to drop this divorce talk."
The Old Ancestor fixed her eyes on him for a beat. "You're my granddaughter's husband?"
"I am."
Turning to her granddaughter—whose cut looked far nastier than when they last met—then back to Lin Jue, the Old Ancestor said cool and crisp, "You batter my granddaughter and haul brothel courtesans into the house, and you expect the Zhu family to let her stay? True, I came alone—do I not outweigh every elder in the Zhu house?"
"You do, of course you do!" Lin Zixiao hastened to smooth things over. "Pay no mind to the lad's rudeness, Old Ancestor—please take the seat of honor!"
The other Lin elders echoed their agreement.
Glowing with pride at having real backing, Qianli Xun guided the Old Ancestor to the right-hand seat of honor, then resumed her own chair ready to let the matriarch work.
Once the Old Ancestor sat right, Lin Zixiao dared not stay seated beside her on the left and signaled a servant to fetch him a side chair.
"You hold standing among the Lin elders—sit beside me," the Old Ancestor instructed.
Lin Zixiao bowed humbly. "Since the Old Ancestor commands, I obey—never dreamed you even knew of me."
"I don't, yet these eyes know how to read people. Everyone sit—let's discuss these two children's mutual divorce calmly."
At her word, the entire hall sat.
Lin Zixiao opened: "Now that the Old Ancestor is here, allow me to state the consensus of our Lin clan and Nephew Lin Jue: marrying Youqi Jushi into our home is our ancestors' greatest blessing. None of us approve a divorce—we hope the young couple stays together."
Turning to her granddaughter, the Old Ancestor asked, "Where do you stand? State it plain before the elders."
Even having made herself clear already, Qianli Xun seized the floor.
"Old Ancestor, esteemed elders—though amnesia erased how Lin Jue treated me before, the pain lingers! Waking up after he knocked me cold, the very first thing I saw was him cozying up to a brothel courtesan! How could any woman swallow that humiliation? I demanded a bill of divorce; maybe conscience pricked him and he suggested mutual divorce instead, bringing us here today. If I didn't even fear repudiation, why would I hesitate over mutual divorce?"
Eyelids lowered, rolling her beads, the Old Ancestor listened to every word before speaking: "Folks say tear down ten temples sooner than break one marriage—yet my Zhu granddaughter suffers so bitterly, how can we leave her trapped in a fiery pit? Today I intend to break this match for her. I hope the Lin clan steps aside and lets these two part in peace."
Before any elder could answer, Lin Jue jumped in: "Old Ancestor, you don't know the truth! Had she consummated our marriage on our wedding night, I never would've sought courtesans! Had she not treated me like air and carried on with young scholars outside, I never would've hit her! Tell you what—right here before you, my clan uncles, and both my brothers, I swear: if Shuzhen stays, I'll never strike her again! Let me take one concubine and I'll never touch another courtesan, and she can attend all the poetry salons she likes—long as she remembers she's married and doesn't flirt with scholars!"
Heads nodded all down the Lin row.
Lin Zixiao chimed in: "Old Ancestor, you can see how much Lin Jue hates to lose Youqi Jushi. Since he vows to mend his ways, why not grant him a chance? Besides, mutual divorce still smudges a woman's name—I would hate to see any blemish touch the Poetess of Jiangnan."
Qianli Xun had to hand it to Lin Jue—brute or not, the guy knew how to play a room! Clearly he had rehearsed this speech to kill her divorce cold. No way was she letting him win! And since Corrosive Paste wasn't moving the needle, why keep suffering?
"Kuqiao—put the healing salve on my cut."
Kuqiao pulled out the vial and dabbed cooling ointment over the wound; the burn faded at once, clearing her head.
Rising with a bow, Qianli Xun spoke loud and clear: "Old Ancestor, elders—nothing can shake my resolve to divorce! Lin Jue and I share neither mind nor taste; living together tastes like chewing wax. No matter how many vows he makes, my mind is set. Calling my poetry salons 'flirting with scholars' is pure slander—proof he neither trusts nor understands me! What point is there staying married to a man like that?"
The Old Ancestor's hand stopped on her beads. "Xiuzhen, bring it out and show Son-in-law. If he solves the riddle inside, I will talk my granddaughter out of divorcing. If he cannot, grant my granddaughter her freedom."
Matron Xiuzhen drew a folded sheet from her sleeve and opened it with care—Qianli Xun's handwritten "The Heartbreak Riddle."
Stepping before Lin Jue, she handed it over. "Examine this, Son-in-law, and announce the riddle's answer before everyone."
Lin Jue took the sheet. He knew every character and could read every line—yet as for any riddle, he hadn't the foggiest clue!
#CH 23
#TITLE: Chapter 23: A Riddle Lyric, Mutual Divorce, and the Seven Grounds
Lin Jue read "The Heartbreak Riddle" silently ten times over—grasping neither its depth nor its hidden answer, tangled up in a knot of words.
Watching sweat bead on his forehead, Qianli Xun almost laughed out loud—what a rotten deal Zhu Shuzhen had gotten marrying this guy!
"If you can't make sense of my lyric, Lin Jue, stop torturing yourself—let me leave the Lin family in peace, and I'll owe you one."
Lin Jue flung the sheet to the floor—right beside Lin Zixiao's feet, who scooped it up and began reading with delight.
"Hmph!" Lin Jue snapped. "Everyone knows I despise fancy word games! Did the Old Ancestor pick this on purpose knowing I couldn't solve it just to force me into divorce? Hardly fair!"
Matron Xiuzhen rebuked him sharply: "Watch your insolence, young man! The Old Ancestor stays shut in the Zhu Buddhist Shrine year round and knew nothing about you until Miss Zhu came begging with nowhere else to turn..."
Raising a hand to check Xiuzhen, the Old Ancestor resumed rolling her beads. "At first I refused to step in. Her lyric moved me—showing both her talent and that her wish to end this marriage is no passing whim, but a weighed decision. Since you cannot solve her riddle, you two are clearly mismatched. Let go so both of you can find peace."
Before Lin Jue or any elder could reply, Lin Zixiao burst out: "Brilliant! Utterly brilliant lyric, Youqi Jushi!"
Fired up to analyze verse, he sprang to his feet: "Allow me to unpack this for everyone!"
Someone groaned, "Second Brother, Nephew Lin Jue's marriage is falling apart and you're lecturing on poetry?! Help save his home!"
Curious what on earth Zhu Shuzhen had written to summon the Old Ancestor and dazzle his uncle, Lin Jue waved a hand. "Let Second Uncle explain—I don't believe some fancy word game can wreck my marriage!"
Setting the divorce aside, Lin Zixiao beamed and launched in: "Titled 'The Heartbreak Riddle,' this lyric not only conveys heartbreak—it conceals ten number riddles!"
Ten number riddles? Where? Lin Jue racked his brain over the lines and blurted out, "Tell us the answers first, Second Uncle!"
"Listen close," Lin Zixiao declared. "'Coming downstairs, golden coins drop for divination'—drop the 'bu' (卜) from 'xia' (下), leaving 'one' (一); 'I ask High Heaven, where has he gone?'—remove 'ren' (人) from 'tian' (天), leaving 'two' (二); 'Resenting the noble lord who walked straight away'—remove the vertical stroke from 'wang' (王), leaving 'three' (三); 'Cursing the heartless one—speaking of parting, hard to hold back'—remove 'yan' (言) from 'li' (詈), leaving 'four' (四); 'Regretting how I first misspoke'—remove 'kou' (口) from 'wu' (吾), leaving 'five' (五); 'There is upper union, no lower tie'—keep the top of 'jiao' (交) and drop the bottom, leaving 'six' (六); 'Why ask right from wrong?'—drop 'bai' (白) from 'zao' (皂), leaving 'seven' (七); 'Parting needs no knife'—drop the knife radical (刀) from 'fen' (分), leaving 'eight' (八); 'From this day on lean no more on a foe'—drop the person radical (亻) from 'chou' (仇), leaving 'nine' (九); 'A thousand miles of longing wiped away with one stroke'—drop the top flick from 'qian' (千), leaving 'ten' (十)! Tell me, everyone—isn't that sheer genius?!"
Understanding dawned on Lin Jue—he really didn't know his wife at all—yet the deeper heartbreak still sailed over his head. He sneered, "So what? Just ten numbers—big deal!"
Around the hall, half the Lin elders marveled while the rest shrugged alongside Lin Jue.
"You're wrong, Nephew," Lin Zixiao countered. "Composing an entire riddle lyric requires selecting exact characters, fitting strict rhyme schemes, weaving every glyph seamlessly into natural phrases, and never drifting from the emotional core! Writing verse is like raising a tower brick by brick—no easier than drilling armies! Youqi Jushi's wit and craft leave this old man in awe. Alas, every line brims with resentment and finality—above all 'from this day on lean no more on a foe.' She already regards you as an enemy, Lin Jue! If I still block this divorce, your future together will be poison. Give Youqi Jushi her freedom!"
Lin Jue slumped in his chair, gloom darkening his face. He understood every word—yet couldn't bear letting her walk out into another man's arms! Dongfang Chenze, Qiao Wenxian—those vultures had eyed his wife for ages; no way was he handing her over!
Thrilled that one poem had flipped Lin Zixiao so fast, Qianli Xun rose and bowed. "Spot-on reading, Second Uncle! Thank you for understanding and granting your blessing."
"Don't mention it, Youqi Jushi. Might I keep this copy to study more at home?"
"Yours to keep, Second Uncle!"
Folding the sheet carefully into his sleeve, Lin Zixiao bowed to the Old Ancestor. "As a Lin elder who now consents to the divorce, it's improper for me to stay. I take my leave."
The Old Ancestor gave a slight nod.
Turning to his kin, Lin Zixiao announced, "Brothers, nephews—I'm off. Carry on."
A clan brother sprang up. "Second Brother, you leave—who presides?!"
"Third Brother's right here—let him preside!" Flicking his sleeve, Lin Zixiao walked out.
The Old Ancestor spoke up: "Son-in-law, I said earlier—fail the riddle, grant my granddaughter her freedom. You failed. Sign the mutual divorce papers."
Qianli Xun looked at Lin Jue full of hope.
Wrinkling his brow in a glum look, Lin Jue pushed back: "Forcing me to divorce over one poem makes zero sense! Out of respect for your age I won't argue with you, Old Ancestor—yet unless you convince every elder here and both my brothers to walk out like Second Uncle, I'm not signing a thing!"
Without a word, the Old Ancestor closed her eyes and rolled her beads.
"Since I'm presiding, I'll take the main seat!" Third Uncle Lin rose from beside Lin Jue, bowed to the Old Ancestor, and settled into the head chair.
Qianli Xun eyed Third Uncle: plump frame, beard, eyes squinted to slits, features bunched close together—looked downright comical. Holding her tongue, she waited to hear his pitch.
Clearing his throat twice, Third Uncle began: "Second Brother caves easy because he loves fancy word games. Not me! Write a hundred lyrics, means zilch to me. Give it a rest, nephew's wife—you're not young anymore, who'd take a divorced woman? Quit dreaming of poems and songs—verse won't put rice on the table! Marriage is one thing, poetry's another. Whoever you marry, come nightfall you blow out the lamp, pull up the quilt, and it's all the same under the covers..."
Finding the remark crude, the Old Ancestor gave a warning cough.
Matron Xiuzhen snapped, "Third Uncle Lin, mind your language!"
Checking himself at once, Third Uncle pivoted: "As the saying goes, a woman's virtue is having no talent! Too much cleverness fills a woman's head with lofty demands and wrecks a home. Lin Jue is Lin'an's Training Officer guarding the whole city—plenty of girls would kill to marry him, yet he picked you out of true devotion! Stop pining for those Lin'an scholars—all flash and no steel, pretty to look at and useless in a fight! Not like my nephew Lin Jue, martial-trained, strong as an ox, come nightfall..."
There went his mouth slipping back under the covers again! Qianli Xun bit back a laugh—what a circus this Lin clan was.
Another cough from the Old Ancestor cut him short, forcing a wrap-up: "Point is, Third Uncle wants what's best for you. Stay together and make it work—harmony brings prosperity. Done!"
Third Uncle's speech swept away every bit of Lin Jue's gloom, puffing his confidence right back up. Lose one Second Uncle? Plenty more clan uncles stood behind him—the real show was just starting!
Done staying quiet, Qianli Xun spoke up: "Very practical advice, Third Uncle—appreciated! Yet my resolve to leave Lin Jue is ironclad; no lecture from the Lin clan will change my mind. If you elders refuse mutual divorce, then let Lin Jue write a bill of repudiation right here! I don't fear a ruined name, and I don't fear being barred from my maiden door!"
Shock rippled across the room. Rumors of her asking to be cast out were one thing—hearing a noble lady demand repudiation to their faces sounded unheard of! Only the Old Ancestor rolled her beads undisturbed.
Matchmaker Yan sprang up at once. "Young Madam, never talk of being cast out! Besides, you haven't violated any of the Seven Conditions for Repudiating a Wife—the Lin family has no grounds to cast you out!"
"Seven Conditions?" First time Qianli Xun had heard that phrase. "What are the Seven Conditions?"
Matchmaker Yan ticked them off: "Disobedience to parents-in-law, failure to bear a son, adultery, jealousy, severe illness, gossip, and theft! The Training Officer's parents passed away early, so disobedience doesn't apply. You've been wed less than a year and are young—bearing children is easy, so no failure there. Adultery and jealousy certainly don't fit. You're clearly healthy—no severe illness. With your poise and refinement, gossip is unthinkable—and theft is absurd! You don't fit a single ground; how could he even write a bill of repudiation?"
Finished, Matchmaker Yan sat back down.
Blocked cold by the Seven Conditions, even repudiation hit a wall! Qianli Xun felt boxed in.
Delighted at her stumped face, Lin Jue gloated inside: Hmph! Walking out on me won't be that easy!
Another Lin spoke up: "Sister-in-law, you only want to leave Third Brother because he hit you and saw courtesans. Stay with him, and Second Brother swears right here: if he hits you, come to me and I'll break his legs! If he visits courtesans, I'll haul him out of the brothel and thrash him in public! If he dares bring one home, I'll make him kneel outside Lin Manor's main gate for three days and nights! Fair enough?"
Living under the same roof—different courtyards or not—Qianli Xun didn't buy for one second his brothers knew nothing before. Now they suddenly play tough? Worthless promises! Only one thing mattered: getting divorced—even if she had to claim one of those Seven Conditions herself!
Going all-in, ignoring Second Brother, she dropped a bombshell: "Seven Conditions, right? One fits! Didn't Lin Jue accuse me of carrying on with Lin'an scholars outside? Doesn't that fit 'adultery'?!"
Eyes bulged all down the Lin row; Lin Jue's jaw dropped—this reborn wife stopped at nothing! Even the Old Ancestor's hand froze on her beads while Matron Xiuzhen wrung her hands in stunned silence. Matchmaker Yan stared as if seeing a stranger, and cold sweat drenched Kuqiao's brow: Reborn Miss is fearless—people run a mile from anything touching a woman's honor, and she's volunteering for it!
#CH 24
#TITLE: Chapter 24: Mutual Divorce Fails, Bold Words Spoken
Judging by every horrified face in the room, Qianli Xun knew she had swung way too hard—she just wanted out of Lin Jue's clutches so bad! She hadn't even tasted romance once in thirty years—why spend her transmigration chained to a man she couldn't stand?
She had burned her own forehead, argued herself hoarse, brought out the Old Ancestor and "The Heartbreak Riddle," even begged for repudiation—nothing budged them. Fight to survive from the brink of ruin: if trashing her reputation bought freedom, so be it!
Matchmaker Yan jumped in first: "Young Madam, how can you slander yourself?! A woman's good name is everything! Even if the Training Officer made accusations, that's just idle guesswork and gossip—nobody caught you in bed with anyone! Not a single elder here takes his words as truth. Using that as grounds to cast you out—I, Matchmaker Yan, will be the first to block it!"
Qianli Xun knew the matchmaker meant well trying to protect her honor—yet she couldn't feel an ounce of thanks when the woman had just slammed her last exit shut.
Resuming her beads, the Old Ancestor said, "My Zhu granddaughter has gone so far as to throw away her own reputation and claim 'adultery' just to break free. Plain as day she cannot bear living with Son-in-law. Why do you Lin elders insist on forcing this marriage?"
One Lin uncle softened: "Nephew Lin Jue, grant the mutual divorce. Clearly her heart is set on leaving—what joy is there dragging it out?"
Refusing to fold, Lin Jue stood and bowed to the room. "Old Ancestor, uncles, brothers—I will neither agree to mutual divorce nor cast Shuzhen out! Two reasons: first, I still love her and want a life with her; second, if Shuzhen won't guard Zhu family honor, I will! Father-in-law prizes the Zhu name above all—I cannot let him down."
Turning directly to the Old Ancestor, he added, "Old Ancestor, you are of the Zhu clan—guard your family's face and persuade Shuzhen to stay."
The Old Ancestor let out a quiet sigh and rolled her beads in silence.
Hearing helplessness in that sigh, Qianli Xun's stomach tightened—was the divorce really slipping away?
Another Lin elder spoke sharp: "Nephew's wife, claiming one of the Seven Conditions admits you failed wifely virtue! No wonder Lin Jue hit you—sounds like you earned it! With you at fault, whether to divorce or keep you is Lin Jue's call alone!"
Another added, "Seventh Brother misses the point—fault or no fault, in our dynasty the husband always decides divorce! Since Lin Jue doesn't hold it against her and protects Zhu family face, this discussion is over!"
Regret stung Qianli Xun—claiming one of the Seven Conditions had backfired hard! She didn't understand this era's rules, and now the room had flipped completely against her.
It dawned on her at last: Lin Jue hadn't suggested mutual divorce out of mercy—he had set a trap to lecture her in public and crush her hopes! Still sore over that black eye and spending the night tied up? Petty, vengeful jerk!
Boiling over, Qianli Xun stood, marched right up to Lin Jue, and glared straight into his eyes without a word.
Never had his wife stood this close staring straight at him—this fiery new wife gave him fresh hope, making it even harder to let her go. He met her glare with a calm smile and sat back down.
Pulling her gaze off him, Qianli Xun figured divorce or not, better clear her name first.
"Old Ancestor, Lin elders—forget what I just said. I only blurted it out desperate for a divorce! When I got back from yesterday's poetry salon and Lin Jue accused me, I offered to bring him along next time so he could watch with his own eyes and clear my name! Ask my maid Kuqiao—she attends every single salon with me and knows every word spoken. Every rumor outside is pure slander!"
Kuqiao stepped forward at once: "I swear it! Every salon Miss attends, all they do is write verse and discuss poetry—never once an improper act!"
Third Uncle piped up again: "Nephew's wife, a woman's duty is tending her husband at home! Quit attending salons and mingling with scholars—keep that up and gossip's bound to follow and ruin your name! Lucky Lin Jue's patient—were it me, I'd lock you inside the house and see you try stepping out!"
At that, the Old Ancestor broke her silence: "If a daughter of the Zhu house faces mistreatment like that under a Lin roof, this marriage cannot stand. Grant the divorce!"
Seizing the opening, Qianli Xun cried, "Lin Jue didn't lock me in, yet weren't his beatings and courtesans mistreatment?! Third Uncle, Lin elders—heed the Old Ancestor and let him divorce me!"
Not about to let the tide turn back, Lin Jue shot back: "Calling that mistreatment stretches the truth! She mistreated me first! Refusing to consummate our marriage on our wedding night—isn't that mistreatment? Beating me three days ago and trussing me up in a locked room—isn't that mistreatment?!"
Qianli Xun froze—never dreamed he'd air that dirty laundry in front of the whole clan! Game over—the divorce battle was lost.
Third Uncle bristled: "Nephew's wife, plain talk—every bit of this is on you! Refuse his bed? Fine, leave you as primary wife and let him take a concubine. Yet raising fists to your husband and locking him up?! Disgracing the name of a talented lady! Wait—Lin Jue, you're a Training Officer, strong as a bull—how'd you let a woman beat you?"
Loath to bring up her strange transformation or lose face as a man, Lin Jue shrugged: "I held back—refused to stoop to fighting my wife."
Third Uncle turned right to the Old Ancestor: "Old Ancestor—can this marriage still be dissolved?"
The Old Ancestor stopped her beads. "I should take my leave."
"Wait, Old Ancestor—hear me out!" Qianli Xun cried.
Granting her granddaughter's plea, the Old Ancestor stayed seated.
Odds stacked against her, Qianli Xun risked one final Hail Mary—spilling everything: "Truth is, I'm not the real Zhu Shuzhen! Lin Jue beat the old Zhu Shuzhen to death! I'm Qianli Xun from a far more advanced era, master of Qianli Fist! Don't believe me? Watch!"
Stepping into the center, Qianli Xun launched into a crisp, seamless martial form—ending with a thunderous punch into the empty chair beside Matchmaker Yan! Wood splintered clean in two and crashed to the floor, shocking every soul in the room.
Unfazed by now, Kuqiao just worried sick for her mistress.
The Old Ancestor rolled her beads without flinching.
On the final beat of her form, Qianli Xun drove a punch straight at Lin Jue's face—stopping a hair's breadth from his nose!
The sheer gust slammed his eyes shut and snapped his head back.
"Well? Scared now? If you're scared, confess to everyone you beat Zhu Shuzhen to death!"
The breeze from her fist brushed his cheeks, carrying a subtle, clean fragrance of skin. Drawing an involuntary breath, Lin Jue felt his anger melt away; opening his eyes to behold her slender white fist poised inches from his face, he went completely starstruck.
Creeped out by his weird, dreamy stare, Qianli Xun yanked her fist back. "What are you gawking at? Confess your crime in front of everyone!"
Snapping back to earth, Lin Jue spun a half-truth on the spot: "I only learned recently my wife practiced martial arts in secret! Always thought she only wrote poems—turns out we share martial pursuits too! Surely we'll have more in common from now on. Quit acting wild and stop saying you aren't Shuzhen."
A collective sigh of relief swept the Lin elders.
Lin Jue's second brother shook his head: "Sister-in-law, you'll say anything for a divorce—trashing your own name and slandering Third Brother too! You look identical to the day you stepped through our doors—who'd swallow tales of another soul? And with fists like yours, Third Brother isn't even your match—no way he ever beat you!"
A chorus of elders chimed in: "Exactly! Nobody buys it!"
Desperate, Qianli Xun called out: "Kuqiao!"
Stepping forward, Kuqiao pleaded, "My Miss never knew a lick of martial arts before—only took Son-in-law's beatings! Three days ago he hit her again and shoved her into a pillar—look at the cut still on her forehead! She fainted dead away, wouldn't wake up for anything, and when she finally opened her eyes she turned into this and knew martial arts! Country folk call it rebirth!"
Lin Jue barked a loud laugh. "Haha! Kuqiao's your own maid—she'll recite whatever script you feed her! Give it a rest, Wife. The Old Ancestor and our elders are busy folks—let's not hold them up!"
For the first time in her life, Qianli Xun hit a brick wall. Part of her longed to beat Lin Jue until he signed—yet fists couldn't unlock this mess. Helpless, she turned to her sole ally: "Old Ancestor..."
Rising to her feet, gaze resting serene upon the floor ahead, the Old Ancestor murmured: "The world is vast and holds every marvel, yet most mortals stay blind—no surprise nobody accepts rebirth. Never having met the old Zhu daughter, knowing only you before me, I cannot tell one from the other for these elders. Today I am powerless—such is a world where women hold no say over their own fate."
With Matron Xiuzhen beside her, the Old Ancestor walked out of the reception hall.
Every Lin elder and Matchmaker Yan rose to bow: "Farewell, Old Ancestor!"
Watching her retreating silhouette, hollow defeat washed over Qianli Xun. Was she really trapped with Lin Jue forever? Was her life over before it began?
Shen Jianchen's image flashed in her mind. Had she known this awaited her, she should have said yes to him and lived out a whirlwind romance—maybe she wouldn't feel this bleak now.
Once the Old Ancestor was gone, Matchmaker Yan offered parting advice: "Young Madam, word to the wise—you beat the Training Officer, tied him up, locked him away, and he still wants to stay married! Count your blessings! Since there's no divorce, I'm off—got several matches waiting on me! Sort the rest behind closed doors."
Once the matchmaker left, Third Uncle waved a hand: "Everyone disperse!"
One by one the clan filed out, leaving only Qianli Xun, Lin Jue, and Kuqiao in the hall.
Rounding on Lin Jue with blazing eyes, Qianli Xun declared: "Don't think you won and I'm playing obedient wife, Lin Jue! Mark my words right here: never set half a foot inside Zhulan Residence! I go where I want, do what I want—don't dare meddle! We share the West Courtyard, so you stay in your lane and stay out of mine, or you'll regret it! My Zhu dowry keeps me rich—I don't need a coin from you! See me coming, walk the other way!"
Furious, Lin Jue sprang up. "Zhu Shuzhen, you go too far...!"
His rage lit a wicked grin on her face. "You refused the divorce—reap what you sow! Think holding me here turns me into a timid little bird obeying your every whim? Hmph! Remember this: I, Qianli Xun, am NOT the old Zhu Shuzhen you used to push around!"
Spinning on her heel, she strode out; Kuqiao shot Son-in-law a fierce glare and hurried after her mistress.
Left alone to savor his "victory," Lin Jue felt a strange emptiness—funny, he didn't feel victorious at all.
#CH 25
#TITLE: Chapter 25: Melancholy Thoughts on Life
Applying Jixiang Pharmacy's healing ointment faithfully, Qianli Xun saw her forehead cut vanish clean in just two days. Examining herself in the bronze mirror, she found not a trace of a scar—no more walking around advertising a wound.
Smooth, clear skin should have cheered her up, yet gloom clung tight. After the failed divorce, she seemed to have caught Zhu Shuzhen's melancholy.
Last night she had dreamed of Shen Jianchen. In the dream he asked, "Qianli Xun, be my girlfriend!"—and for once she nodded yes. He pulled her tight against his chest, and she tasted the sweet warmth of love for the very first time. His embrace felt so warm she woke convinced its heat still lingered across her shoulders.
Defeat in the divorce battle sharpened her hunger for real love—sparking a longing to fall in love that she had never felt back in her old life. Yet this unwanted marriage rose like a thick wall around her heart, barring every path to freedom.
A heavy sigh escaped her lips: "Ah..."
Right behind the sigh rose a lyric of Zhu Shuzhen's that fit her mood to the bone. "Kuqiao, lay out brush and paper—I want to write a ci."
Carrying fresh whisked tea over, Kuqiao smiled. "Already set up, Miss. Drink your tea first before writing."
Drinking the tea by the mirror, Qianli Xun set the bowl aside, moved to the study desk, dipped her brush full in the inkstone, and wrote:
Slanting wind and fine rain chill the spring; before my wine cup, I recall old joys. Once before pear blossoms, lonely tears fell in streams. Fragrant grass fades in mist along the southern parting road; through farewell tears, I gaze at green hills.
Last night in dreams a tender tie was forged; amid water and clouds, we stood without a word. Yet waking finds sorrow and resentment waiting just as before. Tossing beneath the quilt in vain regret—Heaven is easy to behold, yet seeing you is hard. ("Jiangchengzi: Admiring the Spring") [TN: Translator's rendering]
Brush still poised in her fingers, she stayed lost in the closing line: "Tossing beneath the quilt in vain regret—Heaven is easy to behold, yet seeing you is hard."
Had Shen Jianchen transmigrated here too? And if he had, where in this vast world was he? As her thoughts wandered, excess ink gathered at the brush tip and—plop—dropped dead-center onto the character "you" (伊), ruining all her careful work.
Setting the brush down, she rolled up the sheet. "Throw it away."
Kuqiao took the scroll, tucked it into the wooden tub beside the desk, and hoisted the tub toward the door.
Noticing the tub brimmed with rolled sheets, Qianli Xun asked, "Why are there so many in there?"
Kuqiao glanced back over her shoulder. "All your discarded practice drafts, Miss! You always have me save them up and leave the tub outside the front gate."
"Outside the front gate?"
"What you call trash, outsiders treat like gold!" Kuqiao explained. "Every few days folks camp outside Lin Manor waiting to snatch up your scraps—same back at Zhu Manor!"
Amused yet too low-spirited to care, Qianli Xun let it go as quiet sadness filled her chest.
Carrying the tub outside Lin Manor's main gate, Kuqiao set it down, pulled out the fresh lyric her mistress had just written, kept it in her hand, and called out: "Come on out, all of you! Grab what you want fast so I can get back to chores!"
On cue, five or six page boys and study attendants popped out from corners and swarmed the tub, clearing every scrap in seconds while calling out, "Thanks, Sister Kuqiao!"
Kuqiao stopped one page boy: "Nan Shuang! Hold up!"
Nan Shuang trotted back. "What can I do for you, Sister Kuqiao?"
"Heard your young master's up in Bianliang—why didn't you go with him?"
"You never asked about my master before, Sister Kuqiao—what's got you curious today?"
"Didn't think of it before, thought of it today! Quit asking questions!"
"Think about it, Sister—if I left, who'd collect Youqi Jushi's poems for my master?" Unrolling the scrap he had grabbed, Nan Shuang sighed: "Rotten luck today—only two lines on this sheet."
Kuqiao handed him the intact scroll from her hand. "Miss just wrote this—complete from start to finish. Give it to your master."
"Why so generous to my master today, Sister Kuqiao?"
"Need you ask? Young Master Qiao reads my Miss's poetry deeper than anyone—they're true confidants."
"Then why doesn't your mistress just send her finished poems straight to my master instead of making me camp outside Lin Manor every day?"
"Not a chance! Enough rumors already swirl outside twisting nothing into gossip about Miss and your master—better stay cautious! Keep waiting by the gate; whenever something good turns up, I'll slip it to you."
Thanking her, Nan Shuang darted off. Lifting the empty tub, Kuqiao headed back inside—and ran straight into Lin Jue halfway down the lane.
"More people scavenging useless trash?" Lin Jue snapped.
"Trash to you, Son-in-law—pure treasure to everyone outside," Kuqiao shot back.
Lin Jue bristled. "Watch me station soldiers outside the gate today—arrest every scavenger who shows up, one by one or two by two!"
"Better leave anything touching my Miss alone, Son-in-law—she hears about it, she'll give you another taste of her fists!" With that, Kuqiao walked straight past him.
Glaring at her retreating back, Lin Jue fumed: That maid never used to mouth off like this—getting bolder by the day! Like mistress, like servant!
Over the past two days, Lin Jue had wondered himself whether refusing the divorce was right or wrong. Reason said wrong; his heart said he couldn't let her go. Even keeping her untouched inside Zhulan Residence, as long as she bore the name of Lin Jue's wife, it satisfied his pride—especially knowing all those Lin'an scholars could only gaze from afar and never have her! Above all, he still loved her.
Back inside Zhulan Residence, seeing her mistress staring blankly at the desk, Kuqiao set the tub down and tidied the room without disturbing her.
Transmigrating into Zhu Shuzhen had actually infected Qianli Xun with her gloom! Wait—Zhu Shuzhen had died around forty-five! Qianli Xun had zero intention of dying young and heartbroken! Time to stop acting like Zhu Shuzhen and remember who she was.
Slapping her own forehead, she pep-talked herself: Wake up! You're Qianli Xun—since when did Qianli Xun mope over spring winds and autumn rain?! Pull yourself together and plan your next move! No way am I fading away in my forties!
Energy surged right back. Time to take action and reshape her fate—and lacking a concrete plan yet, why not try a bit of superstition?
"Kuqiao, know any fortune-teller around here who's crazy accurate?"
Pausing her chores, Kuqiao brightened. "You asked the right person, Miss! Don't know her personally, yet everybody knows her name: on a hill outside Lin'an stands a nunnery whose abbess, Master Jingguang, sees past and future! Even Master visited her once."
"My dad?"
Kuqiao nodded. "Word is back when Master was young, after one talk with Master Jingguang his career shot straight up till he became Prefect of Lin'an!"
"That legit?!"
"Only catch is Master Jingguang doesn't see just anyone—she sets conditions, and you only get an audience if you meet them."
"What conditions?"
Kuqiao hesitated. "Not quite sure..."
Fired up, Qianli Xun sprang to her feet. "Let's go meet Master Jingguang! Pack plenty of silver!"
"Right now?"
"Right this second!"
Packing a pouch of silver, Kuqiao trailed her mistress out of Lin Manor, ordered the coachman to hitch the carriage, and rode out of Lin'an.
Ignoring the lively market streets along the way, Qianli Xun focused on one wish: finding a ray of hope for her future, learning whether she'd ever break free from Lin Jue and carve out Qianli Xun's own destiny in Zhu Shuzhen's era.
After a long ride out through the city gates and further along country roads, the carriage rolled to a stop at the foot of a mountain.
"We're here, Madam," the coachman called.
Hopping down first, Qianli Xun looked up at a towering hill glazed lush green from base to peak.
Climbing down behind her, Kuqiao pointed upward. "The nunnery sits halfway up the mountain, Miss—carriages can't climb the trail, so we walk from here."
Without a word, Qianli Xun strode straight onto the trailhead. Years of Qianli Fist made hill climbing effortless; though this body belonged to Zhu Shuzhen, Qianli Xun's instincts steered its reflexes just as nimbly—her full-power punches proved as much.
The winding mountain track ran narrow, its dirt packed hard by countless feet—clearly the nunnery drew heavy traffic.
Five or six pilgrims descended toward them. Stepping aside to let the first pass, Qianli Xun asked, "How much further to the nunnery, Auntie?"
"Not far at all—just a bit further up!" the woman called back as she continued down.
Climbing on, they stepped aside several more times along the single-file path.
Taking in the lush hill views as they went, Qianli Xun reached a broad mid-mountain clearing before she knew it. Ahead stood a nunnery bustling with men and women coming and going, incense stalls lining the entrance.
Glancing up at the plaque reading "By the Compassionate Power of the Buddha," Qianli Xun said, "Buy some incense, Kuqiao—let's go pay respects."
Buying three tall sticks of incense, Kuqiao followed her mistress through the gate.
Outside the main hall stood a huge bronze censer where pilgrims lit incense and planted their sticks.
Kuqiao did the same before following her mistress into the hall.
The instant they stepped across the threshold, chanting rose from both sides: "Namo Amitabha, Namo Amitabha..." Flowing like calm song, the cadence soothed the heart on contact.
Glancing left and right, Qianli Xun saw six nuns seated cross-legged along each side, serene and composed, chanting in unison.
Before the towering Buddha statue lay three prayer cushions. Qianli Xun pressed her palms together and knelt to bow; Kuqiao followed suit. Each bow drew a resonant clack from a wooden fish—"tok!"—blending seamless with the chant.
Rising after three bows, they looked toward the sound and saw a young nun seated beside the statue with a wooden fish and mallet before her.
Qianli Xun stepped over politely. "Little Master, I'd like to see Abbess Master Jingguang—is she available today?"
The young nun rose at once. "Follow me."
Thrilled at how easy that was—guess Kuqiao's rumors were overblown—Qianli Xun shot Kuqiao a teasing glance.
Flushing slightly at being proved wrong, Kuqiao grinned anyway, glad her mistress got her wish so fast.
Together they followed the young nun behind the main hall.
#CH 26
#TITLE: Chapter 26: Fate and Three Trials
Behind the main hall stood smaller worship halls flanked on both sides by monastic living quarters.
The young nun lifted the cloth curtain of one room. "Please step inside."
Expecting the abbess to be waiting, Qianli Xun entered—only to find an empty room with a table, chairs, and tea set.
"Wait a moment here," the young nun said. "I'll fetch Master Cixuan."
"Little Master, I asked for Master Jingguang, not Master Cixuan!" Qianli Xun corrected her.
Without answering, the young nun ducked back past the curtain.
Nothing to do but pull up a chair and wait.
About a quarter hour later, the curtain lifted and an older nun entered holding prayer beads. "Amitabha. Forgive the wait, benefactor."
Rising, Qianli Xun asked, "You're Master Cixuan?"
"I am."
"I came to beg an audience with Master Jingguang—hoping she'll point the way through my troubles."
"The Abbess does not receive outside visitors lightly..."
Qianli Xun flared in alarm. "Not lightly? Then why did that young nun bring me back here?!"
"She only recently took vows and tends the wooden fish in the main hall; she didn't know the rules. I have already reprimanded her."
Stumped—so much for smooth sailing! Having climbed all the way up here, she wasn't leaving empty-handed.
"Please make an exception, Master! I wouldn't dare trouble the Abbess if I weren't facing a crisis of a lifetime."
"Please head back down the mountain, benefactor."
Qianli Xun signaled Kuqiao with her eyes. Kuqiao slipped out one tael of silver and pressed it toward Master Cixuan. "A small offering, Master—please accept."
"Monastics do not covet worldly wealth. Take it back, benefactor."
Master Cixuan's face stayed cold as stone; pulling the silver back, Kuqiao glanced at her mistress for orders.
Thinking one tael looked stingy, Qianli Xun gave another nod.
Kuqiao drew the entire silver pouch, dropped the single tael back inside, and offered the whole bag. "Please accept our sincere offering, Master."
Not a muscle twitched on Master Cixuan's face; Kuqiao tucked the pouch away again.
Seeing silver fail, Qianli Xun dropped straight to her knees, Kuqiao dropping right beside her.
"I'm not leaving this mountain until I see Master Jingguang! Please grant this favor, Master Cixuan!"
