Surprised by the abrupt kneel, Master Cixuan spoke milder: "Rise and go home, benefactor. The Abbess is advanced in years and no longer reads fortunes for laypeople. Just yesterday a senior Lin'an official came seeking her, and I sent him down the mountain too."
"I'm not getting up and I'm not going down! If Master Jingguang won't see me, I'll kneel here forever—going back to that house is worse than staying in a nunnery anyway! Push me and I'll shave my head right here; then you can't keep me from seeing the Abbess!"
Her iron stubbornness put Master Cixuan in a real bind.
Just as she hesitated, another nun of about the same age slipped in, whispered a single line in Cixuan's ear, and slipped straight back out.
Master Cixuan's stony face softened at once. "Rise. Fate links you to the Abbess—follow me."
Breakthrough! Beaming, Qianli Xun climbed up and followed her out of the quarters—sure she was about to meet the Abbess face to face now that fate had spoken.
Passing through a stone archway and turning east, they soon came upon two mountain caves side by side.
Master Cixuan lifted the curtain over the left cave. "Please enter, benefactor."
Leading Kuqiao inside, Qianli Xun found not a single soul.
Straight ahead stood a Buddha statue fronted by a large donation box; beside it sat a tea table stocked with whisking gear and supplies; to the left stood a study desk with the Four Treasures of the Study neatly lined up; and along both walls hung Tang poems—odd decor next to a Buddha, though written in rich, vigorous calligraphy.
Paying no mind to art or verse, Qianli Xun asked, "Where's the Abbess?"
"In the right-hand cave. Fate is only step one—to meet the Abbess, you must pass three tests and win her approval."
Hoop after hoop! Having come this far, she wasn't turning back now.
"Name the three tests, Master."
"First is simple: place every coin and every valuable item on your person into the donation box."
Simple indeed! Qianli Xun thought wryly: Refused my silver earlier just to clean me out completely here! Fine, whatever! "Kuqiao!" she called.
Kuqiao emptied every last coin they had brought straight into the donation box.
Qianli Xun stripped off her jade bangle, earrings, and gold hairpins and dropped them all inside.
"Test one done, Master—what's number two?"
"Your maid still wears something of value," Master Cixuan pointed out.
Kuqiao balked. "Miss is the one seeing the Abbess, not me—why take my one and only silver bracelet?!"
Stepping over, Qianli Xun grabbed Kuqiao's right wrist, pushed back her sleeve, and slipped the silver bracelet off. "I'll buy you a whole stack later!"
Relieved, Kuqiao grumbled, "You'd better keep your word, Miss!"
Dropping the bracelet into the box, Qianli Xun promised, "A promise worth a thousand gold!"
Satisfied, Master Cixuan moved on: "Second: pick any poem on the wall, write out your interpretation on paper, and I will present it to the Abbess. If she approves, you advance to test three; if not, leave the mountain at once."
Analyzing poetry? Easy! She scanned the Tang poems lining both walls.
Before transmigrating, Qianli Xun hadn't cared much for Wang Wei—yet right now his "The Bamboo Pavilion" pulled her eyes like a magnet. Stepping close to study the lines, ideas surged into her head; turning to the desk, she sat down and poured every thought onto paper.
Watching her mistress write with deep focus, Kuqiao smiled inside: Right now she looks identical to the old Miss!
Filling a full sheet in crisp small regular script, Qianli Xun set down her brush. "Please deliver this to the Abbess, Master."
Kuqiao carried the sheet over to Master Cixuan, who folded it neat and stepped past the curtain.
Minutes later Cixuan returned empty-handed. "Congratulations, benefactor—the Abbess approved your essay. Ready for test three."
No surprise there—literary essays were her bread and butter. "What's test three?"
"Whisk a bowl of tea for the Abbess. If she approves upon tasting, you may meet her; if not, descend the mountain."
Whisking tea was Kuqiao's specialty—Qianli Xun hadn't learned a lick yet! "Can my maid make it?"
"No. You seek the audience, not your maid. You must prepare it with your own hands."
"Can my maid stand beside me and talk me through it?"
"No—that is no different from her making it herself."
Leaving the desk for the tea table, Qianli Xun stared blankly at the array of tools. Straining to recall Kuqiao's moves—first, warm the tea bowl! Picking up a bowl, she poured in hot water from the kettle and swirled it in both hands—kind of fun! Done swirling, she looked around clueless where to dump the rinse water.
Kuqiao screamed inside her head: The waste water bowl is RIGHT THERE! Dump it in there!
Instead, her mistress glanced at the floor and splashed the water straight onto the ground, leaving Kuqiao staring in despair.
Setting the bowl down—next step: tea powder! Opening the caddy, she scooped one wooden spoonful in, thought it looked skimpy, and dumped several more big scoops on top.
Too much! Way too much! Kuqiao wailed silently.
Next, hot water! She remembered Kuqiao adding water in rounds—how many rounds though?
Things come in threes—so three rounds it is! If three pours had to fill the bowl, round one needed a hefty splash. Going by gut, she poured in a third of a bowl.
Too much water! You flooded it! Kuqiao nearly fainted inside.
Grabbing the bamboo whisk, Qianli Xun started stirring in circles.
Whisk back and forth—not stir like soup! Unable to watch another second, Kuqiao clapped both hands over her eyes. Miss herself taught me how to whisk tea—how did she forget every single step?! Peeking through her fingers, she saw her mistress finish in three pours and a few clumsy stirs.
Seven infusions turned into three splashes—can you even call that tea?! Kuqiao lamented silently, glancing at stone-faced Master Cixuan, whose strict posture kept the maid rooted in place.
Qianli Xun peered down at her creation: worlds apart from Kuqiao's creamy froth, just murky liquid dotted with pathetic specks of foam nobody in their right mind would drink.
Oh well—she'd given it her best shot. Plan B was already set: if the Abbess turned her away over bad tea, she'd storm straight into the next cave, drop to her knees, and cry until the Abbess caved! Mind made up, she ordered, "Kuqiao, hand the bowl to Master."
Lifting the bowl and peeking inside, Kuqiao thought: Who on earth could drink this?!
Master Cixuan took it, glanced at the brew, knitted her brows without a word, and walked out.
Rising, Qianli Xun whispered to Kuqiao, "No way the Abbess likes my tea—in a minute I'm rushing straight into the next cave!"
"Will that even work?" Kuqiao worried.
"Sure it will! Playing by the rules gets you nowhere sometimes—you've got to catch folks off guard! The second Master Cixuan tells us to leave, we play nice and step outside; the instant she looks away, you grab her arm and I bolt straight inside!"
Kuqiao nodded.
Moments later Master Cixuan parted the curtain. Before she could open her mouth, Qianli Xun jumped in: "Don't bother saying it, Master—my tea was awful, the Abbess won't see me, we'll pack up and go!" She shot Kuqiao the signal glance.
A faint smile broke across Master Cixuan's face. "The Abbess grants you leave to enter her cave for an audience."
Jackpot! The Abbess accepted that swamp water?! Guess she wasn't picky about tea—then why make her whisk it in the first place? Never mind! Getting in without causing a scene saved both the effort and Zhu Shuzhen's ladylike image.
#CH 27
#TITLE: Chapter 27: Changing Fate at the Nunnery
From outside, both caves looked identical—except Master Jingguang's cave had its wooden window propped half open. Peering inside, Qianli Xun saw only pitch darkness.
Weird—why was the first cave bright with shut windows while the Abbess's cave was dark with an open one? Then she remembered: oil lamps had been burning inside the first cave; too busy with her three trials, she hadn't even noticed.
Just then a butterfly drifted straight out of the half-open window—bigger and lovelier than any butterfly Qianli Xun had ever seen, electric blue wings edged in velvet black. Fluttering in a circle around her, it landed right on the back of her outstretched hand instead of fleeing, softening her impulse to catch it into quiet awe.
Master Cixuan called toward the cave: "Abbess, the lady benefactor awaits outside."
The instant she spoke, the window pulled shut from within. The cave door opened to reveal the same nun who had whispered to Cixuan earlier: "Let her enter."
As if understanding human speech, the blue butterfly lifted off and vanished.
Watching it flutter away, Qianli Xun stepped toward the cave entrance.
Kuqiao started in behind her, only for Master Cixuan to block her: "Stay outside and wait."
Halting, Kuqiao waited until Cixuan walked off before taking in the quiet yard outside the caves.
Swept spotless and spare, it held only one unfamiliar tree blooming with vibrant rose-red flowers, petals scattered across a stone table and stools beneath its branches.
Blowing petals off a stone stool, Kuqiao sat down to wait.
The second Qianli Xun stepped inside, the nun shut the cave door behind her.
Pitch darkness swallowed everything. Stopping dead until her eyes adjusted, she made out the nun's dim outline beside her. "Master, where is the Abbess?"
Without speaking, the nun moved aside and struck a tinder tube to light an oil lamp, bathing the chamber in warm glow.
This cave ran much deeper than the other; she stood only in an outer chamber holding a table, two oil lamps, and a wooden box. Does the Abbess hate sunlight? Qianli Xun wondered.
The nun explained: "Benefactor, the Abbess is in ascetic retreat and sees no guests. Only because a butterfly braved the dark to fly inside and alight upon her did she sense a fated visitor and grant you this meeting."
Remembering the blue butterfly, gratitude warmed Qianli Xun's heart. Moths seek flame, yet that butterfly flew straight into darkness—no wonder the Abbess saw an omen. Either way, that butterfly had done her a massive favor; if she ever crossed paths with it again...
Not that she could think what favor you even do for a butterfly.
Peering toward the inner cave, she saw solid blackness—without the nun beside her, she'd have gotten chills.
Extending a hand, the nun said, "The Abbess sits within. Take my hand and follow me."
Gripping the nun's hand, Qianli Xun walked deeper into the dark. "With a name like Master Jingguang—Pure Light—I figured she'd love bright rooms, not sit in the dark."
"As a layperson, benefactor, you wouldn't know that ascetics also train in enduring the hardship of darkness."
Too deep for Qianli Xun—and off topic anyway. After walking several paces through ink-black air, the nun stopped, and she stopped too.
"Abbess, the lady benefactor is here," the nun announced.
An aged woman's voice floated out of the dark: "Cikong, have her sit."
Master Cikong guided her hand. "One step forward, benefactor." Qianli Xun stepped forward.
"Sit now." Feeling her way down in the dark, she landed square on a soft prayer cushion.
Listening to Cikong step aside without a stumble, Qianli Xun marveled at her dark-room navigation skills.
At last face to face with Master Jingguang—unseen yet unmistakably seated right across from her—Qianli Xun wasted no time.
"Abbess, I'm trapped in a life crisis with nowhere left to turn, which is why I climbed this mountain to beg your guidance—please show me a clear path forward."
"Unhappy in your marriage and desperate to break free of your husband?"
Spot on! Qianli Xun gasped inside—the Abbess really sees everything! "Yes!" she blurted out.
"Since ancient times, women have followed whatever husband fate dealt them, rarely escaping an unhappy match—and even when they do, gossip clings to their name. Have you thought what lies ahead once you leave him?"
"I haven't thought that far—I just want out! To me, leaving this marriage is the only way my real life can begin."
Silence descended, heavy and still in the dark.
As her eyes adjusted further, Qianli Xun made out a shadowy silhouette seated across from her, with Cikong standing beside. Tired of the hush, she spoke again:
"You guessed why I came on the first try—proving you truly see past and future! Tell me: do I actually get out of this marriage? And what does my future hold?"
At length the Abbess spoke, sidestepping her questions: "The instant you entered, I sensed your spirit does not belong to this era. Tell me first how you came here."
Stunned by the Abbess's insight, Qianli Xun held nothing back—telling of the earthquake, her death, and waking up inside Zhu Shuzhen's body. Finishing, she asked, "Above all, I want to know: am I Zhu Shuzhen or Qianli Xun? Will I end up living Zhu Shuzhen's tragic fate, or carve out Qianli Xun's own destiny?"
The Abbess countered, "Who do you believe yourself to be?"
Pausing a beat, Qianli Xun answered firm: "Everyone else sees Zhu Shuzhen—yet I still believe I am Qianli Xun."
"A sage of our dynasty wrote the Treatise on Response and Retribution, whose opening line states: 'Good fortune and calamity have no fixed gate; people summon them upon themselves.' Likewise Buddhism teaches the Five Precepts and Ten Virtues—all reminding mortals that the surest way to change one's fate is changing one's own conduct, not bowing to idols or chasing divination..."
Qianli Xun tuned out the preaching on treatises and precepts—until "change one's fate" perked her ears right up.
"Whether you live as Zhu Shuzhen or Qianli Xun depends on your actions—and actions follow mindset. Transform your mindset first, and you will live Qianli Xun's life in this era."
Relief warmed her—wearing Zhu Shuzhen's body or not, her mindset couldn't be more different! Clearly she was already on the right track.
"Still, this body belongs to Zhu Shuzhen, so her destiny will pull at yours—meaning you must strive harder. This marriage is one such tether. From what I perceive, Zhu Shuzhen's chart carries a rocky road in love; reborn through her, your path won't run smooth either, yet it differs from hers. Once you pass through a few romantic trials, your road opens clear."
Wait—how is she reading my face in pitch darkness?! Qianli Xun wondered. Can she see in the dark while I'm blind? Do Buddhist masters unlock night vision?!
Catching the hint about future romantic trials, she lit up: "Does that mean I'll get out of this marriage?!"
"Even reading your fate, Heaven's secrets must not be revealed—some things cannot be spoken plain. Remember only this: whatever strikes, hold to Right Thought and Right Mindfulness, and peril turns to blessing."
"Your words wake me like cold water, Abbess! I'll hold to Right Thought and Right Mindfulness without fail!"
"Since fate brought us together, let me offer one more boost." Pausing a breath, the Abbess went on: "You chose Wang Wei's 'The Bamboo Pavilion,' and I read your essay word for word. Original view—arguing the poem doesn't praise a hermit's loftiness so much as voice helplessness and critique the times. There's truth to that: talent like Wang Wei belonged in office serving the people, not hiding alone in mountain woods—a waste shaped by his era's politics. Thinking that independently proves you have a mind of your own, which is step one in mastering your destiny. Yet I noticed you never once mentioned the poem's key word: 'bamboo.' From that I sensed bamboo brings you good luck. Go home, find a place surrounded by a bamboo grove, and stay there one full month—you'll see its gift."
So the poem test wasn't an exam—it was a reading of her mind!
"Done! I'll do exactly that!"
"As for your tea—utterly dreadful, didn't touch a drop! Care to know why I still agreed to see you?"
"I have no idea—please enlighten me, Abbess."
"Because your awful tea looked identical to the very first bowl I ever made in my youth! Cixuan described every move—especially dumping the warming water straight onto the floor! Haha—I did the exact same thing my first time! Made me feel close to you right away!"
Qianli Xun grinned in the dark—dumb luck at its finest!
"On your way out, Cikong will hand you a red silk ribbon. Write your name on it—which name is your choice. Behind the mountain grows a Millennial Divine Willow; tie your ribbon to a western branch, and it will aid your path."
A Millennial Divine Willow?! Chills raced up her spine—a Divine Willow back in her old era, and another right here! Her destiny really was tangled up with that tree.
The Abbess fell silent, and Cikong's voice stepped in: "You may leave now, benefactor."
Stepping near, Cikong extended a hand. "Take my hand."
Rising, Qianli Xun took her fingers and followed her out.
Stepping from pitch darkness back into lamplight seared her eyes shut—yet Cikong didn't even blink!
Slowly squinting open, Qianli Xun asked, "Master, how come the light didn't blind you coming out of there?"
"You get used to it," Cikong replied brief, lifting a red silk ribbon from the wooden box on the table. "Take this. Ask the young nun at the wooden fish in the main hall—she'll point you toward the Divine Willow."
Accepting the ribbon with thanks, Qianli Xun pushed the door open and left.
Once she was gone, Cikong shut the door, lit the second oil lamp, and carried it into the inner cave, flooding the chamber with light.
On a stone platform spread with soft cushions sat Abbess Jingguang cross-legged, eyes closed, hands resting on her knees.
Setting the lamp down, Cikong teased, "Master never lights a lamp, so none of your devotees even know what you look like."
"All the better," Master Jingguang replied calm. "Saves me from getting mobbed every time I go down the mountain."
"Master, will the advice you gave that girl actually work?"
"Whether it works rests with her, not me. Blind mortals cling to fortune-telling; awaken to your true mind, and you see fate is crafted by your own hands."
"Master sees right through everything—that's why your words cut straight to the bone."
#CH 28
#TITLE: Chapter 28: Another Millennial Divine Willow
Seeing her mistress emerge carrying a red silk ribbon, Kuqiao jumped up at once. "Did it go well, Miss?"
Qianli Xun nodded. "Back to the main hall."
Retracing their steps, they reentered the main hall to steady chanting. Pilgrims knelt in prayer while the young nun tapped her wooden fish; waiting respectfully to the side, Qianli Xun held back until the worshipers exited through the rear.
Spotting the red ribbon in Qianli Xun's hand, the young nun pressed her palms together and stepped over. "Amitabha. Come with me, benefactor."
She led Qianli Xun and Kuqiao behind the main hall.
"Brush and ink are right here—write your name first, then I'll show you the way to the Divine Willow."
Spread across a small table behind the hall sat brush and ink. Laying the red silk flat and lifting the brush, Qianli Xun paused.
Deep down she was Qianli Xun, yet everyone in this world called her Zhu Shuzhen. Which name to write? After a long beat, she decided: write both! In neat small regular script she wrote "Qianli Xun," then "Zhu Shuzhen" beside it.
Remembering how modern book credits drew a black box around the name of a deceased co-author, she traced a neat rectangular box around "Zhu Shuzhen." Setting down the brush, she lifted the ribbon and nodded in satisfaction.
"All done, Little Master—lead the way."
"Right this way."
Exiting through the west door behind the hall, they soon reached the nunnery's back gate opening straight onto the rear mountain slope.
"This is as far as I guide you, benefactor. The Divine Willow stands at the foot of the rear slope—follow this path straight down till you see a pond. The willow stands right by the water, easy to spot with all the red ribbons tied to its boughs by devotees."
With that, the young nun stepped back through the gate and shut it tight.
"Wait—why'd she shut the door?! How do we get back?" Qianli Xun yelped.
"No worries, Miss—once we get down to the bottom, we just skirt the base of the hill back around to the front trailhead!" Kuqiao pointed out.
"Good point! Let's head down."
"Let me hold the ribbon for you, Miss."
Handing it over, Qianli Xun watched Kuqiao roll the silk neat and tuck it inside her sleeve.
Together they headed down the rear mountain path.
Lovely as the rear slope looked, Qianli Xun had eyes for one thing alone—this Divine Willow also stood beside a pond! Would seeing it feel like stepping back into her old life?
The trail ran smooth and well trodden—no doubt young lovers trooped down from the nunnery every day to hang ribbons.
Going downhill flew by fast. Soon they reached the base amid boulders and waist-high wild grass—some blades tall as Qianli Xun herself! Luckily a narrow path cut through the weeds; Kuqiao parted the brush ahead while Qianli Xun pushed stalks aside behind her.
Stepping into the clear first, Kuqiao called out, "Miss, there really is a pond!"
Pushing past the last tangle of tall grass, Qianli Xun stepped out before a quiet pond blooming with lotuses. A frog perched silent on a lotus leaf, bulging eyes scanning the bank before springing into the water, scattering green duckweed across the ripples.
Déjà vu hit like a wave. Hurrying along the shore, Qianli Xun scanned for the willow.
Impossible to miss: a lone ancient willow stood by the water, draped head to toe in red silk ribbons blazing against green leaves, lending the venerable tree a striking charm.
Rounding the pond toward the willow, Qianli Xun felt her pulse hammer. Was this a dream, or had she slipped back to before the quake? Trunk for trunk, branch for branch, this tree mirrored the exact Divine Willow that had toppled in the earthquake—only dressed in red ribbons.
Reaching the trunk, emotion broke loose; tears spilled down her cheeks as she threw her arms around the thick bark like hugging long-lost kin, whispering: "Take me home... I hate this era—especially this ridiculous marriage!"
Eyes shut tight, she waited, willing the willow to answer.
Nothing moved—only cold, inert bark beneath her palms. Opening her eyes, hollow disappointment washed over her.
Releasing the trunk and stepping back, gazing up at lush fronds and ribbons fluttering in the breeze, she knew beyond doubt this was the same willow that had hurled her through time.
Spinning around, she scanned for the clearing where she and Shen Jianchen had dueled—nothing but weeds, stones, and hills; aside from pond and willow, not a single landmark matched. Reality settled back in: this was the willow a thousand years earlier—how could modern traces exist here?
Stranger still, back in her era the willow grew in another city altogether, yet here it stood outside Lin'an—dream or reality?
Drawing the ribbon from her sleeve, Kuqiao offered, "Let me tie it up for you, Miss." She reached for a low willow branch.
Snapping out of her daze, Qianli Xun stopped her. "Master Jingguang said tie it to a western branch!"
Pausing to check the afternoon sun slanting west, Kuqiao stepped around to the western side. "West side right here, Miss—let me find a nice branch."
Glancing up, Qianli Xun saw almost every branch within arm's reach crowded with ribbons. No way was she squishing hers in down low where anyone could yank it off!
Pointing high toward the crown, she declared, "I'm tying mine up there!"
Following her finger, Kuqiao gasped, "Whoa, Miss! How do we climb way up there?! You're messing with me!"
"Hand it here!"
Placing the ribbon in her palm, Kuqiao blinked. "You can actually reach that high, Miss?"
Without a word, Qianli Xun clenched the silk, kicked off with light-foot skill, and shot straight up into the willow canopy, balancing on supple boughs light as air.
"My Miss is awesome!" Kuqiao cheered from below.
Tying the ribbon tight to a high western bough, Qianli Xun paused just before leaping down—catching sight of an estate tucked deep inside a dense forest not far past the pond, far too grand for common folk.
Dropping light beside Kuqiao, she pointed toward the woods. "There's a manor hidden over there—know whose it is?"
Squinting where she pointed, Kuqiao saw only solid green canopy and not a single rooftop.
"Guess you don't know either. Never mind—none of our business. Let's go!"
Casting one last look back at the willow—she planned to visit often, drawn by its familiar comfort in a strange age—Qianli Xun set off with Kuqiao skirting the mountain base amid weeds and boulders. Remembering Master Jingguang's advice, she asked, "Kuqiao, know anywhere with a bamboo grove?"
Walking ahead, Kuqiao lit up. "My home village grows bamboo for a living—we're surrounded by a huge bamboo grove! Why ask, Miss?"
"Master Jingguang's advice—says bamboo brings me luck and told me to live near a bamboo grove for one month!"
"Awesome! Come stay at my house, Miss—I get to spend time with my parents too!" Joy flashed, then dimmed with worry: "Only... Son-in-law probably won't let you go."
Qianli Xun scoffed, "Who cares what Lin Jue thinks? My life, my call!"
Chatting along, they rounded the hill back to the front trailhead, where the Lin coachman sat on a rock chewing a flatbread; spotting Madam, he pocketed the rest and climbed onto the box ready to roll.
Night had just fallen when they reached Lin Manor.
Alerted by servants that Zhu Shuzhen had been out all day, Lin Jue summoned the coachman the moment she returned to Zhulan Residence. "Where did she go today?"
"To the nunnery, Master."
"The nunnery? Doing what?"
"I only stayed by the carriage at the foot of the hill, Master—couldn't say for sure. Guessing burning incense and praying for blessings."
"Anyone could guess that! Get out!" All Lin Jue really wanted to know was whether she had sneaked off to meet some scholar under cover of praying.
Back in Zhulan Residence, eyeing the clumps of bamboo planted in her own yard, Qianli Xun wondered why staying here didn't count—then figured a handful of potted stalks couldn't compare to a real forest.
Changing into house clothes, hunger hit. "Starving, Kuqiao—bring dinner!"
Kuqiao dashed to the kitchen and soon returned with two dishes and a bowl of chicken soup—nothing unusual.
Until she set down a bowl of rice dotted with pink petals! "What's this fancy rice?"
"Peach Blossom Rice."
"Peach Blossom Rice?" Qianli Xun murmured, recalling Lu You's famous couplet: "Soft-steamed fragrant rice with peach blossoms, light sips from clear cups of bamboo-leaf brew." [TN: Translator's rendering] Good thing she kept it silent—in the fourth year of Xuanhe, Lu You hadn't even been born yet! "Peach season passed ages ago—where'd peach blossoms come from?"
"Haven't you heard of Caesarweed—ground peach blossom, Miss?"
Shaking her head: "Never."
"Grows all along the paths in Lin Manor—you've passed it plenty, just didn't know its name!"
Right—those waist-high bushes with flowers shaped just like peach blossoms! So much to learn in this era. Scooping a bite, she savored sweet, sticky fragrance. "Yum! Tastes like red dates too!"
"You always steam Peach Blossom Rice with red dates, Miss—dig deeper and you'll hit them."
After dinner, Qianli Xun told Kuqiao to pack tonight so they could head out to the bamboo grove first thing tomorrow. Thinking of Lin Jue—well, she lived under his roof and still wore his wife's title; might as well drop him a courtesy notice.
Walking solo to Lin Jue's quarters, she heard a woman's coy giggle drifting through the closed door—another brothel courtesan!
Half tempted to walk away, she stopped herself. She didn't care two cents about Lin Jue anyway—why act shy? Letting him see she couldn't care less might make him regret refusing divorce sooner! Besides, servants kept passing through the yard; turning tail would make them smirk at her for being weak.
Flagging down a passing servant, she ordered: "Go knock and tell Lin Jue I have something to tell him."
Stepping up to the door, the servant called in: "Master, Madam is here to speak with you."
The giggling cut off dead. Moments later the door opened—and the servant bolted fast, sure a brawl was about to erupt.
Standing in the doorway, Lin Jue sneered: "Didn't you swear we stay out of each other's hair? What brings you to my door—changed your mind and came to consummate our marriage?"
That crack lit her fuse on the spot. "Consummate my foot! I'm here to give you notice—let's talk inside!"
Shouldering Lin Jue aside, she marched right in—curiosity half nudging her to peek at his guest, only to find the front room empty.
Spinning back with a mocking laugh, Qianli Xun teased, "Ha! Why hide her, Lin Jue? Am I that scary? Relax—I don't care one bit who you bring home! Consider rule two revoked: haul as many courtesans into Lin Manor as your heart desires!"
Chun Jinqiao sauntered out from the inner chamber. "Told you it was fine, Officer Lin—you're the one who made me hide!"
Eyeing heavily painted Chun Jinqiao, Qianli Xun laughed again. "Well, well—loyal to one courtesan at least!"
Irritated, Lin Jue snapped, "What do you actually want?"
"Just giving notice: starting tomorrow I'm moving out to the countryside for a spell. When I come back depends on my mood."
"Just you, or somebody else?"
"Not alone—Kuqiao's coming."
Relief relaxed his jaw. "Long as you aren't off with some wild man."
"Wild man" pricked her temper. "You haul wild women straight into the house—even if I went off with a wild man, none of your business! Tend your own mess first!"
Slamming the door hard behind her, she walked out.
Latching the door, Chun Jinqiao sidled up and purred in a sugary voice, "Officer Lin, what kind of life is this? Just agree to mutual divorce and let her go! I don't expect you to buy my freedom—just want you married to a sweet wife who loves and pampers you!"
Helpless against her cooing, Lin Jue scooped her right off her feet. "Not letting her go just yet—wait till I'm ready." Carrying Chun Jinqiao, he strode into the inner room.
#CH 29
#TITLE: Chapter 29: The Bamboo Grove's Owner Returns
Early next morning right after breakfast, Kuqiao had the carriage hitched and rounded up three or four maids to load last night's cloth bundles and a wooden chest aboard.
The bundles held clothes, cosmetics, and the Four Treasures of the Study; the chest held full whisked-tea gear—country villages lacked such tools, and her mistress drank tea every day.
Everything loaded, Kuqiao returned to Zhulan Residence. "All set to go, Miss."
"Packed enough silver?"
"Plenty, Miss!"
Stepping out of Zhulan Residence and leaving the West Courtyard, Qianli Xun lingered a beat over the Caesarweed bushes along the paths, last night's fragrant Peach Blossom Rice still fresh in her mind.
Right then a familiar voice piped up behind her: "My, Young Madam really means business—leaving even earlier than me this morning!"
Stopping to glance back, Qianli Xun saw Chun Jinqiao. "Heading back to Chunxiang Brothel?"
"Where else? A woman like me can't compare to Young Madam, free to come and go as you please."
Spotting a mosquito dive toward Chun Jinqiao, Qianli Xun raised a hand to swat it.
Chun Jinqiao clapped a handkerchief over her cheek in terror. "Going to hit me again, Young Madam?!"
Dropping her hand, Qianli Xun shrugged. "Swatting a mosquito—flew off already."
"Scared me half to death—thought you were slapping me again!" Lowering her cloth, Chun Jinqiao swayed past her down the path.
Watching her swaying hips—so that's Lin Jue's type—an idea struck Qianli Xun. "Hey! Hold up a second!"
Chun Jinqiao halted and turned. "Yes, Young Madam?"
Stepping close, Qianli Xun asked, "Every time Lin Jue brings a courtesan home, it's always you, right?"
Pride flashed on Chun Jinqiao's face. "Of course! Out of all Chunxiang Brothel, Officer Lin only cares for me."
"Then do him a favor and talk him into granting me a mutual divorce."
Studying Lin Manor's primary wife with sharp curiosity, Chun Jinqiao murmured, "You really are one of a kind, Young Madam—pity your heart isn't with Officer Lin. Don't worry, for his sake I'll try talking him round, though right now he still can't let you go."
Swaying on toward the front gate, Chun Jinqiao crossed paths with Lin Jue's two older brothers, neatly dressed on their way out. Ignoring them completely, she climbed straight into Chunxiang Brothel's carriage.
Eldest Brother fumed: "What a disgrace! Third Brother hauling brothel women home again—shaming the family name!"
Second Brother sighed: "Cut him some slack, Eldest Brother—his wife refuses his bed and has zero heart for him, and he's a healthy young man."
"Next chance I get, I'm telling Third Brother to take a proper concubine!"
Overhearing right behind them, Qianli Xun chimed in cheerful: "Morning, Eldest Brother, Second Brother! Brilliant idea—hurry up and get Lin Jue a concubine! Chun Jinqiao who just walked out looks like a great fit—why not her?"
Both brothers exchanged awkward glances.
Eldest Brother balked: "Even a concubine has to come from a respectable family—how could we pick a courtesan? Never!"
Dodging the topic, Second Brother asked, "Where are you headed so early, Sister-in-law?"
Making no secret of it—they were the ones who had blocked her divorce—Qianli Xun shot back, "Staying in the countryside for a spell to breathe fresh air! Stuck inside Lin Manor was suffocating me!"
Second Brother held back Eldest Brother before he could scold, leaving both watching helpless as Qianli Xun and Kuqiao stepped through the gate and boarded their carriage.
Eldest Brother shook his head: "What on earth is this?! Ever seen a wife act like that?!"
"If even Third Brother can't handle her, what can we do?" Second Brother sighed. "Might as well talk Third Brother into agreeing to mutual divorce and letting her go."
Eldest Brother nodded. "Let's invite him for drinks tonight—just us three brothers—and talk sense into him."
...
Rolling through bustling city streets, Qianli Xun spotted a jewelry shop out the window and ordered the driver to stop.
Inside, counters gleamed with gold and silver pieces in every style.
Spotting a handsome silver bracelet, Qianli Xun picked it up and smiled. "Hold out your wrist, Kuqiao."
Knowing her mistress meant to replace the bangle donated at the nunnery, Kuqiao didn't hesitate. "Thanks, Miss!"
Slipping the bracelet onto her wrist, Qianli Xun watched Kuqiao admire it—even prettier than the old one!
"One tael of silver," the shopkeeper said.
Without blinking, Qianli Xun ordered, "Wrap up a matching one!"
"One's plenty, Miss—no need to buy me two!" Kuqiao protested.
"Not for you—for your mother! I'm crashing at your family's place for a whole month; can't show up empty-handed."
"No need to be polite, Miss—my parents will be over the moon just hosting you! They'd never ask for gifts."
"Whether they ask is their business; bringing gifts is mine."
Wrapping the package, the shopkeeper said, "Two taels of silver."
Kuqiao accepted the bundle and counted out two taels from her pouch.
When Qianli Xun insisted on buying more, Kuqiao talked her down to picking up a box of Lin'an specialty pastries.
Once clear of the city gates, bamboo thickened along the roadside—taller and sturdier than the stalks in Zhulan Residence—until a vast emerald bamboo sea opened ahead.
Rolling deep into the dense grove, Qianli Xun marveled that anyone could live hidden in here—until the stalks thinned into a clearing dotted with bamboo houses!
Rough yet sturdy—human ingenuity never ceased to amaze her, lashing stalks into entire dwellings.
Where the track ended, the carriage rolled to a stop.
Peeking out, Qianli Xun saw villagers turning heads from every direction.
Carriages barely entered the village once a year, drawing instant crowds: faces poked out of doorways, hands paused mid-chore, folks headed out turned back for a look, and playing children ran right up.
"I'll hop down and grab some hands to haul our luggage, Miss," Kuqiao said.
The moment Kuqiao jumped out, a villager called, "Hey, it's Kuqiao! Working for rich folks in the city really upgrades you—rolling in on a fancy carriage! Thought the rent collector came early!"
Kids who knew Kuqiao swarmed around: "Sister Kuqiao, bring any treats from town?!"
Prepared, Kuqiao pulled five or six candies from her sleeve to hand out, then called to the crowd: "Couple of strong arms to carry luggage to my house!"
Three or four young men jumped forward at once: "I'll grab it! Me too!"
Climbing back up, Kuqiao passed down the bundles and wooden chest while the lads eagerly shouldered them.
Kuqiao stepped down first, followed by Qianli Xun.
Kuqiao instructed the driver: "Uncle, head on back to Lin Manor—come pick Miss and me up on this exact date next month!"
Nodding, the coachman turned the carriage back out of the village.
Turning around, Kuqiao caught every villager staring wide-eyed at her mistress—no surprise, country folk had never seen a refined noble lady like her up close.
Swelling with pride, Kuqiao announced: "This is Eldest Miss of Zhu Manor! She's staying at my home for a spell—and she's the owner of this bamboo grove, so mind your manners!"
"Owner of the bamboo grove?!" Qianli Xun blinked in surprise, shooting Kuqiao a sharp look—how many more secrets did this girl have?
So used to her lively mistress by now she'd clean forgotten her amnesia, Kuqiao scrambled to explain: "Didn't mean to hide it, Miss—forgot you don't remember!"
Holding further questions back in front of the crowd, Qianli Xun smiled at the villagers. "Good folks, apologies for imposing for a bit—hope you don't mind my stay."
Right then the village head hurried up; with the estate proprietor arriving in person, he had to pay respects. "Welcome, Proprietor! Forgive us for not greeting you sooner—had you sent word ahead, I would've readied proper quarters!"
"And you are?" Unfamiliar with him yet sensing status in his manner, Qianli Xun asked polite.
"Our village head!" Kuqiao introduced him fast. "Uncle Li, Miss is staying at my house—no need to trouble yourself."
"Don't stand on ceremony, Village Head," Qianli Xun added. "Kuqiao's place is fine."
"In that case, right this way, Proprietor."
Leading them forward—two steps in, a bold villager yelled: "Proprietor! Any chance you'll cut our rent?!"
Not having met Matron Wei yet and clueless about rent rates, Qianli Xun kept silent.
The village head barked at the man: "Shut your trap and get lost!"
Narrow lanes twisted past bamboo huts lined on every side by bamboo stalks. Rounding about ten homes, they arrived at Kuqiao's family house—decent-sized yet worn by years of weather, barely holding together.
Outside, the village head hollered: "Old Ku! Big guest at your door!"
Two men and a woman emerged: an older weathered man with frost at his temples; a sturdy lad around fourteen or fifteen, sun-darkened and muscled from toil; and a silver-templed woman with creased brow and skin weathered by years of work.
Spotting Kuqiao first, the woman rushed down the bamboo steps in joy. "Qiao, you're home! Mother missed you to pieces!"
"Mother, this is my Miss—she's staying with us for a while!" Kuqiao introduced her.
Turning to Qianli Xun, the mother beamed: "This beauty straight out of a painting is Miss Zhu? Such a kind face—knowing you serve her puts my heart at ease!" Taking Qianli Xun's hand, she urged, "Quick, come sit inside!"
Qianli Xun let herself be drawn into the bamboo house.
Having set the luggage inside, the young helpers lingered outside craning their necks through the doorway.
The village head bellowed: "What's there to gawk at? Get back to your own work!"
Scared of the village head, the lads scattered grinning.
Old Ku and his son invited the village head inside. "Anything the Proprietor needs, just say the word—don't be shy," the village head told them.
Grinning ear to ear, Old Ku replied, "With your backing, Village Head, you bet! Need anything, I'll send Kujue right over."
Lowering his voice, the village head voiced his worry: "Every year Matron Wei comes to collect rent—why'd the Proprietor show up in person this year? Something changing?"
"Always suspicious, Village Head!" Old Ku waved it off. "Didn't Kuqiao say she's just staying a spell? Doubt it's about rent."
"Either way, let me step in and greet the Proprietor before heading off."
They walked in together, Old Ku and Kujue trailing behind.
Every piece of furniture inside was crafted of bamboo—plain yet charmingly rustic. Seated on a low bamboo chair, Qianli Xun found steaming bamboo leaf tea waiting on the side table.
The village head bowed politely: "Settle in at Old Ku's first, Proprietor—need anything at all, send Old Ku for me."
"Thank you for looking out for me, Village Head. Go tend your duties—I'll call if anything comes up."
"Got village business waiting, so I won't intrude." With a bow, the village head departed.
Once Kuqiao introduced her family, Qianli Xun had her bring out the silver bracelet and pastries, delighting the entire Ku household.
Old Ku asked, "How long will you be staying, Proprietor?"
"One month."
"A noble lady like you can't stay in a rundown shack like ours," Old Ku said. "Lucky we built a brand-new bamboo house for Kujue last year—nobody's lived in it yet! Sits right beside the eastern bamboo grove with a fine view. If you don't mind, stay there!"
Sounded like heaven to Qianli Xun—she agreed on the spot.
Kuqiao blinked: "Father, Mother—is Kujue getting married? Why build his house so early?"
"About time! Match is already sealed—his bride comes home next year," Old Ku beamed.
Seated on a stool nearby, Kujue turned bright red at mention of his wedding.
Kuqiao's mother turned to Qianli Xun: "Thank you for looking after Kuqiao, Proprietor—when it comes to her marriage someday, we'll have to trouble you too."
Kuqiao cut in fast: "Mother, quit talking nonsense! I'm never marrying—I'm staying by Miss's side forever!"
Qianli Xun laughed: "Don't worry, Auntie—I'll make sure she lands a wonderful husband!"
After chatting a while longer, Kuqiao went to help her mother steam bamboo tube rice and stir-fry rehydrated dried bamboo shoots from last year; in honor of the Proprietor, Kujue netted a fresh fish from the stream behind the village for soup, and everyone gathered around one table.
Simple compared to Lin Manor's banquets, the meal carried a crisp mountain freshness Qianli Xun loved.
After lunch, the Ku family helped haul her luggage into the new bamboo house. Once they left, Kuqiao got busy arranging everything to her mistress's taste.
Furniture was sparse, yet a low bamboo table faced the open doorway—perfect for the Four Treasures of the Study so Miss could gaze straight into the bamboo grove while writing! Since it sat low for floor seating, Kuqiao fetched a square cotton cushion from home—just the right height.
A low bamboo cabinet doubled as a tea table; draping patterned homespun cloth across it, Kuqiao lined up every tea tool.
Inside the bedroom stood a fresh bamboo bed; Kuqiao spread Kujue's brand-new quilts over it and hung her mistress's garments along a bamboo rack.
Thrilled with the cozy setup, Qianli Xun eyed the fine silks hanging on the rack—and her own fancy dress—feeling way too flashy for a rustic village.
"Kuqiao, lend me a couple sets of your clothes."
"Why wear my clothes, Miss?"
"Wearing these silks around the village makes me stick out like a sore thumb—yours fit right in."
"If you don't mind rough cloth, Miss—happened to pack two spare sets. Try one on."
Changing into Kuqiao's simple dress, Qianli Xun still couldn't hide her poise beneath plain cotton.
She pulled off her hair ornaments too and had Kuqiao stow them away.
Stepping to the doorway, she faced a lush wall of swaying green bamboo; a cool breeze carried crisp bamboo scent straight to her lungs. Living a month in a paradise like this was bound to turn her luck around.
#CH 30
#TITLE: Chapter 30: Drum Songs and a Run-In with the Dandy
Her first night in the countryside, Qianli Xun slept like a rock.
Waking at first light before sunrise, she rolled over to catch a bit more sleep—and fell straight into a dream: wearing a wedding gown, standing beside Shen Jianchen at their wedding surrounded by cheering guests.
Just as Shen Jianchen slipped the ring onto her finger, she jolted wide awake! Squeezing her eyes shut, she tried diving back in, yet sleep refused to return—the sweet dream gone like smoke.
Sitting up hollow-hearted, she murmured aloud: "Shen Jianchen, where are you? I actually want to fall in love now—why'd you hover around nonstop back when I didn't care, and vanish without a trace the second I do?"
Then sober reality crept in: wearing Zhu Shuzhen's face, even standing right in front of Shen Jianchen he'd never recognize her—and if he had transmigrated into another body too, she wouldn't recognize him either! Two souls who knew each other inside out, killed together in one quake, flung into the past unable to recognize each other—what crueler irony existed?
Up early, Kuqiao walked home to cook breakfast and returned carrying a bamboo basket. Hearing a sigh inside, she set the basket down and stepped into the bedroom.
"Awake, Miss? After breakfast I'll show you around the area—guaranteed to lift your spirits!"
Dressing in plain clothes, Qianli Xun had Kuqiao tie a simple bun with zero hairpins and zero makeup—blending right into village life.
Out front, Kuqiao cleared the writing tools and laid breakfast on the low table: steaming rice porridge scented with bamboo and a plate of stir-fried dried bamboo shoots with pork.
Eyeing the pork, Qianli Xun knew Kuqiao's family rarely ate meat—cooked special just for her.
"Kuqiao, make sure you pay your parents board money—don't let them spend out of pocket for me."
"Already paying them, Miss! Just worried simple country fare won't suit you."
"I'm easy—eat anything."
Looking out the doorway at towering emerald stalks thrusting skyward filled with raw vitality, she felt half the dream's ache dissolve. With a faint smile, she picked up her chopsticks and ate.
Halfway through breakfast, singing drifted over on the breeze punctuated by rhythmic drumbeats—too far to catch the lyrics.
"Somebody singing outside?"
"Oh, a drum-song performer! Travels village to village earning enough to get by."
Having read about Song drum songs, Qianli Xun perked up, polished off her bowl fast, and set her chopsticks down. "Leave the dishes for now, Kuqiao—let's go listen!"
Following the rhythm to a small clearing at the village entrance, they found a crowd already gathered around. Kuqiao spread her silk handkerchief over a flat rock. "Sit right here, Miss."
Sitting down, Qianli Xun watched the performer—a blind old man perched on a wooden stool, steadying a small drum in his left hand and tapping beats with a wooden stick in his right, weaving spoken narrative and song together. His patter flowed smooth as water, his melodies rang clear and moving—neither opera nor pop, an ancient vocal cadence she had never heard before.
Most mesmerizing was how a single drum could carry such gripping rhythm. Listening closely to the lyrics, she realized the blind drummer was performing the tale of Han scholar Cai Yong—passing first in the imperial exams, disowning his loyal wife, and marrying the chancellor's daughter!
Qianli Xun chuckled inside: Poor Cai Yong's name dragged straight through the mud—turned into a Song-dynasty Chen Shimei!
For a beat she considered clearing Cai Yong's historical name once the song ended—then thought better of it. Drum songs were entertainment, and fiction bent history all the time; how many plots had she invented herself as a web novelist? Small potatoes next to modern web fiction! Maybe Cai Yong's spirit up above didn't even mind giving common folk a good show.
Watching the villagers laugh, gasp, and sigh along, Qianli Xun had a lightbulb moment: classical ci poetry belonged to refined Song literati, whereas everyday people craved lively, down-to-earth storytelling!
Borrowing Zhu Shuzhen's verse for salons was fine, yet deep down those poems didn't belong to her.
An itch flared in her fingertips—she wanted to write her own stories in this era! Inspired by the blind drummer, she thought: Ordinary folks eat up popular tales—why not write stories for them here and put my own real talent to work?!
Mid-epiphany, a terrified girl's scream sliced the air: "Grandpa! Save me! Bad men are after me!"
The blind drummer froze; every head in the clearing whipped round.
Qianli Xun sprang up as a breathtaking young girl sprinted toward them—clad in patched rags that couldn't dim her striking beauty.
Gasping for air with four or five burly thugs pounding on her heels, she burst through the crowd and threw herself into the blind old man's arms: "Grandpa! They're trying to drag me away!"
Four thugs shoved straight into the ring.
One barked: "Hey, blind old man! Talk sense into your granddaughter and hand her over to Young Master Chen! Go with him and she eats like royalty—beats dragging your old bones around singing for scraps!"
Shielding his granddaughter, the blind man shook with rage: "I earn honest bread by my craft—better than selling my granddaughter for dirty coin! Dare snatch her in broad daylight? I'll drag you straight to the yamen!"
Villagers broke into angry murmurs: "Lawless bullies!"
"Poor grandpa and girl—leave them alone!"
"Nothing but local thugs preying on honest folk!"
Flexing thick arms and shaking fists with ugly snarls, the four thugs shut the crowd right up; half the villagers hurried home wanting no trouble, the rest backed far off.
Qianli Xun stood her ground without blinking, sizing up the thugs ready to flatten them the second they moved—and backed by her mistress, Kuqiao didn't flinch either.
One thug leered straight at them: "Hey, you two—why aren't you backing off? Pretty little face there—volunteering to go with Young Master Chen in her place? His carriage is right down the road, let me escort you!"
Qianli Xun snorted cold: "I was trying to hold back, and you had to walk right up asking for it—don't blame me for what happens next!"
Before the thug blinked, she drove a lightning punch square into his jaw—bruising his cheek black, splitting his lip, and sending him staggering over two paces back!
Wiping blood off his mouth, he roared: "Jump her together!"
All four surrounded Qianli Xun, swinging fists from every side to teach this meddling girl a lesson.
Leaping light off the ground, Qianli Xun unleashed a storm of crisp punches and kicks, weaving unpredictable Qianli Fist combos that dropped all four men flat onto the dirt in seconds!
Gritting his teeth, one thug scrambled back up charging full force; cool and steady, Qianli Xun met him with rapid strikes that battered him backward straight onto the ground again.
Villagers erupted in cheers: "Nice punch, Proprietor! Knock 'em flat! Brilliant!"
Glaring down at the four thugs groaning and clutching bruises as they crawled up, Qianli Xun snapped: "This is MY turf! Bully people on my turf and you're begging for a beating! Listen close—this old man and his granddaughter are under my protection now! Touch one hair on her head again, and see what my fists do!"
Outmatched, the four thugs muttered among themselves: "Let's tell Young Master Chen—let him handle her!"
"Strong as she is, Young Master Chen can't beat her either!"
"Still gotta report, or he'll skin us alive!"
"Right—let him see how fierce she is so he doesn't blame us!"
Just as they turned to flee, a familiar drawl floated over: "Took you four this long to grab one girl? Keeping me waiting!"
Glancing back, they saw Young Master Chen strolling up himself.
Limping over clutching aches, they whined: "Young Master Chen, that woman jumped out claiming this is her turf! She's a monster—look what she did to us!"
One glance and Qianli Xun recognized Chen Haoyu—talk about bad pennies turning up!
At first Chen Haoyu didn't recognize her in Kuqiao's plain cotton dress—until he spotted Kuqiao beside her and smirked: "How do you pop up everywhere? What's a noble lady doing slumming out here?"
Qianli Xun sneered right back: "Wondered which Young Master Chen—of course it's the one rat dropping ruining every pot of soup!"
Burning at being called a rat dropping yet knowing full well he stood zero chance against her fists, Chen Haoyu swallowed his rage.
"Out of respect for you, I'll spare the girl today. Hey, girl—don't think you got lucky! Stick with me and you'd live in luxury. Move out!"
A few steps away, he looked back with a venomous smirk: "Twice now, Zhu Shuzhen—pray we don't make it three! Your turf, huh? Wait till tomorrow and see whose turf it really is!" Turning on his heel, he whistled his way off with his four bruised thugs.
Hard to believe anyone walked around this arrogant!
Shaken, the blind drummer could perform no more; leaning on his granddaughter, he bowed low. "Thank you for saving us, young lady! Nothing to repay you with—if you enjoy drum songs, let me bring my granddaughter to your door someday for a private performance."
The girl dropped to her knees kowtowing: "Thank you for saving me, Elder Sister!"
Pulling her up, Qianli Xun asked, "What's your name?"
"Xiaguo."
"Pretty name!" Studying Xiaguo—a true natural beauty—pity stirred in Qianli Xun's chest. "How about coming to work for me?"
Overwhelmed, Xiaguo started kneeling again; Qianli Xun caught her arms. "Quit kneeling—I hate kneeling! Who else is in your family?"
"Grandmother and my parents."
"Good—taking you won't leave your grandfather without help."
She shot Kuqiao a nod; Kuqiao slipped one tael of silver into the blind old man's hands. "A reward from my Miss, Grandpa."
Feeling the heavy silver, the old man stammered: "Too much! Way too much!"
"I'm taking your granddaughter away from your side—one tael's barely enough! Keep it!"
"Giving Xiaguo a safe place beside you is already a blessing beyond words, Miss—how could I take your silver?"
Kuqiao jumped in playful: "Don't be polite, Grandpa—turn it down and Miss won't take Xiaguo as her maid!"
"Alright, alright—no more refusing!" the old man hurried to accept.
Qianli Xun told Xiaguo to escort her grandfather home first, settle things with her family, and report to the bamboo house afterward.
With the performance cut short, the villagers dispersed, leaving quiet behind at the village edge—just Qianli Xun and Kuqiao.
Silence rushing in right after excitement left a hollow ache inside. Never having loved once in her life, Qianli Xun felt an empty space craving warmth—where was the man meant to love her, the man she wanted to love? If she couldn't find him yet, better keep busy.
"Kuqiao, get me some huaben storybooks."
"What do you want those for, Miss?"
"Bored sitting around the bamboo house—something to pass the time."
"You never read lowbrow tales like that before, Miss!"
"Even you know 'lowbrow'! Truly a maid of Qian—uh, of Zhu Shuzhen! Remember this, Kuqiao: highbrow art or lowbrow tales, if ordinary folks love reading it, it's good stuff!"
"Alright, I'll have Kujue buy a few in town for you."
Qianli Xun sighed: "Pity I can't leave this bamboo grove for a month, or I'd hit town myself—visit teahouses and Goulan Wasi to hear live storytelling firsthand! Guess I'll have to trouble you and your brother."
"No trouble at all, Miss!"
Taking another stroll around the bamboo grove, Qianli Xun returned to her cottage.
Once her mistress was settled, Kuqiao went to give Kujue the book errand. On her way back, she bumped into Xiaguo asking directions to Miss's cottage, brought her right over, and walked her through every daily routine—which Xiaguo soaked up word for word.
#CH 31
#TITLE: Chapter 31: No Love for Sister, Plenty for Estates
Life in Prince Yi's Manor ran smooth and pampered for Shen Jianchen: Princess Jiahe and Matron Zou handled every detail, servants waited on his every breath, leaving nothing to fret over—nothing except the quiet alienation sitting in his chest.
Unfamiliar halls and faces could grow familiar in time; Song customs could be learned. Only Qiao Mengrong's devotion left an awkward wall between them that made him squirm.
Beautiful as Qiao Mengrong was, her adoring eyes sparked zero chemistry in him. Plain truth: Qiao Mengrong was Zhao Pu's type, not Shen Jianchen's. He loved Qianli Xun—loved her during the quake, after death, and right here in this new life.
If the Divine Willow's force had carried him across time, surely Qianli Xun had crossed too. Where was she? Would he ever find her in this foreign era?
Thinking of her chased sleep away. Sitting up in bed—already sliding into princely habits after a few days—he called out natural as breathing: "Time to dress."
Attendant maids hurried forward bearing robes.
Seated on the bedside, he let one maid slip on his shoes while another held out a washbasin, then handed him a soft towel to dry his face. Standing up, he let two more maids straighten his sash and robes.
Finished, he said, "Going out for a walk. Stay behind."
The maids halted at the doorway.
Stepping out solo into pre-dawn twilight, Shen Jianchen drifted back over memories of Qianli Xun.
They had met at a folk martial-arts exhibition where he represented Shen Fist against Qianli Fist—and Qianli Xun stepped into the ring for Qianli Fist.
One sparring match stole his heart. To chase her, he kept showing up at Qianli Fist Manor using "sparring" as an excuse, even when she met him with nonstop cold shoulders.
At first he figured she had a boyfriend; discovering she was single kept him grinning awake all night! Banking on sheer persistence wearing down stone, he stuck to her like glue, convinced she'd crack soon—right up until the earthquake struck. Without that quake, maybe that very night he'd have held her in his arms.
Gazing at the pale eastern sky brightening overhead, he felt his muscles itch. Back home he trained every dawn; busy adjusting since crossing over, he hadn't thrown a single punch. Spotting an open yard ahead, he stepped out and ran through a sequence of Shen Fist using Zhao Pu's body—fluid, crisp, and effortless.
Hearing from servants that Prince Yi was up at dawn practicing martial arts, Qiao Mengrong's personal maid Xilan hurried to wake her mistress with the astonishing news.
Dressing in haste, Qiao Mengrong rushed straight toward the courtyard.
Nearing the yard, she spotted clusters of maids and servants peeking from afar and whispering.
She gave Xilan a sharp glance.
Catching the signal, Xilan marched over and snapped: "What are you all loitering around for? Get back to work! Plenty of chores waiting across the manor this morning!"
The servants scattered at once.
Stepping closer to watch Prince Yi flow through crisp, powerful strikes, Qiao Mengrong felt a chill of strangeness. Since when did her brother know martial arts? Maybe he really wasn't her brother...
Tears welled in her eyes—had her real brother truly left her forever?
Seconds later she pushed the dread down: identical face, identical frame—who else could he be? She had watched him wake with her own eyes; nothing uncanny had happened. This martial prowess had to be Heaven's blessing bestowed on his recovery!
Comforted by her own logic, she blotted away the tear slipping down her cheek with a silk handkerchief.
"Brother!"
At her voice, Shen Jianchen halted his form and turned. "Mengrong? Up this early?"
"Weren't you up early too, Brother?"
"Couldn't sleep."
Stepping right up beside him, Qiao Mengrong beamed: "What marvelous boxing, Brother! Even knowing nothing of martial arts, I see how much you've changed since waking up—Heaven took pity and gifted you such strength! Now with Brother protecting me, nobody will ever dare bully me!"
Saying so, she leaned into his chest and wrapped both arms around his waist, eyes shutting in bliss as she drank in the feeling of him standing tall like a sturdy tree shielding her from every storm.
She and Zhao Pu had embraced before, always with him propped frail in bed—never pressing her whole body against a solid, upright chest like this, never daring hug tight lest she strain his weak lungs, never really hearing his pulse.
Now, feeling his heartbeat quicken beneath her ear, she tightened her arms.
Watching from nearby, Xilan blushed and smiled in delight.
Ambush-hugged out of nowhere, Shen Jianchen froze, his heart kicking faster by pure reflex.
Catching himself fast—hateful to wound the girl Zhao Pu loved, yet refusing to wrap arms around her like a lover—he searched for an exit line: "Mengrong, haven't had breakfast yet—I'm starving."
Though disappointed he hadn't closed his arms around her, this was their first real embrace since he came back from death.
Forgetting their love through amnesia made shyness natural—what counted was he hadn't shoved her away! Not pushing her away meant he didn't dislike her, and that was a hopeful start.
Releasing him with a warm smile, Qiao Mengrong said, "The kitchen is still making breakfast, Brother. If you're hungry, come back inside for some pastries to tide you over."
They walked back side by side in silence. For Qiao Mengrong, simply walking beside him without a single word felt like paradise.
Shen Jianchen's mind stayed miles away—missing Qianli Xun while agonizing over how to handle Qiao Mengrong's devotion.
What a crazy twist: hadn't even won Qianli Xun yet, got flung into another century, and landed straight inside someone else's romance!
Accept or reject? Reject her, and her melting, devoted gaze tore at his conscience; accept her, and living a lie—holding or marrying a girl he didn't love—betrayed them both! Worst of all, his heart still belonged to Qianli Xun, and a deep gut hunch told him they would cross paths again in this era.
Inside the hall, Qiao Mengrong had maids bring pastries.
Sitting at the table, Shen Jianchen bit into one—melting sweet on his tongue without being cloying—and polished off the whole piece. Glancing around at the ring of young maids hovering nearby, he frowned; being fussed over all day by delicate girls felt deeply awkward.
As heir to Shen Fist, he had enforced one strict rule: male apprentices only—convinced delicate girls weren't cut out for hard boxing. Outside corporate meetings, a pack of male disciples followed him everywhere; surrounded by maids here made his skin itch. Turning to Qiao Mengrong, he asked, "Why are all my personal attendants maids? Don't we have guards or anything?"
"Prince Yi's Manor keeps guards for security, Brother, yet they're clumsy for daily care," Qiao Mengrong replied. "When you were frail, you needed gentle, careful hands—maids suited best, and I handpicked every single one."
Touched by her endless care for Zhao Pu, Shen Jianchen murmured, "You went to a lot of trouble."
"Never speak like an outsider with me, Brother—we're family."
Still piecing together both the manor and this era, he pressed on: "Now that I'm healthy, I don't need maids crowding around me all day. I want a personal guard—do any of the manor's guards know real martial arts? Or are there skilled martial artists out in the city?"
Brother really has changed, Qiao Mengrong thought with a quiet sigh, answering plain: "I don't pay much attention to such matters. You're master of this manor—summon the guards and see for yourself. As for outside martial heroes, ask Young Master Qiao once he gets back."
"Who's Young Master Qiao?"
"Consort Qiao's younger brother—only two when she gave birth to you, nearly your own age! Over half a year ago he came up from Lin'an to Bianliang and stayed here at Prince Yi's Manor. A month back a friend invited him to visit Laizhou, so he missed your death and recovery—he should be back any day now. Scholar though he is, he travels wide and knows all sorts of people; he'll surely know where martial heroes gather."
An uncle practically his own age—Shen Jianchen smiled wryly. "Alright, I'll ask him when he returns. Wait—one question I've been meaning to ask..."
"Go ahead, Brother."
"Why is your surname Qiao too?"
"My birth surname was Mu—Mu Mengrong. After you picked me up and took me in as your sister, to honor Consort Qiao—and because you loved the lines from the Book of Songs: 'Chop, chop go the axes on the trees; chirping, chirping sing the birds; leaving the dark valley, they rise onto the lofty Qiao tree' [TN: Translator's rendering]—you gave me the surname Qiao."
"Got it."
Just then a maid entered: "Your Highness, Princess—Matron Zou asks whether you wish to take breakfast in the dining hall or right here?"
"Your choice, Brother," Qiao Mengrong deferred.
"Right here," Shen Jianchen tossed out.
"Right away!" Stepping out, the maid sent others moving two small round tables and chairs into position.
Moments later Matron Zou walked in followed by three maids bearing trays—one placed breakfast before Shen Jianchen, the other two set the small round tables where Matron Zou and Qiao Mengrong took their seats while maids stood ready.
"Please eat, Your Highness—tell us if anything doesn't suit your palate," Matron Zou beamed. "We knew your old sickbed diet by heart; now that Heaven restored your health, we almost don't know how to spoil you!"
"Whatever you and Mengrong eat works for me—don't make anything special. I'm not picky."
"Not picky" made Matron Zou and Qiao Mengrong trade startled glances—as if silently confirming: He isn't the old Prince Yi.
Catching their dismay, Shen Jianchen smoothed it over fast: "Healthy now means everything tastes good—of course I'm not picky!"
Reassured, both smiled and tucked into breakfast.
Chewing away, a practical question hit Shen Jianchen: Feeding this massive household three lavish meals a day must cost a fortune—and since arriving he hadn't worked a day! Where did the money come from?
Looking over at Matron Zou, he asked, "Matron, since I forgot everything—where does the money to run Prince Yi's Manor come from?"
Setting down her spoon, Matron Zou explained: "Though illness kept Your Highness from holding official posts, His Majesty pitied you and kept your princely stipend flowing in full. As an imperial son, you also own vast estates and storefronts managed by stewards bringing in yearly profits—plus Consort Qiao's dowry from the Qiao family and imperial gifts from His Majesty, all landed estates and shops spread across Bianliang, Lin'an, and beyond. Less than your imperial brothers hold, perhaps, yet more than enough to keep Prince Yi's Manor thriving!"
Delight lit Shen Jianchen's face—landed wealth handed straight to him! Zero stress over payrolls now!
#CH 32
#TITLE: Chapter 32: Strolling, Fortune-Telling, and a Young Thief
Roughly fifty guards served Prince Yi's Manor. Summoned by the prince, they lined up crisp and orderly in the main courtyard awaiting orders—first time in memory His Highness had ever called roll, leaving every man eager to see their healthy, revived prince up close.
Stepping before the ranks, Shen Jianchen felt his spirits lift—surrounded by sturdy, masculine energy at last, bringing back a taste of his old world!
"Who commands you day to day?"
One guard stepped forward crisp: "Reporting to Your Highness—I am Captain of the Guard Duan Hui, in charge of scheduling patrols to protect the manor."
"Know your men well?"
"Reporting to Your Highness—I know every mole on every man's back!"
"Good. Then tell me: do any of them know real martial arts?"
Duan Hui weighed it a second. "Rest easy, Your Highness—every man can swing a saber and handle rough brawling."
Pleased, Shen Jianchen nodded: "Good. Step back in line."
Once Duan Hui rejoined the ranks, Shen Jianchen barked: "Everyone—spread out one arm's length front, back, left, and right!"
The guards shifted smartly into spacing.
"Horse stance!"
Fifty men sank into horse stances at once.
"Clench fists! Alternate punches straight ahead! Left! Right!..."
Calling cadence as he paced down the rows, Shen Jianchen scrutinized every guard—hunting for a personal bodyguard with martial roots and, above all, prime natural aptitude he could train in Shen Fist.
Watching from afar, Qiao Mengrong listened to his booming voice drill the men, energy bursting from every stride.
Memories surfaced unbidden...
One sunny afternoon, bedridden Zhao Pu had wished to sit in the sun. Qiao Mengrong had guards haul out a long couch piled with thick cushions; assisted by a maid, she helped him sit outdoors. Barely seated, breathless already, he leaned weak against her shoulder and whispered: "Mengrong... once I'm gone, promise you'll marry a good man."
"No! I'll stay and guard Prince Yi's Manor for you!"
"Silly girl—what use is guarding an empty manor once I'm gone? I've already written my will: half the estate goes to you, half to Matron Zou..."
Those few sentences drained his breath. Back then she had wished with all her soul she could pour her own strength into him just so he could talk longer—yet all she could do was slip her arms gentle around his frail frame, dreading the moment he stopped breathing.
Gazing now at the strapping man before her, transformed into everything she had prayed for, she longed for next Mid-Autumn Festival to race closer—done being his sister, eager to become his wife!
Having inspected all fifty-plus guards, Shen Jianchen found plenty of brawny men good with sabers and brawls—fine for guarding gates, yet not a single gem with top-tier martial bones.
"Dismissed for today. Back to your posts."
Snapping back into tight formation at his command, the men stood ready.
Duan Hui stepped out: "Carry on with morning patrols—guard the manor tight! Dismissed!"
The guards dispersed.
Duan Hui bowed low: "Anything Your Highness needs, just give the word—through fire and boiling water, I won't blink!"
Pleased by his loyalty, Shen Jianchen nodded. "I'll call when I need you. Go tend your duties."
Once Duan Hui left, Shen Jianchen felt cabin fever creeping in—cooped inside the manor without seeing Bianliang outside? Time for a stroll! Barely halfway to the front gate, Qiao Mengrong called out: "Brother! Where are you headed?"
Turning, he smiled: "Ah, Mengrong—just heading out for a walk."
"I'll come with you, Brother!"
"No carriage—walking on foot, real tiring. Better stay home."
"I don't mind getting tired! Growing up, you never once strolled the streets with me—I've dreamed of this day forever!"
Melting under her eager, adoring eyes, he couldn't say no. "Alright, let's go together."
Lighting up with a breezy smile, she stepped over and linked her hand in his. "Let's go!"
Xilan ordered a carriage right behind them—walking or not, royal habit kept a carriage trailing in case either tired.
Uncomfortable holding hands yet loath to hurt her feelings, Shen Jianchen let her pull him out past the manor gates.
Once they left the quiet lanes and hit bustling market avenues, he spoke up: "Mengrong, hard to walk through crowds holding hands—mind letting go?"
She slipped her hand free at once. "If it bothers you, Brother, I won't hold on—whatever makes you happy."
Guilt pricked Shen Jianchen—why couldn't he fall for a girl this sweet? Life would be so simple if he could!
Bianliang's dazzling bustle soon swept guilt aside, grabbing every ounce of his attention.
Crowds surged past clamoring vendors, pulsing with raw energy rivaling any modern metropolis he had known! Roadside stalls overflowed with vivid silks, delicate porcelain, fragrant teas, fresh fruits and vegetables.
Every walk of life streamed by: scholars in blue robes and white sashes strolling with small baskets; folks sampling street snacks or watching jugglers and buskers; peddlers shouting wares to catch eyes; grandmothers towing kids while haggling and gossiping with neighbors.
Hawkers' cries, vendors' calls, whinnying carriage horses, and cheerful chatter blended into a lively chorus of city life, showing Shen Jianchen the vibrant heartbeat of Song-dynasty Bianliang.
Spotting a shop sign reading "Foot Shop" (Jiaodian), he chuckled: "Whoa—even the Song dynasty had foot-massage parlors!"
"Foot massage?" Qiao Mengrong blinked blankly.
Pointing at the signboard, he grinned: "Look right there—Foot Shop!"
Glancing over, Qiao Mengrong burst into laughter. "That isn't a foot-massage parlor! It's a little tavern where travelers pause to rest their feet—Bianliang is too big to walk in a day, so tired shoppers drop into a Foot Shop for a breather!"
Red-faced at his own blunder, Shen Jianchen muttered, "Turns out Song cities were pretty thoughtful!"
Still puzzling over his phrasing, she heard him ask next: "And what do shops marked 'Heaven's' and 'Beautiful Boon' sell?"
"Wine taverns," Qiao Mengrong explained.
"Why not just write 'Tavern' instead of fancy riddles nobody gets?" Pretentious ancients—what did "Heaven's Beautiful Boon" have to do with booze?
"You told me yourself before, Brother!" Qiao Mengrong smiled. "The Book of Han says, 'Wine is Heaven's beautiful boon'—praising fine liquor as a gift from Heaven."
Hit again by how thin his classical education was, Shen Jianchen laughed awkwardly: "I told you that? Amnesia wiped everything clean—guess I ought to dust off my old books!"
"You really should, Brother! His Majesty prizes men of learning—especially masters of poetry, calligraphy, and painting. While you were sick, he left you be; now that you're healthy, you're bound to face him sooner or later. Reading more can only help."
Clearly his little sister still hoped he'd win imperial favor. Shen Jianchen couldn't care less about favor or court audiences—with estates paying his bills, he was set for life! Only one question mattered: could he find Qianli Xun?
Strolling along lost in thought, he wandered beneath a massive shade tree.
Underneath stood a canvas awning sheltering a fortune-teller's stall, three paper banners hanging overhead: "Divine Divination," "Destiny Reading," and "Doubt Resolution"—full service!
Eager to ask whether he'd ever find Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen eyed the long line of townsfolk waiting—could take hours! He started turning away.
Catching his look, Qiao Mengrong signaled Xilan with her eyes.
Stepping right up, Xilan laid two taels of silver smack in front of the old fortune-teller.
Scooping up the silver with a wide grin, the old man waved aside the customer mid-reading: "Step over there a moment, friend—honored guests first!"
Grumbling, the displaced man shuffled aside.
"Since something's weighing on your mind, Brother, go ask!" Qiao Mengrong drew him into the seat before the table.
"What does the young lord wish to ask?" the fortune-teller beamed.
"Doubt Resolution."
"Write down any single character for me to read."
Pausing a beat, Shen Jianchen picked up the brush and wrote "Xun" (寻)—having never practiced calligraphy, the strokes looked atrocious.
A pang twisted Qiao Mengrong's heart—seldom as her brother wrote before, his hand had been far cleaner than this! How could brushwork this rough ever impress His Majesty?
Spinning the sheet around and squinting hard, the old man asked, "Did the young lord write the character 'Xun' (寻, meaning 'to seek')?"
Realizing he had written the modern simplified form, Shen Jianchen nodded sheepishly: "Yes."
Lifting the brush, the old man rewrote it in full traditional form (尋): "Ought to be written like this."
Eyeing the dense traditional character, Shen Jianchen admitted he barely recognized it.
"Asking about finding someone?" The old man set down his brush, hitting the bull's-eye first shot.
"Yes—finding someone! Can I find her?" Shen Jianchen leaned in eager.
Studying the strokes a beat, the old man spoke: "The top radical of 'Xun' (尋) comes from 'Gui' (歸, 'return'), stripped of all its remaining strokes—meaning the place you came from is closed to you forever; you can never return..."
Bull's-eye again! Having crossed centuries, going back was impossible.
"As for your search: 'Gong' (工, 'effort') in the middle means it will take real work; 'Kou' (口, 'mouth') shows you will encounter the one you seek at a place linked to the mouth—a restaurant, a wine tavern, or a hall where verse is recited..."
Joy surged in Shen Jianchen's chest—Qianli Xun really had crossed over too, and their paths would meet!
"At the bottom sits 'Cun' (寸, 'inch')—meaning even standing inches apart, you two won't recognize each other at first..."
"No problem—long as I find her! Can you tell which direction to look?"
Listening beside him, Qiao Mengrong frowned in puzzlement—who on earth was Brother hunting for? Why had he never breathed a word of this person?
"Judging by the orientation of my stall, the character you wrote faces straight south. Unless I miss my mark, the one you seek lies to the south!"
Light flooded Shen Jianchen's mind! Rising with cupped hands, he thanked him: "Much obliged for clearing my doubts, Old Sir! Xilan, hand him one more tael as an extra tip!"
Xilan produced another tael; beaming wide, the old man rose to accept it with profuse bows: "Thank you, young lord! Thank you! May you soon reunite with the one you long for!" Sitting back down, he called the next patron.
Walking away beside her smiling brother, curiosity gnawed at Qiao Mengrong.
Unable to hold back, she asked: "Brother, who are you looking for?"
Caught on the spot—tell her the truth and she'd dismiss it or ache realizing his heart belonged to another! Improvising fast, he shrugged: "An old friend I lost touch with ages ago—figured I'd never find them, so I asked the old man if our paths would cross."
Bedridden all his life, who did Zhao Pu ever call friend aside from Qiao Wenxian? Pain flickered in Qiao Mengrong's chest—her brother had started lying to her.
"Didn't you say you forgot everything, Brother? How do you remember an old friend?"
Spinning faster, he said, "Just spotting that stall flashed a blurry face in my head—got curious and figured I'd test if the old fortune-teller was any good!"
"And? Was he accurate?"
"Pretty solid—why else tip an extra tael?"
Right then frantic shouts broke through the crowd: "Stop that little thief! He stole my money pouch!"
Turning toward the clamor, Shen Jianchen saw a middle-aged man pushing through the throng yelling, "Catch the thief! My money pouch!"
A lithe young figure darted straight past them like a streak of wind.
Great martial bones! Shen Jianchen thought in a flash, barking to Xilan: "Take Princess Jiahe into a Foot Shop to rest—I'm catching that thief!"
Whoosh—he shot forward and vanished into the crowd before Qiao Mengrong's eyes.
Left standing with a hollow sigh, Qiao Mengrong murmured, "Let's wait for Brother at the Foot Shop."
#CH 33
#TITLE: Chapter 33: Picking an Apprentice, Meeting Qiao Wenxian
Years of Shen Fist had honed both his hands and his footwork; Shen Jianchen tore through the crowded street at blazing speed, closing fast on the pickpocket.
Ducking into a quiet lane and then a dead-end alley, the boy ran himself ragged—glancing back to see his pursuer cruising effortlessly without breaking a sweat, he gave up, doubled over gasping: "Stop... stop chasing... I quit... here—take the pouch!"
Halting calm without a heavy breath, Shen Jianchen took the pouch and sized up the kid: fifteen or sixteen, ragged clothes, smudged face, yet sharp, handsome features underneath. "Pickpocket, huh?"
"Who... who's a thief?! Don't slander me!"
"Not a thief? Then why steal his pouch?"
"Didn't steal—I borrowed!"
Cheeky kid! Shen Jianchen smirked: "Borrowed? Tell that to the guy screaming 'thief' down the whole street! If grabbing and running isn't stealing, what is?"
Plopping straight onto the dirt unfazed, the lad shot back: "Believe me or not—I'll sit right here till he catches up and you can ask him yourself!"
With the owner not here yet—and no way the boy could slip past him—Shen Jianchen asked, "What's your name?"
"Why should I tell you?"
"Because I outran you."
"Hmph—fast legs, I'll give you that! Fair enough: Bai Feng."
"Bai Feng—nice name. Stand up and drop into a horse stance so I can see."
"Weird guy! Why should I squat in a horse stance for you? No way!"
