Reaching down, Shen Jianchen yanked Bai Feng straight up, pressed one palm down on his shoulder, tapped both legs apart with his foot into a textbook horse stance, and tapped an acupoint locking him motionless—stepping back to inspect his frame with rising glee: Top-grade martial bones! Worth every step of today's stroll—found myself a prime apprentice!
Fuming, Bai Feng yelled: "What's wrong with you?! I already handed over the pouch—why freeze me like a statue?! Let me go!"
"Punishment for swiping someone's pouch! When the owner gets here, if he says let you go, I let you go."
Right then the middle-aged man stumbled into the alley, wheezing hard: "Thank... thank you, young master... for catching him..."
Handing over the pouch, Shen Jianchen watched the man collapse onto the ground catching his breath before scolding: "Bai Feng, you rascal! If you want to borrow money, ask proper—why snatch my whole pouch and run?! If not for this gentleman, you'd have wiped me out!"
"You two know each other?" Shen Jianchen asked.
"Hear that?!" Bai Feng crowed. "Not stealing—taking! Better yet, borrowing!"
Shen Jianchen snapped sharp: "Taking or borrowing without permission IS stealing!"
Silenced, Bai Feng glared in stubborn defiance.
Catching his breath and climbing up, the man explained: "I know this boy, young master. He asked to borrow coin; when I opened my pouch to count some out, he grabbed the whole bag and bolted."
Bai Feng shot back bitter: "Hmph! Five hundred wen wasn't nearly enough! My mother needs five taels of silver to save her life, and you only offered five hundred copper coins! Sure I grabbed your bag—I swore I'd earn it back and repay you! Stingy miser!"
"My coin doesn't blow in on the wind either!" the man argued. "I sweat for every copper to feed my whole family—lend you all of it and what do my wife and kids eat?!"
"My mother's dying at Grand Physician Zhao's Clinic right now!" Tears burst down Bai Feng's grimy cheeks: "Sob...! She's gravely ill—the doctor wants five taels to treat her, or she'll die! Sob...!"
Softening with pity, the man fished one tael from his pouch and held it out: "This is all I can spare—hope you understand."
Frozen by the acupoint, Bai Feng watched Shen Jianchen catch the silver and press it right back into the man's palm: "Keep your silver. I'll pay for Bai Feng's mother's treatment."
Relieved, the man beamed as he tucked his coin away: "Bless you, bless you! You met a true savior today, Bai Feng! Got chores at home—I'm off!"
Once the man left, Shen Jianchen tapped Bai Feng's acupoint free. "My silver isn't free—from now on, you work for me."
Shaking out stiff legs from holding that horse stance, Bai Feng asked wary: "Why should I follow you?"
"For one tael of silver every month."
Doing quick math—a sweet deal!—Bai Feng grinned: "Deal! Hand over the five taels now!" He stuck out an open palm.
Cashless himself, Shen Jianchen said, "My maid holds the silver—come back to the Foot Shop with me."
Leaving the quiet alley for the noisy street, they threaded through crowds back toward where he had left Mengrong, spotted Prince Yi's carriage parked outside the nearest Foot Shop, and stepped inside.
Sure enough, weary shoppers sat resting over tea and light wine—not free, of course—while waiters bustled refilling cups.
Spotting Qiao Mengrong seated at a corner table beside a tea bowl with Xilan standing watch, they walked over.
Rising to greet him, Qiao Mengrong smiled: "Back at last, Brother—you kept me waiting!" Eyeing the scruffy boy behind him, she asked, "Who is he?"
Without answering, Shen Jianchen told Xilan: "Give him five taels of silver."
Reluctant yet bound by royal command, Xilan handed over a pouch holding five taels: "Here! Take it!"
Clutching the pouch, Bai Feng beamed with gratitude: "Thank you, young master—my mother's saved!"
He spun to dash off, only for Shen Jianchen to catch his collar and murmur low in his ear: "Remember—once your mother's settled, report to Prince Yi's Manor and ask for me. Break your word, and I'll dig three feet underground to drag you back!"
Half warm, half menacing—and "Prince Yi's Manor" nearly floored Bai Feng! Holy cow—is this guy Captain of the Guard at Prince Yi's Manor?! Hit the jackpot today—wants to hire me as a manor guard! Beaming wide, he swore: "No need to dig—why would I pass up a gig like this? Soon as Mom's treated, I'm coming straight to Prince Yi's Manor!"
Shen Jianchen let go. "Go on!"
Bai Feng bolted straight out the door toward Grand Physician Zhao's Clinic.
Doubts cleared and a prime apprentice recruited—today's walk had paid off big. Ready to wrap up.
Figuring Mengrong's feet must ache after hours walking—plus Prince Yi's Manor sat way out in the suburbs—Shen Jianchen suggested riding the carriage home.
Delighted—clearly healthy enough to walk back effortlessly, he only took the carriage for her sake!—Qiao Mengrong glowed inside.
Aboard the carriage, she sat close beside him and leaned her head onto his shoulder. Before, frail Zhao Pu had always leaned on her; flipping roles now filled her with bliss as she rested against his newly solid shoulder.
Half raising a hand to nudge her upright, Shen Jianchen paused midair and dropped his arm—fine, let a little sister lean on her brother's shoulder, no harm done.
Thoughts drifted straight back to Qianli Xun and the fortune-teller's warning: "Even standing inches apart, you won't recognize each other..." Worry about recognition later—find her first! Head south, the old man said—yet the south was huge! Where on earth to start?
Turning the puzzle over all the way back to Prince Yi's Manor, he stepped through the gate to a waiting maid: "Your Highness, Princess—Young Master Qiao has returned from Laizhou!"
"Understood," Qiao Mengrong said. "Where is he? I'll take Brother to meet him."
"Waiting in the reception hall, Your Highness."
"Mengrong, what did I call Young Master Qiao before?" Shen Jianchen asked.
"His name is Qiao Wenxian, courtesy name Wenyuan—you always called him Wenyuan."
Whew—long as I don't have to call a guy my own age Uncle!
Entering the reception hall, they found a young man in crisp white robes who rose and bowed at once: "Prince Yi. Princess Jiahe."
Shen Jianchen practically froze in awe.
Face fair as carved jade, clear smooth skin glowing with health; deep, bright eyes sparkling with quiet intellect; straight nose, clean lips curving into an effortless confident smile; tall, lean, upright carriage that turned heads on sight.
Two idioms popped straight into Shen Jianchen's head: elegant and refined—handsome as Pan An!
"Heard news about you the moment I entered Bianliang and rushed straight back to see for myself! Truly cured—Sister's spirit in Heaven must be watching over you!" Clasping Prince Yi's hands to look him over, Qiao Wenxian beamed: "Shedding that frail shell brought out pure manly vigor—what a blessing!"
Pulling Prince Yi into a hearty hug, he added: "Solid build too—steady as Mount Tai! Marvelous!"
Warmth surged in Shen Jianchen's chest; having a real friend and kinsman in this strange century felt amazing. Wrapping his arms around Qiao Wenxian in return, he grinned: "Wenyuan, illness is gone, yet my memory's blank—if Mengrong hadn't told me, I wouldn't even know what to call you."
Letting go, Qiao Wenxian waved it off: "Forget what's gone—health matters most! Spent all those years stuck in bed barely leaving the manor anyway; nothing worth remembering except that one trip outside as a boy when you picked up a little sister! Toss the rest!"
"He forgot that too, though Matron Zou and I filled him in," Qiao Mengrong smiled.
"Good, good! Quit standing by the doorway—sit and talk!" Qiao Wenxian guided him in.
Qiao Mengrong motioned Xilan to fetch tea at once.
Once all three sat, Qiao Wenxian pitched an idea: "Now that you're healthy, Prince Yi, why not come down to Lin'an with me to meet your mother's family? They'll be overjoyed seeing you like this!"
"Lin'an? Is that to the south?" Shen Jianchen sat straight up.
"Straight south! Gorgeous scenery—come along and I'll walk you around West Lake, guaranteed worth the trip!"
"Deal! I'm going to Lin'an with you!" Just wondering where down south to search for Qianli Xun, and Qiao Wenxian drops Lin'an straight into his lap! Had to be Heaven guiding his steps.
Listening beside him, Qiao Mengrong recalled the fortune-teller's words—and seeing the eager spark light her brother's eyes sent a chill down her spine: Who is Brother chasing down south?!
A maid served fresh tea and withdrew. Composing herself, Qiao Mengrong asked, "Heading home to Lin'an for good, Young Master Qiao?"
"About time—been away nearly a year, plus someone I love lives there. High time I went back to see her."
"Oh? Young Master Qiao already has someone in his heart—how many adoring ladies will weep at that!"
"Only sorrow is—she's already married."
"If she's wed, better to let go, Young Master Qiao."
"You don't know, Princess Jiahe—her husband shares none of her soul and beats her all the time! Never saw her wounds with my own eyes, yet reading my friend's letters tears my heart out! I'm going back to urge her into mutual divorce."
"Bold indeed, Young Master Qiao—I admire your nerve."
Having no background on Wenyuan's love life, Shen Jianchen just listened quietly.
Just then a guard stepped in: "Your Highness, two letters just arrived from Lin'an for Young Master Qiao."
A maid took them and passed them to Qiao Wenxian.
Tearing open the first, Qiao Wenxian flared in fury: "Just talking about her husband beating her, and here's another letter saying he did it again! No way I let her suffer another day in that living hell!"
Watching his intense reaction, Qiao Mengrong wondered what kind of married woman held Qiao Wenxian this enthralled.
Reading further, relief lit his brow: "Letter says she's filing for mutual divorce—right in line with my plan! I'm leaving for Lin'an first thing tomorrow morning; get back early and maybe I can help!"
Opening the second envelope, he unfolded a large sheet holding a handwritten lyric—a single drop of black ink staining the character "you" (伊). Reading silently, he murmured: "'Through farewell tears, I gaze at green hills... Yet waking finds sorrow and resentment waiting just as before...' How deep must her anguish run to write lines like these?! Forget tomorrow—I'm leaving for Lin'an right now!"
Shen Jianchen sprang up too: "I'll pack right now and ride with you, Wenyuan!"
Qiao Mengrong jumped up: "I'm coming with you, Brother!"
Just as all three locked plans, a guard entered to announce: "Your Highness, an Imperial Attendant from the palace awaits outside."
All three stepped out to meet a lone eunuch—not the one from before.
Bowing low, the eunuch beamed: "Prince Yi truly looks the picture of health—and Young Master Qiao is here too, wonderful! I bring His Majesty's invitation summoning both of you to a palace banquet tomorrow."
Opening the card for a quick glance, Shen Jianchen cursed the timing inwardly—wrecking his immediate departure for Lin'an!
Still, knowing enough imperial etiquette never to refuse the emperor, he inclined his head: "Inform Imperial Father that Wenyuan and I will attend on time tomorrow."
#CH 34
#TITLE: Chapter 34: Asking After His Majesty, Meeting Treacherous Officials
Once the eunuch left, Shen Jianchen stared at the invitation feeling annoyed—never favored Prince Yi before, why summon him now and stall the trip to Lin'an?
Funny how he had changed: back as a CEO, he loved rubbing elbows with powerful, wealthy elites; now he couldn't care less and found court politics a headache. Guess dying once really showed how hollow cash and status were.
On the bright side, his new apprentice Bai Feng hadn't shown up yet—staying one extra day gave the kid time to report in.
Qiao Mengrong beamed with pride: "His Majesty finally invited you, Brother! Don't be nervous—he's never met you before and doesn't know your old self, so just be natural and relaxed!"
Shen Jianchen gave her a warm smile for her thoughtfulness.
Turning to Qiao Wenxian, Qiao Mengrong asked, "Young Master Qiao attends palace banquets all the time and knows His Majesty's tastes—please brief Brother so he knows what to expect."
"At your command, Princess," Qiao Wenxian smiled, rattling off details with ease. "His Majesty excels at calligraphy, painting, poetry, and lyrics alike—he adores scholarly elegance and throws banquets constantly, inviting both high ministers and noted literati. Dabbling a bit in art critique myself, somehow my modest name reached court; while living in Bianliang, I get invited to nearly every palace gathering."
"Too modest, Young Master Qiao!" Qiao Mengrong praised. "You're already celebrated across Jiangnan, and now your fame spreads through Bianliang too! Speaking of Jiangnan's rich talent, there's a female ci poet in Lin'an named Zhu Shuzhen—I've read several of her lyrics, truly brilliant! Have you ever met her?"
Qiao Wenxian let out a soft sigh. "She is the very woman I love."
Qiao Mengrong sighed in echo. "So it's her..."
Literature had never been Shen Jianchen's strong suit—his exposure stopped at high-school Chinese class; respectable grades back then, sure, yet professional literary critique terrified him.
Only because Qianli Xun wrote web novels had he forced himself to scroll through her books on his phone—just to find shared topics beyond boxing while wooing her!
Driven by love, he had slogged through every single novel she published, digesting them enough to toss out decent comments on web fiction.
Classical poetry and ink painting? Total blank! Showing up at an emperor's artsy banquet risked embarrassing Zhao Pu flat.
Plan set fast: keep a low profile, blend into the crowd—with dozens of guests and zero imperial favor, His Majesty wouldn't spare him a glance.
Spotting worry on his face, Qiao Wenxian reassured him: "Don't sweat it, Prince Yi—stick close to me, anything unfamiliar I'll whisper in your ear."
"With Young Master Qiao looking out for you, Brother, nothing to worry about," Qiao Mengrong added.
"Much obliged, Wenyuan," Shen Jianchen thanked him.
Next day, riding the carriage toward the palace with Qiao Wenxian, Shen Jianchen figured he ought to learn basic facts about the father he was about to meet: "Wenyuan, what's my father's name?"
Leaning close, Qiao Wenxian whispered: "One shouldn't speak the sovereign's personal name aloud—since you asked, quietly: His Majesty is Zhao Ji."
Rusty on reign titles like Xuanhe he might be, yet emperor names from school history still stuck—Zhao Ji was Emperor Huizong of Song!
One of history's most notorious incompetent emperors—elevating treacherous scoundrels like Cai Jing, Wang Fu, Tong Guan, Gao Qiu, Zhu Mian, and Liang Shicheng to bleed the people dry in luxury, sparking the whole Water Margin saga of the hundred and eight heroes driven to Mount Liang!
Mixed feelings washed over Shen Jianchen: bummed to wake up son to an incompetent emperor, yet oddly relaxed—zero respect meant zero nerves! Who cared if he slipped up in front of Huizong? History remembered Huizong's blunders, not some obscure thirteenth prince's gaffes!
Puzzled, Qiao Wenxian asked, "You look way more relaxed hearing His Majesty's name—why?"
Skipping the question, Shen Jianchen asked instead: "What do you honestly think of my father—and how he rules this empire?"
"Subjects shouldn't gossip about court in private—yet since you ask, let me speak plain."
Lowering his voice further, Qiao Wenxian leaned close: "His Majesty is a towering master of calligraphy and painting—calling him gifted is no exaggeration. On the surface, the Great Song looks prosperous and peaceful, townsfolk live fairly well—just look at Bianliang's booming streets! Someday I'll take you out to see the night markets—dazzling..."
Cutting right in, Shen Jianchen pressed: "On the surface—what about underneath?"
Dropping to a bare murmur, Qiao Wenxian said, "Hidden perils everywhere. Up north the Jurchens of Jin eye us like tigers; inside, rebellions flare. Last year Huainan bandits rose up until Prefect Zhang Shuye of Haizhou defeated them and captured their deputy leader, forcing their surrender. Know who led those Huainan rebels?"
Shen Jianchen shook his head.
"A rural clerk named Song Jiang."
Eyes lighting up, Shen Jianchen burst out: "Song Jiang—I know him! Bold, generous, rallying heroes across the land, beloved by common folk, leader of the Mount Liang uprising—nicknamed Timely Rain!..."
Mid-rave, he caught Qiao Wenxian staring at him wide-eyed: "Song Jiang rebelled against YOUR Zhao dynasty—why are you glowing about him?!"
Scrambling to cover, Shen Jianchen lied fast: "Read a book once with a hero named Song Jiang—didn't realize there was a real historical Song Jiang! Mixed the two up!"
"Sounds like that book copied the real Huainan bandit leader—how else match him so close? What book is it? Lend it to me!"
Stuck—Water Margin wouldn't be written for centuries!—he spun on the fly: "Read it in the afterlife after I died! Woke back up remembering nothing else except that book."
"Pulling my leg, Prince Yi? How could anything that wild happen?"
"I literally came back from the dead—doesn't that prove wild things happen?"
Qiao Wenxian had to concede: "Fair enough... frail as you were before, even with nine heads I never would've pictured you sitting healthy in a carriage bantering with me. Fine, I'll buy it."
Changing tracks, Shen Jianchen asked, "What's today's palace banquet even celebrating?"
"His Majesty throws banquets nonstop—especially his birthday, planned a full month ahead! Today, if I guess right, celebrates completing Genyue."
"What's Genyue?"
"Truly wiped your memory clean! Genyue is His Majesty's grand imperial landscape park—rare scholar rocks hauled from across the empire, exotic southern trees and blooms, carved railings, pavilions and towers—took decades to build!"
Shen Jianchen cursed under his breath: Pure excess—how much of the people's blood and sweat did that suck dry?!
Chatting on, they pulled up outside the palace walls beneath the sign "Xihua Gate"—closest gate to Prince Yi's Manor, nice and convenient.
Shen Jianchen showed his invitation to the gate guards, who bowed deep: "Enter, Prince Yi, Young Master Qiao."
Inside stretched a broad stone avenue lined with waiting sedan chairs, four guards stationed beside each.
An attendant hurried forward: "Your sedan awaits, Young Master Qiao."
Seeing the eunuch ignore his companion, Qiao Wenxian introduced him: "This is Prince Yi."
The eunuch dropped into a hasty bow: "Blind eyes, forgive this humble servant—Prince Yi, right this way!"
Each boarding a sedan chair hoisted by four guards, they passed through Youchengtian Gate and rode a long stretch before halting outside Chuigong Hall.
The instant they stepped out, guards whisked the chairs away—the sacred forecourt of Chuigong Hall tolerated no clutter.
Approaching them came a purple-robed official wearing court headgear—bearded, sharp slanted brows, beady rat eyes, stern expression.
Qiao Wenxian bowed politely: "Grand Commandant Gao, Wenyuan pays respects."
Sizing up Qiao Wenxian, Grand Commandant Gao nodded: "Ah, Young Master Qiao—quite His Majesty's favorite of late! And your companion?"
"Prince Yi," Qiao Wenxian announced.
Instantly the official's stony face melted into a broad smile: "Prince Yi! Heard of your miraculous revival and full recovery—joyous news indeed! Meeting Your Highness in person honors this old minister! If you don't mind, enter Chuigong Hall with me—His Majesty receives the kowtows of court officials and scholars here first before leading everyone to tour Genyue. After you!"
Grand Commandant Gao... could this be the infamous villain Gao Qiu from Water Margin?! With the man walking right beside him, Shen Jianchen held back from asking Wenyuan.
Stepping through Chuigong Hall's outer gate opened into a vast courtyard teeming with officials in court robes and scholars like Wenyuan.
Fellow ministers stepped up greeting Grand Commandant Gao, who beamed: "Guess who stands before you!"
Every eye turned curious onto Shen Jianchen: "Never met him—please introduce us, Grand Commandant."
"His Majesty's thirteenth son—Prince Yi!"
Having heard whispers of his resurrection, the ministers crowded in with smooth greetings: "At last we behold Prince Yi in person!" "An honor to see Your Highness standing hale before us!"
Spotting someone across the yard, Grand Commandant Gao excused himself fast: "Pardon me, Prince Yi—let me step over there."
"Go right ahead, Grand Commandant."
The instant Gao left, the cluster of officials melted away too, finally leaving Shen Jianchen room to whisper: "Is Grand Commandant Gao's name Gao Qiu?"
"Well, well—you actually remember Grand Commandant Gao's name!" Qiao Wenxian marveled.
"Major treacherous villain!" Shen Jianchen blurted out.
Qiao Wenxian hissed in panic: "Lower your voice! Court ministers everywhere—let word reach Grand Commandant Gao and you're in deep trouble!"
"I'm the emperor's son—scared of him?!"
"Unfavored prince—sometimes lower in pull than senior ministers! Didn't you notice nobody here even recognized you?"
Glancing back toward Gao Qiu, Shen Jianchen asked, "Who's he talking to over there?"
"The Crown Prince—your eldest brother," Qiao Wenxian murmured.
Looking the other way at another young man ringed by officials: "And him?"
"Prince of Kang—your ninth brother."
Just then an Imperial Attendant strode toward them—the very senior eunuch who had visited Prince Yi's Manor first! Shen Jianchen whispered: "Who's that eunuch walking over?"
"Liang Shicheng—Honorary Grand Tutor! Sinecure title, yet massive clout," Qiao Wenxian breathed low.
Another treacherous villain! Two down already—how many more today?!
As Liang Shicheng arrived, Qiao Wenxian bowed: "What instructions, Attendant?"
Returning the bow, Liang Shicheng spoke smoothly: "His Majesty arrives shortly and everyone enters the hall. Since this is Prince Yi's first attendance, allow me to brief you on protocol."
"Go ahead, Attendant."
Liang Shicheng ticked off the rules: "Including Prince Yi, twenty-six imperial princes attend today. Inside the hall, Your Highness cannot stand with Young Master Qiao—you join the princes' ranks to kowtow before His Majesty together. Also, your revival is extraordinary; I expect His Majesty will ask about it, so have your answer ready. That is all—excuse me, other duties call."
Once Liang Shicheng withdrew, Shen Jianchen asked, "Wait—do we have to split up inside the hall?"
"Only for the formal kowtow inside—once we leave the hall, everyone mingles free and we stick together."
"Good."
Liang Shicheng's sharp cry echoed across the yard: "All assemble inside the hall!"
#CH 35
#TITLE: Chapter 35: Veiled Barbs and Touring Genyue
Officials and scholars parted to both sides while imperial princes formed neat ranks down the center.
Nudged forward by Qiao Wenxian, Shen Jianchen walked over to join his brothers.
Huizong sure had plenty of sons! Lined up oldest to youngest, the Crown Prince led the front row looking mid-twenties, while the back rows held ten-year-olds down to toddlers barely three or four.
Thirteenth son—somewhere right in the middle! Shen Jianchen slipped straight into the center row.
Led by the Crown Prince, the princes filed into the hall, followed by civil and military officials, and scholars last.
Standing mid-pack, Shen Jianchen saw only the backs of older brothers' heads ahead and blank, expressionless faces on either side. Bored already, he studied the imperial throne instead.
Carved from prized red sandalwood on a wide, massive dais, draped in gold brocade with lifelike dragon armrests, the seat radiated imperial majesty.
Liang Shicheng's cry rang out: "His Majesty arrives!"
Shen Jianchen shifted his eyes as Zhao Ji strode up to the throne—tall build, crimson dragon robes, long-winged court cap, pale refined features, neat mustache, fine brows and eyes—actually quite a handsome guy!
Settling onto the dragon throne, His Majesty swept a leisurely gaze across the hall. "Relax, everyone—no formal court session today! I'm eager to show you the wonders of Genyue, followed by fine food, fine wine, and merrymaking! And you scholars—be ready to shine!"
At his cue, Liang Shicheng announced: "Kneel and pay homage to His Majesty in order!"
Princes stepped into the center first, dropping to their knees in unison: "Long live Imperial Father!" Shen Jianchen knelt and chanted right along.
"Rise!" As the princes stepped back into rank, His Majesty spoke up: "Did Prince Yi come?"
Stepping out from the middle row, Shen Jianchen bowed: "Your son is here."
Zhao Ji looked over the son he had never seen face to face: "Fine bearing indeed! Sickly from birth, I never summoned you to court, and state affairs kept me from visiting—blink and you're grown! Heard you returned from the dead—tell me every detail! Rare marvel like that deserves hearing."
Disgust stirred in Shen Jianchen—ignoring state crises to build pleasure parks and chase ghost stories! Hopeless ruler! Why not spin a spicy tall tale poking straight at his rotten court?
"Hear your son's account, Imperial Father. That day my breath grew short; gasping my last, I shut my eyes and felt light as air, floating higher and higher until I landed in a strange world! It had a sovereign and ministers too—only that ruler and his court did nothing all day but feast and play while ignoring the common people! Disgusted, I turned to leave—when a divine sage appeared and taught me a peerless boxing art! Mastering it in a flash, I beat that sovereign, slew his treacherous officials, and with nowhere left to go, dropped straight back into my body—waking alive with every illness gone!"
Dead silence fell over the throne—not a minister dared breathe.
Breaking into cold sweat back in the scholars' rank, Qiao Wenxian hesitated one heartbeat before stepping out with cupped hands: "Your Majesty, Prince Yi means that after death he glimpsed a realm far inferior to our Great Song, felt repelled, and hurried back! Proof of his deep devotion to our Song empire—his miraculous revival confirms how deeply Your Majesty's reign wins all hearts!"
Smooth spin melted the freeze on the spot.
Zhao Ji broke into a pleased grin: "Well put, Qiao Wenxian! At the feast, I reward you a pot of imperial fine wine!"
"Thank you, Your Majesty!" Qiao Wenxian backed smoothly into the scholars' line.
His Majesty waved at Prince Yi: "Step back too."
Glad to dodge the spotlight, Shen Jianchen slipped back among the princes.
Next civil officials, military brass, and scholars kowtowed in turn. Ceremonies done, everyone relaxed; changing into informal robes, Zhao Ji led the crowd out to sedan chairs bound for Genyue.
Palace consorts and officials' wives joined too, streaming toward the imperial gardens.
Arriving and dismounting, senior favorites clustered tight around the sovereign while everyone else split into congenial small groups. Naturally Shen Jianchen fell in beside Qiao Wenxian toward the rear.
Qiao Wenxian hissed in low rebuke: "Nearly scared the soul out of me back there! Never pull that again!"
Unbothered, Shen Jianchen shrugged: "Only talked about some place I saw while dead—never mentioned His Majesty or his cronies! What's there to panic over?"
"Keep your voice down! Any fool caught your drift—and neither His Majesty nor these court foxes are fools! Watch your tongue from now on!"
"Fine, fine—I'll watch it."
Ahead loomed a monumental boulder inscribed "Genyue." Stepping past it revealed marvel after marvel: craggy peaks bristling with ten thousand plum trees, waterfalls plunging into emerald pools clear to the bottom, wild ducks swimming and perching over sculpted stones—rare blooms, exotic beasts, luxury pushed beyond imagination.
Touring along, Shen Jianchen marveled silently: Nobody on earth knew how to splurge like Huizong—practically hauled every wonder under Heaven inside one garden!
Just then a poised woman's voice hailed them: "Prince Yi, Young Master Qiao—mind if I walk with you?"
Stopping, Shen Jianchen met a woman in her forties—dignified, graceful, clearly a stunner in her younger days.
Qiao Wenxian bowed with warm pleasure: "Walking alongside Lady Wei honors Wenyuan!"
Turning to Shen Jianchen, Lady Wei asked, "And Prince Yi? Mind my company?"
"If Wenyuan welcomes you, count me in."
Qiao Wenxian introduced her: "This is Lady Wei, wife of Chancellor Zeng—one of our dynasty's foremost female ci poets! Up north she rivals Yi'an Jushi, down south she matches Youqi Jushi—a towering master of the inner chambers!"
Silent curses flew toward the Divine Willow again—why drop him into this bookish Song dynasty crawling with treacherous court officials and world-class poets instead of a martial era full of warriors?! Barely dared open his mouth around people like this!
Still, manners first—he bowed smooth: "Long admired Lady Wei's great name; meeting you today is an honor."
Lady Wei laughed warm: "With Your Highness tucked away in Prince Yi's Manor, I only knew your name until today—glad to behold the real person! Congratulations on your new lease on life! Drop by our manor anytime you're free."
"Whenever I have the chance, certainly."
Strolling three abreast taking in the vistas, Lady Wei asked after a while: "When are you heading back to Lin'an, Young Master Qiao?"
"Meant to be on the road today—delayed by His Majesty's banquet."
"Once you return to Lin'an, invite Youqi Jushi on my behalf! Tell her to find time to visit Bianliang—Lady Wei longs to make her acquaintance."
"Count on it—I'll pass word straight to her."
Touring Genyue took the entire morning; exiting by sedan chair, everyone reconvened at Jiying Hall, the grand palace banquet hall. At the seat of honor stood a huge square table laden with delicacies encircled by drum stools where His Majesty, the Crown Prince, and top ministers sat.
Grouped nearby sat smaller tables for imperial consorts, princes, lesser ministers, scholars, and officials' wives. Steering clear of his royal brothers, Shen Jianchen stuck with Qiao Wenxian—and moments later Lady Wei walked right over to share their table.
Warm after their walk through Genyue, Shen Jianchen teased, "Why sit squeezed in with Wenyuan and me instead of the other court ladies, Lady Wei?"
Lady Wei smiled back: "Why sit squeezed in with Young Master Qiao instead of your royal brothers, Prince Yi?"
Touché! Answering plain, he said, "Sick all my life, barely left the manor—I'm a stranger to my brothers with nothing in common."
"Exact same answer! All those ladies gab about is whose jewels cost more, whose silk is rarer, whose daughter married whom and whose son took which bride—drives me crazy! So I fled to your table! Wait—why are there only you two sitting here?"
Qiao Wenxian sighed wryly: "Blame Prince Yi! After his speech in the hall displeased His Majesty, everyone steers ten paces clear—costing me company too!"
Lady Wei laughed: "Then Prince Yi owes me a toast!"
"Why's that?" Shen Jianchen blinked.
"For defying power and sitting with you when everyone else ran clear!"
Snatching the wine pot at once, Shen Jianchen filled both her cup and his to the brim and raised his glass: "A true heroine deserves a toast! Bottoms up for me—drink at your pace!" He tossed his straight back.
Lady Wei drained hers clean in one shot too!
Qiao Wenxian protested: "Drinking up a storm while leaving me high and dry? Unfair! I stuck by Prince Yi through thick and thin and saved his neck back there—don't you owe me several cups?!"
Without a word, Shen Jianchen waved a palace maid over and called for three extra cups, filling all four brimful.
"For loyalty deep as Wenyuan's, one cup won't cut it! Four for me—sip as you please!"
Downing all four cups straight without flushing a shade, he set them dry.
"Iron liver!" Qiao Wenxian cheered, emptying his own cup.
Right then an eunuch approached carrying a wine pot: "Young Master Qiao, His Majesty's promised gift of fine wine."
Rising with cupped hands, Qiao Wenxian accepted: "Wenyuan bows in gratitude for His Majesty's favor!"
Once the eunuch left, Lady Wei teased: "Got room left for this whole pot, Prince Yi?"
Shen Jianchen grinned: "Piece of cake!"
Food, wine, and lively talk flowed on.
Course after sumptuous course graced the tables while dancing girls twirled and singing girls plucked Chinese lutes—a dazzling vision of gilded peace.
Next Liang Shicheng relayed His Majesty's call for every scholar to compose a poem praising Genyue. Palace maids set writing gear along rows of desks; scholars dipped brushes eagerly and submitted their pieces—Lady Wei winning top prize and glowing praise from the throne!
Caught up in the mood, His Majesty called for brush and ink himself. Maids swept the great square table clean, spread fresh cloth, and laid out the Four Treasures; senior minister Cai Jing ground ink while Gao Qiu presented the brush with both hands. Taking a sip of tea, Zhao Ji took the brush and swept bold strokes across the paper.
In minutes "Winter Plum and Mountain Birds" stood complete, crowned with an inscribed quatrain: "The mountain bird boasts a leisurely grace, / The plum blossoms display their gentle softness. / An eternal pact in ink and color we embrace, / For a thousand autumns, till our heads turn white." [TN: Translator's rendering] Signing his cipher, he had maids stamp several vermilion imperial seals.
Ministers erupted in ecstatic applause.
Setting down his brush, His Majesty spoke: "You lot know my work too well—spare me empty flattery! Let me pick two fresh eyes to critique it."
His gaze swept over to the scholars' side: "Qiao Wenxian—critique my poem! As for the painting—Prince Yi, step up and critique it!"
Every head in Jiying Hall swiveled toward Qiao Wenxian and Prince Yi...
Everyone knew Qiao Wenxian's brilliance with verse—could Prince Yi actually critique an imperial masterpiece?
Betting His Majesty remembered those barbs in Chuigong Hall and meant to humiliate him in public, Shen Jianchen knew refusing wasn't an option—time to bite the bullet and step up.
#CH 36
#TITLE: Chapter 36: Poetry, Painting, and Cuju
Under hundreds of eyes, Qiao Wenxian kept still and murmured low: "Don't panic—the poem sits right on the painting and mirrors its scenes. Once I unpack the verse, you'll have plenty to say about the painting. Hurry up and thank His Majesty with me."
Walking together up to the great table beneath every stare, Qiao Wenxian bowed: "Thank you for your grace, Your Majesty."
Shen Jianchen echoed: "Thank you for your grace, Imperial Father."
"Good! Which of you goes first?" Zhao Ji asked.
"This humble subject always loves going first lest someone steal my words—don't compete with me, Prince Yi!" Qiao Wenxian volunteered smooth.
Staring blankly at the scroll with zero ideas yet—and grateful for Wenyuan covering him—Shen Jianchen kept a calm face: "Wouldn't dream of stealing your thunder, Wenyuan. Go ahead."
As His Majesty and senior officials stepped back to clear the view, Qiao Wenxian bowed, studied "Winter Plum and Mountain Birds," focused on the poem a beat, and began: "Your Majesty's calligraphy grows ever more vigorous and crisp yet never loses sleek beauty—every stroke balances iron bone with supple grace, radiating imperial aura that leaves this subject in awe..."
Pleased, Zhao Ji nudged: "Enough praising my brushwork—unpack the verse."
"Yes, Your Majesty!" Bowing again, Qiao Wenxian launched in: "The verse pairs mountain birds with winter plum—birds poised in ease, blossoms unfolding in tender softness, both brimming with charm. Purest genius lies in the two words 'boasts' and 'displays'—'boasts' captures stillness inside motion, dignity within leisure; 'displays' captures life stirring within stillness! Motion and stillness interweave to weave boundless poetic atmosphere! Moving to the third line reveals a pact sealed in ink and color, bursting with wit. And the final line, 'For a thousand autumns, till our heads turn white,' lifts the entire poem on three layered meanings: first, lifelong devotion between lovers growing old together; second, Your Majesty's lifelong passion poured into calligraphy and painting; third, Your Majesty's steadfast dedication to a thousand-year imperial legacy—pure sovereign grandeur that leaves Wenyuan humbled!"
Delighted, Zhao Ji clapped: "Nobody reads my mind like Wenyuan! Matchless literary gift—reward him three pure gold bowls!"
Liang Shicheng stepped up bearing a brocade box: "Guard His Majesty's golden bowls well, Young Master Qiao!"
Bowing thanks, Qiao Wenxian accepted the box.
Listening beside him, Shen Jianchen marveled that Wenyuan squeezed an essay out of twenty characters—while grumbling inside at that closing praise: Keep blowing smoke! Northern Song's about to collapse on this fool's watch—some "thousand-year legacy" while promoting crooks!
Hearing gold bowls handed out for flattery annoyed him even more: Three solid gold bowls for kissing up?! Gold ought to reward soldiers bleeding for the country!
Right then His Majesty turned to him: "Now, Prince Yi—what do you make of my painting?"
Still drawing a blank yet totally calm—not worth sweating over an incompetent emperor—Shen Jianchen answered breezy: "Imperial Father, illness kept me from books; I know nothing of poetry or painting and fear clumsy words would stain your masterpiece. Better invite someone learned so your painstaking art gets its due."
Snickers rippled among the surrounding ministers.
Zhao Ji's face darkened: "Refusing my command before the whole court?"
"Your son wouldn't dare."
"Then critique it right now! Impress me and rewards await; disappoint me and never set foot in this palace again!"
Couldn't care less about palace invites—might as well wing a couple sentences and call it a day! "Yes, Imperial Father."
Stepping up to "Winter Plum and Mountain Birds," he stared—still blank, yet even winging it needed an opening line!
Suddenly a memory clicked: once Qianli Xun went to an art gallery, and he had trailed along faking a "chance encounter" to hang out with her. Strolling through the rooms, he only had eyes for Qianli Xun, ignoring every canvas—until she asked his take on one painting and he quipped, "Whatever you think, I think!"
She had scolded him for pretending to appreciate art, then lectured him like a teacher: "To understand a painting, latch onto the visual subjects—every tiny detail reflects the painter's heart! And never parrot others—say what YOU actually see and feel, that's what makes it interesting!"
Light exploded in Shen Jianchen's mind! Glancing around the circle of officials pressing close, aside from Wenyuan's genuine worry, every single face—His Majesty's included—smirked waiting to watch him crash and burn!
Those smug faces killed his plan to half-ass it! Pulling every ounce of wit from memory and locking his eyes back onto the painting, a faint grin touched his lips as he began:
"First, your son sees orchids—blooming pure in secluded valleys, untouched by worldly dust. Painting orchids expresses Imperial Father's love for noble souls pure as orchids! A man is what he loves; a sovereign pure as orchids will surely shun treacherous flatterers—how could our Great Song fail to prosper?!"
"Well said!" Zhao Ji blurted out. "Keep going!"
Typical incompetent emperor—loves getting his ego stroked! Shen Jianchen smirked inside. Fine, I'll lay it on thick! Think I made CEO young on talent alone? Sucking up is a core skill!
"Rising past the orchids stands a winter plum—no leaves yet, half buds tight, half petals open bright and proud. Winter plum blooms in the twelfth lunar month, deepest bitter winter..." Suddenly a poem memorized in school popped straight into his head: "It brings to mind a lyric: 'Wind and rain escorted Spring's departure, / Flying snow welcomes Spring's return. / On the ice-clad rock rising high and sheer / A flower blooms sweet and fair. / Sweet and fair, she craves not Spring for herself alone, / To be the harbinger of Spring she is content. / When the mountain flowers are in full bloom / She will smile mingling in their midst.' [TN: Official Foreign Languages Press translation] That lyric reminds us to stay unbroken and hopeful amid harsh trials—just like the winter plum beneath Imperial Father's brush!"
Overwhelmed, Zhao Ji stepped forward and gripped Prince Yi's hand: "Never dreamed you'd see such depth—truly my own son! And what a lyric you wrote—'On the ice-clad rock rising high and sheer, a flower blooms sweet and fair' captures the very soul of my plum branch! What else do you see? Tell me!"
Sorry, Grandpa Mao—borrowing your masterpiece! This clueless emperor thinks I wrote it—please don't haunt me!
Beside them, Qiao Wenxian muttered under his breath: "Prince Yi sure hides his talents deep!"
Letting go of his hand, Zhao Ji listened rapt.
Studying the canvas closer, Shen Jianchen went on: "Perched atop the plum bough sit two white-headed bulbuls facing opposite ways, serene and composed. To your son, the front bird represents Imperial Father, while the rear bird represents the empire and its people—a true sovereign rests his back upon land and people so his foundation stands rock-solid! Imperial Father is just such a ruler, backed by Song hills and rivers, holding millions of subjects in his heart..."
"Magnificent!" Zhao Ji cried out again!
"Knowing nothing of painting or composition, your son still senses utter harmony across the scroll—every element staggered in balance, upper and lower echoing each other, verse and painting intertwined so removing either would mar perfection! Even the vermilion seals belong: crimson stamps poised high above enliven the whole composition, symbolizing a sovereign exalted on high whose heart stays forever bound to his subjects!"
Zhao Ji beamed wide: "Unschooled in painting yet gifted with sharp eyes—your brilliance rivals Wenyuan's! Reward Prince Yi TEN pure gold bowls!"
Pure hot air crowned "brilliance" rivaling Wenyuan?! Hopeless ruler! Ignoring Huizong's glow, Shen Jianchen shot Wenyuan a cheeky wink.
Qiao Wenxian flashed him a discreet thumbs-up.
Liang Shicheng hurried over bearing an even larger brocade box: "Ten gold bowls for Prince Yi—keep them safe!"
Taking the box, bowing thanks to the fool on the throne, and backing up beside Qiao Wenxian, Shen Jianchen whispered: "More gold bowls than you—do I have to lug this thing around all day?"
"Watch a pro," Qiao Wenxian grinned, waving a palace maid over: "Pass both my box and Prince Yi's to our guards—have them placed inside our carriage at Xihua Gate."
"Yes, Young Master." Carrying both boxes, the maid walked off.
"Won't somebody swipe them out of the carriage?"
"Look at you turning into a miser—ten gold bowls bought you off fast! Relax, guards stay by the carriage, nobody dares touch them."
Right then Liang Shicheng called out loud: "To celebrate Genyue's completion, the palace has arranged a cuju match! Please proceed to the cuju field!"
Located just steps from the banquet hall, the cuju field drew everyone on foot except His Majesty in his sedan. A shaded pavilion held the sovereign's seat before a screen painted with auspicious clouds and cranes, a plain rosewood chair centered in front; rows of guest chairs paired with small tea-and-snack tables ringed the field.
Shen Jianchen and Qiao Wenxian picked seats as His Majesty settled in with Liang Shicheng standing guard; the Crown Prince, Grand Commandant Gao, Cai Jing, and Chancellor Zeng sat close by beaming, while consorts, princes, and court ladies filled the surrounding tiers.
"Is Grand Commandant Gao actually good at cuju?" Shen Jianchen asked.
"Top-tier—master level!" Qiao Wenxian replied.
"Getting on in years though—hasn't he lost a step?"
"Sharp as ever—not that old yet!"
Mid-chat, Lady Wei walked over and took the seat beside Prince Yi: "Prince Yi is the craftiest dark horse I've ever met!"
"How so?" Qiao Wenxian asked.
"Playing dumb about poetry and painting—claiming he'd barely read a book—then wowing His Majesty straight out of ten gold bowls, outdoing even Young Master Qiao! If that isn't hiding your brilliance, what is?"
Shen Jianchen lowered his voice with a smirk: "Truth be told, Lady Wei—master the art of flattery, and both your brain and your tongue run like greased lightning!"
"Don't buy for one second you only know flattery!"
"Believe what you want—facts are facts! Plus I had pointers from a friend."
"Pointers on flattery?"
"Nope—pointers on how to look at paintings."
Qiao Wenxian frowned: "Sick in bed till days ago—aside from me, what other friend do you even have? Don't remember teaching you art critique!"
"Wrong!" Lady Wei chimed in: "Who says you're Prince Yi's only friend? There's his new friend right here—though I didn't coach him either, Young Master Qiao can vouch!"
A shadow crossed Shen Jianchen's eyes: "A friend from another world—someone I can't find now."
"Another world?" Trading amused glances, Lady Wei and Qiao Wenxian wrote it off as princely nonsense and let the subject drop.
Drums boomed—the cuju exhibition began, drawing every eye to the field.
Dead center stood the tall netted goal ring called the Fengliu Yan. Two teams of five marched out—one in blue uniforms, one in red—lining either side of the goal. Bowing to each other, at the next drumbeat they launched into fierce action.
Watching cuju live for the first time, Shen Jianchen saw instant kinship with soccer—except the ball couldn't touch ground, juggled teammate to teammate until passed to the Lead Striker to volley clean through the elevated ring!
Grinning inside, he thought: I'm a soccer beast—keepie-uppies were child's play, and add Shen Fist power on top? Easy!
Glancing over at Gao Qiu watching rapt, his Water Margin hero complex flared straight up: Might have let you slide if we'd never crossed paths—now that we have, time to settle a score for the hundred and eight heroes!
Spotting a player poised to loft a pass to his Lead Striker, Shen Jianchen kicked off with light-foot skill, vaulted straight onto the pitch, intercepted midair before the striker blinked, and blasted the ball dead-center through the goal ring!
Gasps froze the crowd before applause rippled out.
"Prince Yi's skills keep getting wilder!" Qiao Wenxian cheered.
"Heard court whispers a divine sage taught Prince Yi martial arts after death—looks true!" Lady Wei marveled.
His Majesty's voice boomed down: "Prince Yi! What is the meaning of this?!"
Every player froze at the interruption.
Shen Jianchen bowed: "Imperial Father, your son itches to play cuju too!"
Zhao Ji laughed: "Haha, wanted to play? Should've said so—plenty of room! Why pull a stunt like that?"
"Your son heard Grand Commandant Gao's cuju skills are unmatched and longs to test himself against him—begging Imperial Father's permission!"
Zhao Ji glanced at Gao Qiu: "Grand Commandant Gao, Prince Yi just came back from death—indulge him with a friendly match!"
With His Majesty speaking, Gao Qiu couldn't refuse; rising with a bow: "Your minister obeys."
Shen Jianchen slipped on a red jersey, Gao Qiu donned blue, each taking Lead Striker for his squad. Drums sounded—game on!
Red launched a "Meteor Chasing the Moon" combo—resting one hand on a teammate's shoulder, Shen Jianchen vaulted and drilled home the opening goal! Blue countered with "Fallen Flowers and Flowing Water," Gao Qiu answering right back with a crisp score.
Seven or eight rounds later, both sides stood dead even.
Old or not, Gao Qiu's footwork was wicked—beating him on pure finesse looked tough. Speed and raw force would break him! Shen Jianchen called a brief halftime breather, His Majesty nodding assent.
Huddling his squad during the break, Shen Jianchen issued crisp orders: "No matter how wild the pass, I'll catch it—quit wasting time juggling! Ping the ball straight to me fast as you can!"
Never having played under a striker like this, the teammates gaped yet complied—Prince Yi called the shots, plus it saved them tons of work!
Drums rolled again—second half exploded! Every rapid pass from red jerseys met Shen Jianchen's foot on the volley, rockets drilling clean through the goal ring until red jumped two goals clear!
Flustered, Gao Qiu fired a "Meteor Chasing the Moon" strike straight at the goal—only for Shen Jianchen's counter-kick to slam the ball dead in midair right in front of the ring, knocking it down to the turf!
Three straight shots blocked cold! Out of breath, Gao Qiu cupped his hands in defeat: "Prince Yi strikes too fast and fierce! This old minister's strength fails him—I concede, I concede!"
Stepping close, Shen Jianchen asked cool: "Grand Commandant Gao—ever heard of the hundred and eight heroes of Mount Liang?"
Blinking baffled, Gao Qiu replied, "Your Highness means the Marshes of Mount Liang? Bandits swarm there year round—never heard of any 'heroes.'"
"Guess we mean different folks. Either way, those heroes asked me to test your mettle for them."
Totally baffled—this revived prince spoke in riddles nonstop!—Gao Qiu bowed: "Your minister takes his leave, Prince Yi."
Turning toward the throne pavilion, Shen Jianchen bowed: "Did your son's footwork please Imperial Father?"
Beaming wide, Zhao Ji laughed: "Never knew your cuju ran this sharp! Proof that divine sage truly trained you! Since your martial skills shine so bright, I hereby appoint you Military Commissioner of Lin'an! Stay a few days in Bianliang, then head south to take office!"
Floor-dropped—one game of cuju just landed him a regional military command! No wonder Gao Qiu climbed the ladder kicking balls! With His Majesty's word spoken, he bowed low: "Your son thanks Imperial Father for this boundless grace!"
Both Qiao Wenxian and Lady Wei beamed with delight—what a jackpot trip to the palace!
#CH 37
#TITLE: Chapter 37: Returning from the Palace Banquet
By the time the palace banquet wrapped up and Shen Jianchen and Qiao Wenxian rolled back to Prince Yi's Manor, twilight had settled over the city.
Skipping over every pompous palace detail, Shen Jianchen rolled one gleaming prize around in his head: Military Commissioner of Lin'an!
He was already heading to Lin'an—now he held an official imperial mandate to live there! Talk about fate opening every door! Did that mean Qianli Xun truly waited in Lin'an? Anticipation tugged him south harder than ever.
Stepping through the manor gate alongside Wenyuan, Qiao Mengrong hurried straight up to meet them: "Back at last! I worried sick at home all day, Brother!"
Qiao Wenxian grinned: "Worried for nothing, Princess Jiahe! Not only did Prince Yi walk out without a scratch, His Majesty named him Military Commissioner of Lin'an on the spot!"
Pure joy lit Qiao Mengrong's face: "His Majesty finally recognizes Brother's worth! Nobody will ever look down on Prince Yi's Manor again!"
Her radiant happiness stirred another pang of guilt in Shen Jianchen. "Wenyuan and I are parched—let's not stand in the yard, head inside for tea."
"Silly me blocking you in the courtyard out of worry! Come inside—I'll have maids steep loose-leaf tea! Xilan, hurry!"
"Yes, Princess!" Xilan dashed off grinning.
Moments after the three settled inside, Xilan returned with a teapot and two tea bowls, poured for Prince Yi and Young Master Qiao, and stood beside her mistress listening.
"Freshly steeped loose-leaf tea, Brother—still scalding, let it cool a bit," Qiao Mengrong cautioned.
Shen Jianchen nodded.
Catching Qiao Mengrong's melting gaze locked onto her brother, Qiao Wenxian teased: "Think I'd better bow out before I feel like a third wheel!"
Flushing hot as she realized she had been staring nonstop, Qiao Mengrong lowered her eyes shyly: "Always teasing, Young Master Qiao—how could you ever be in the way? You surely looked out for Brother at the palace today; allow Mengrong to thank you on his behalf!" Rising, she offered a formal curtsy.
Jumping up to return the bow, Qiao Wenxian protested: "You overwhelm me, Princess Jiahe! Prince Yi needed zero help from me—skilled in letters and arms alike, he won His Majesty's favor entirely on his own!"
"Quit being so stiff, you two—acts like strangers," Shen Jianchen cut in.
"Right as always, Brother—I'll listen to you."
Sitting back down, Qiao Mengrong turned over Wenyuan's phrase: skilled in letters and arms alike! She had witnessed his martial skill—yet in letters, Zhao Pu used to say poetry drained his energy too much for his frail frame and never wrote a line.
Curious, she asked: "Tell me what Brother did today, Young Master Qiao—let me celebrate too!"
"Nothing worth telling—just a pile of bootlicking flattery," Shen Jianchen tried shutting it down.
Seated again, Qiao Wenxian countered: "Don't sell yourself short, Prince Yi! Plenty of flattery, sure—yet real talent too! Take that ci lyric you recited—utterly extraordinary! Let me recite it for Princess Jiahe..."
Not his poem—Shen Jianchen moved to stop him: "I didn't write that lyric—someone else did!"
"Who else could've written it?" Qiao Wenxian demanded. "First time in my life I ever heard those lines!"
Cornered, Shen Jianchen shrugged: "Learned it in the other world after I died—written by a great man."
Disbelieving as ever, Qiao Wenxian smirked: "Knew you'd say that! Never seen this 'other world'—first heard it from your lips, so as far as I'm concerned you wrote it!"
Bursting with curiosity, Qiao Mengrong pleaded: "Recite it, Young Master Qiao—I want to memorize every word!"
Unable to muzzle Wenyuan, Shen Jianchen gave up—letting Grandpa Mao's masterpiece visit the Song dynasty sounded kinda fun anyway!
Checking the tea temperature, Qiao Wenxian took a few sips, set down his bowl, rose, gathered feeling, and recited aloud: "'Wind and rain escorted Spring's departure, / Flying snow welcomes Spring's return. / On the ice-clad rock rising high and sheer / A flower blooms sweet and fair. / Sweet and fair, she craves not Spring for herself alone, / To be the harbinger of Spring she is content. / When the mountain flowers are in full bloom / She will smile mingling in their midst.' [TN: Official Foreign Languages Press translation]"
Savoring every word, Qiao Mengrong drifted straight back into memory...
One brutal winter, her brother's illness worsened; thick quilts and one brazier couldn't warm him. Ordering guards to haul in a second brazier, she fetched another quilt herself until he drifted off to sleep.
Outside, heavy snow drifted down like goose feathers. Throwing on a red cloak, she stepped into the garden with Xilan through ankle-deep snow—where one winter plum tree bloomed brave against the frost.
Unable to step outdoors in winter, her brother rarely beheld winter plum blossoms; Qiao Mengrong had Xilan snap several branches.
Back inside, she filled a vase with water, arranged the plum twigs by hand, and sat beside his bed watching him sleep.
Waking to a crisp, delicate fragrance, Zhao Pu murmured: "Where is plum blossom scent coming from?"
"I had Xilan break a few winter plum branches for a vase."
"Help me sit up to see!"
Propping him up while Xilan set a stool by the bedside, she placed the vase right before his eyes.
Like magic, the instant the branches met his gaze, one tight bud unfurled open before them! Joyful, Qiao Mengrong took it for a heavenly omen predicting his recovery.
Yet Zhao Pu whispered: "In my eyes, Mengrong, you are that blooming plum—the plum flower in my heart giving me courage to hold on. Without you, Brother would have given up long ago..."
Tears misted Qiao Mengrong's eyes; gazing through shimmering blur at the healthy man seated across from her, she wondered: had memories stirred when he recited that poem?
Spotting her reddened eyes, Shen Jianchen asked: "What's wrong, Mengrong?"
Glancing at the princess, Qiao Wenxian read the room fast: "Business calls—excuse me, you two!" Stepping briskly out, he left them alone.
Only then did Qiao Mengrong speak: "I adore your lyric, Brother. Whatever was in your heart when you spoke it, to me you wrote it for me."
Struck speechless—unable to shatter her dream yet refusing to lie—Shen Jianchen kept silent, lifting his tea bowl to drain the rest and hide his awkwardness.
Collecting himself behind the cup, he set it down with an easy smile: "Exhausted from the palace banquet, Mengrong—heading to bed early. Aren't you sleepy?"
A sharp sting of letdown pierced her—the old Zhao Pu would have answered with tender devotion, whereas this brother dodged her heart every single time.
"Let me walk you back to your room."
Rising, Shen Jianchen waved it off: "No need—I'll head back myself."
Standing to watch his retreating back, ache deepened in her chest: sick, she had prayed for his health; healthy, he no longer leaned on her.
"Brother!"
Halting, he turned back: "Something else?"
"When do you leave for Lin'an to take office?"
"Have to pick up my imperial commission and seal at the palace tomorrow—one day delay, then we set out the day after."
"Will you take me along, Brother?"
"If you want to see Lin'an, how could Brother say no? Come right along."
Brightness bloomed back on her face: "Thank you, Brother!"
"We're family—no need to thank me." With that, he headed off to his quarters.
"We're family"—hearing those words from his lips warmed Qiao Mengrong straight through; clearly she still held a vital place in his heart!
Plans set with Qiao Wenxian, they would handle palace paperwork first, pack up, and head south.
Next morning at court, Liang Shicheng handed over the imperial commission stamped with the sovereign's seal alongside the official seal of Military Commissioner of Lin'an.
"Lin'an is a fine post—need local guidance, turn straight to Prefect Zhu Xiyan," Liang Shicheng advised. "Though imperial armies crushed Fang La last year and granted amnesty to the Huainan bandits, straggler outlaws still roam southern roads—travel with care, Prince Yi."
"How did the Huainan bandits fare after amnesty?" Shen Jianchen asked.
"The court settled them properly, yet rebel hearts never die—they rose up again! Clearly their surrender was fake from the start. A week ago General Zhe Kecun crushed them, killing their leader Song Jiang and executing every ringleader."
"Dead?!" Hoping to meet one legendary character from the novels he knew, Shen Jianchen felt a dull thud of loss.
Baffled by the prince's gloom, Liang Shicheng scoffed: "Of course dead! Defying the throne brings only one end."
Leaving the palace inside his carriage, Shen Jianchen brooded over missing Song Jiang.
Soon the lively roar of Bianliang's market avenues pulled him from his gloom. Peering out the window, he took in the teeming streetscape: noble ladies, wandering monks, storytellers, sedan chairs threading through pedestrians, camels lumbering down the boulevard loaded with goods and riders...
Then a familiar shop sign caught his eye: "Grand Physician Zhao's Clinic"—an apothecary! Wait—that reminded him of Bai Feng! The kid still hadn't shown up at Prince Yi's Manor; why not check inside?
Just as the thought struck, loud wails burst right out of the clinic door: "Sob...! You killed my mom! Give back my mom! Give back my five taels of silver! Sob...!"
Halting the carriage, Shen Jianchen hopped down and stepped inside Grand Physician Zhao's Clinic.
Sprawled on the floor howling sat Bai Feng, ringed by clinic clerks and waiting patients.
Stepping right up, Shen Jianchen asked: "Bai Feng! Why are you making a scene here instead of reporting to me?"
Spotting his savior, Bai Feng scrambled up and latched onto his sleeve: "Sob! Big Brother—you're Captain of the Guard at Prince Yi's Manor, help me! This doctor killed my mom and won't give back my silver! Sob...!"
Hearing "Prince Yi's Manor," the physician stepped out from the back bowing low: "May I ask your honored position in Prince Yi's Manor, sir?"
"What if I told you I AM Prince Yi?" Shen Jianchen replied calm.
The doctor dropped into a deep bow at once: "Prince Yi! When this lad brought his mother in, she was already beyond saving..."
Bai Feng cried back: "If you couldn't save her, why take my five taels?!"
"Moved by your filial devotion, I still tried my utmost!" the doctor defended himself. "Her condition needed costly herbs—five taels barely covered ingredients, I didn't even charge a consultation fee! Checked her pulse, weighed the herbs—she passed away at home before taking a single dose! How is that my fault?!"
"Her dying isn't your fault—yet she never swallowed a drop of your medicine! Why keep all the silver?!" Bai Feng refused to budge.
Catching the gist—Bai Feng just wanted his five taels back—Shen Jianchen asked: "Doctor, can you take back the herbs he filled?"
The doctor sighed awkwardly: "Mixed together by prescription weight—impossible to separate and resell. Still, out of respect for Prince Yi, herbs or no herbs, I'll refund the boy two taels to pay for his mother's burial—fair enough?"
Still bristling to argue more, Bai Feng got cut off by Shen Jianchen: "More than fair."
The moment the clinic handed over two taels of silver, Shen Jianchen hauled Bai Feng by the arm out the door and straight into his carriage.
Stashing the two taels safe, Bai Feng squinted up at him: "You're REALLY Prince Yi?"
"Of course."
Leaning close for a long look, Bai Feng muttered: "Huh—princes look just like regular folks, just fancier threads!"
"Where do you live? I'll drop you home."
"Mom's gone—what home? No point going back."
"Don't you need to go bury your mother?"
"Buried her yesterday."
"Any other family left?"
"Dad died when I was born, just Mom and me—now she's gone too, I've got nowhere left. Planned to get my five taels back today and head straight to Prince Yi's Manor—never figured I'd bump right into you! Guess fate really links me to Your Highness!"
"Then straight to Prince Yi's Manor right now—from today on, you follow me."
"Yes, Your Highness!" Grief flipping to pure grin, Bai Feng had a home again.
#CH 38
#TITLE: Chapter 38: Meeting Matron Wei at Last
Seven or eight peaceful days flew by in the bamboo grove, Qianli Xun alternating between strolling under emerald stalks and devouring the huaben storybooks Kujue brought back.
Excitement mounted with every page—she had found her calling in this era! She could adapt her old modern web novels into Song-dynasty huaben scripts for common folk to enjoy!
No sooner thought than done—she grabbed her brush and started writing nonstop...
Dozens of pages later, she set the brush down at last.
Tidying the sheets into a neat stack, Xiaguo stole admiring glances at the neat ink rows.
"You're amazing, Miss—writing so many characters!"
"Can you read them?"
"Only a few."
What a waste, Qianli Xun sighed inwardly—lovely as Xiaguo was, born into a wealthy home she'd have mastered zither, chess, calligraphy, and painting alike!
"I'll have Kuqiao teach you to read from now on."
Xiaguo lit up: "Thank you, Miss!"
"Where's Kuqiao?"
"Sister Kuqiao went back to the old cottage to cook your lunch."
Glancing out the doorway at the high sun, Qianli Xun marveled: "Time flies—already noon!"
Soon Kuqiao returned carrying a bamboo basket of lunch, waited on Miss as she ate, and cleared up—her happy daily routine.
Qianli Xun told her to pass the finished huaben manuscript to Kujue and have him drop it with any storyteller in town.
Packing the basket and clutching the manuscript, Kuqiao headed back to her family's old house.
When Kuqiao returned, an older woman walked beside her flanked by two maids and two page boys.
Slipping inside first, Kuqiao grinned: "Guess who I brought, Miss!"
Still flipping through a storybook, Qianli Xun looked up: "Who?"
"Matron Wei! Done collecting rents elsewhere, she came here for the bamboo grove, heard you were staying here, and hurried right over!"
Holder of Zhu Shuzhen's purse strings and her loyal wet nurse—Qianli Xun had been dying to meet her! Never dreamed she'd turn up right here in the grove!
Tossing the book aside: "Quick—invite her in!"
Stepping back out, Kuqiao called: "Miss invites you inside, Matron Wei!"
Around fifty, radiating a blend of warmth, authority, and sharp business sense, Matron Wei stepped inside and bowed low; Kuqiao slid a cushion across from her mistress, and Matron Wei settled down at ease.
"Never dreamed I'd find you out here, young lady! Kuqiao caught me up on the walk over—had I known, I would've delayed rent collection instead of leaving you to be beaten into amnesia by Son-in-law!"
Two tears slipped down her cheeks; dabbing them with a handkerchief, she pressed on crisp: "Don't lose heart over the failed divorce, young lady! Once back in Lin Manor, I'll talk to Son-in-law myself—and if he still refuses, we move right out! Plenty of vacant courtyards across Lin'an—buy one of our own instead of swallowing his garbage!"
Music to Qianli Xun's ears! Now THIS was real family! Instant warmth flooded her heart—no wonder Zhu Shuzhen trusted this woman with her whole dowry!
"Don't worry, Matron—my resolve to divorce hasn't budged an inch, and if Lin Jue drags his feet, we'll do just what you said and move out!"
Hearing steel and spirit in her voice, Matron Wei saw the change plain—good! Better strong than pushed around like before.
"Hearing you talk like that puts my heart at ease."
"Do you have somewhere to stay while collecting rent, Matron?"
"Village head set up a bamboo cottage close by—same place I stay every year."
"Good, glad you're taken care of."
Right then Xiaguo brought fresh tea over and set it before Matron Wei.
Glancing up at the striking girl, Matron Wei paused: "Don't recognize this maid."
Xiaguo curtsied quick: "Good day, Matron—I'm Xiaguo, Miss's newly hired maid."
"Wait outside the cottage a moment, Xiaguo—private talk with my young lady."
Obediently Xiaguo stepped out beside Matron Wei's own servants.
Once alone inside, Matron Wei spoke blunt: "Why take on a maid THIS stunning, young lady?"
Qianli Xun blinked: "What's wrong with that, Matron?"
"A noble lady's personal maids ought to look neat yet modest like Kuqiao! Xiaguo is way too eye-catching—bring her out in public, folks stare at the maid instead of the mistress! Won't she steal your spotlight?"
"Overthinking it, Matron—I don't care about stuff like that! Besides, if some good young gentleman spots her and takes a shine to her, all the better for her future!"
"If that's how you feel, I won't argue—just worried her face will stir up trouble for you."
Kuqiao piped up: "Calling me plain, Matron—I'll forgive you! Still, you hit the nail on the head: the second Xiaguo showed up, trouble hit—lucky Miss's fists sent those local thugs packing!"
"To my eyes you're just right—beats Xiaguo by a mile! Haha..." Laughing warm, Matron Wei added: "Kuqiao told me you woke up knowing martial arts—thrilled for you! Let's see Son-in-law dare lay a finger on you now!"
First person in this whole dynasty to cheer rather than freak out over her fighting skills—Qianli Xun liked Matron Wei more every minute! "Got something to ask you, Matron."
"Go ahead, young lady."
"Living here these days, I see how tough these bamboo villagers have it. Can we reduce their rent this year?"
Beside them, Kuqiao beamed in joy.
"Great minds think alike—except this bamboo grove won't belong to you much longer, so we can't collect rent this year anyway."
"Wait—with amnesia I didn't even know what estates I own! Only found out this grove was mine when Kuqiao told me and everyone started calling me Proprietor! Why isn't it mine anymore?"
"Not amnesia—you never bothered tracking estates before and left it all to me; once back in town I'll lay out every single ledger! As for this grove, it WAS yours—until I went to collect rent and the village head told me Young Master Chen now holds the land root! He plans to redeem the grove at original price and pay this year's village rent out of pocket too!"
Unsure what "land root" meant yet catching "Young Master Chen," Qianli Xun saw red—Chen Haoyu's dirty handiwork! "Wait—if I'm the owner, how does the Chen family butt in?"
Matron Wei explained: "When the Zhu family bought this grove, they signed a conditional sale contract with the original owner—giving the Zhus use and rent rights while the 'land root' stayed with the seller, letting him buy it back at original cost anytime."
Light dawned: "Was the seller the Chen family?"
"No, someone else—my guess is the Chens paid top silver to buy the land root off the seller! Richest family in Lin'an, swimming in cash! Weird thing is, this grove barely turns a profit, plus Zhus and Chens are in-laws—why pull a stunt like this?"
Qianli Xun fumed: "Because Chen Haoyu wants revenge on me!"
"Revenge?" Matron Wei frowned.
Kuqiao filled her in: "Out collecting rents, you missed it, Matron—Miss busted up Young Master Chen harassing innocent girls twice! Clearly he's nursing a grudge and pulled this to spite her!"
"Aha! Good on you for stepping in, young lady! If Young Master Chen wants the bamboo grove, hand it over—your other estates leave you rolling in silver anyway; losing one grove is pocket change."
Matron Wei's cool head matched Qianli Xun's own vibe—still, no way was she letting Chen Haoyu walk off smug without bleeding a little!
"Wants to use the land root to reclaim the grove? Fine—let me meet him face to face!"
Chatting done, Matron Wei toured the cozy bamboo cottage, praised Kuqiao's tidy setup, and bade farewell.
Stepping outside, she eyed Xiaguo standing dutifully by the door, pausing to deliver a calm yet steel-edged warning: "When you go out serving our young lady, remember your place—never steal the spotlight, keep a maid's proper bounds. Clear?"
Xiaguo bowed low: "Yes, Matron—I'll engrave it on my heart."
"Go back in and wait on her well." Gathering her attendants, Matron Wei left.
Stepping inside to see Miss reading again, Xiaguo slipped quietly to the back room where Kuqiao sat embroidering and sidled close: "Sister Kuqiao, Miss asked you to teach me to read—will you teach me?"
Keeping her needle moving, Kuqiao remarked: "Truth is, reading or not doesn't matter for maids—serving our mistress well is what counts."
"Still, Miss told you to, and I really want to learn! Besides, you're a maid and you know how to read!"
"Fine, since Miss ordered it! Every day gather Miss's discarded practice sheets, and I'll teach you from those characters."
"Thank you, Sister Kuqiao!"
Stiff from sitting over storybooks, Qianli Xun set her volume down and stepped outside to stretch.
Right outside hovered Kujue, hesitating whether to knock.
Walking down the bamboo steps, Qianli Xun smiled: "What are you standing out here for?"
Sturdy as Kujue was, shyness tied his tongue around refined ladies like Zhu Shuzhen.
"Pr... Proprietor, gave your pages to a storyteller... he loved it, praised it nonstop, begs you to keep... keep writing!"
Qianli Xun laughed: "Why so jumpy? I'm not a tiger!"
Scratching his head sheepish, Kujue mumbled: "Proprietor's a grand lady—I get nervous around bigwigs..."
"While I'm staying here, I'll trouble you to run manuscripts—not for free, Kuqiao will pay your errand fee."
"Happy to run 'em free!"
"Too honest for your own good! Go on back to work."
Excused, Kujue spun around and trotted off.
Turning back toward her cottage, Qianli Xun caught movement out of the corner of her eye—Chen Haoyu swaggering straight toward her with an entourage! Didn't even have to hunt him down—he marched right to her door!
Beside his usual four street thugs strode a newcomer whose solid gait screamed martial master—clearly Chen Haoyu had brought heavy muscle today.
Sizing up the newcomer—broad shoulders, bare brawny chest rippling with thick muscle, intimidating build—Qianli Xun couldn't tell who'd win until fists met.
Strutting close, Chen Haoyu sneered: "Hey, Zhu Shuzhen! This bamboo grove belongs to me now—pack up and get out!"
Qianli Xun scoffed right back: "Holding the land root and still zero clue how contracts work, Chen Haoyu! The Zhu family signed a conditional sale contract—you have to redeem it at original price first! Haven't paid me a single coin and you're claiming the land? Hilarious—some rich young master!"
"Silver's on the way! Second it arrives, haul yourself out of my grove—and leave that new maid behind for me!"
"Stuff your garbage! Even after you pay, I'm not budging an inch till my full month is up—after that, you couldn't beg me to stay!"
"Tsk, tsk—a noble lady tossing out street curses! Wrecking your classy image! Heard from my sister yesterday you're fighting Lin Jue for divorce—acting this crude, who'd even want you? Were I your husband, I'd have cast you out ages ago!"
Spotting his bait to spark a brawl early—bad timing while she still wanted leverage—Qianli Xun kept ice-cool.
"Want this bamboo grove, Chen Haoyu? Yours! Bring every relevant party together tomorrow and agree to a few terms, and I'll hand it over with both hands!"
"Deal! Tomorrow at the village head's place—be there! We'll hear your terms then." Waving carelessly over his shoulder, Chen Haoyu swaggered away with his crew.
Qianli Xun muttered after him: "Spoiled dandy!"
#CH 39
#TITLE: Chapter 39: A Duel of Masters and a New Contract
The village head's bamboo house stood biggest and sturdiest in the whole village, fronting a spacious yard.
Beneath a shaded bamboo arbor set with tables and chairs, Chen Haoyu sat waiting early with his crew while the village head hovered pouring tea with a fawning grin.
Soon Qianli Xun arrived with Matron Wei and both maids. Spotting the same five men beside Chen Haoyu from afar, she studied the brawny fighter again—clearly brought as muscle against her fists.
The village head bustled forward: "Right this way, Proprietor and Matron Wei!"
Qianli Xun and Matron Wei took seats across from Chen Haoyu while Kuqiao and Xiaguo stood behind their mistress.
Leering straight at Xiaguo, Chen Haoyu purred: "Taking a fancy to you is pure luck—what future is there trailing after her? Criminal waste of that pretty face working as a maid!"
Xiaguo flared back: "Go with Young Master Chen just to end up sold off?!..."
Matron Wei cut her off sharp: "Xiaguo! Who gave you leave to speak? Shut your mouth!"
Xiaguo clamped her lips shut at once.
Disgusted by Chen Haoyu's sleazy stare, Qianli Xun barked: "Told you to bring every relevant party, Chen Haoyu—why only you?"
Dragging his eyes off Xiaguo, Chen Haoyu smirked: "What, these five behind me don't count as people?"
"I meant people legally tied to this grove!"
"Expected me to drag your dad and my dad out here? Waste of time! I hold the land root—meaning ownership; your father gave you the grove as dowry—meaning usage and rent rights. With you and me sitting here, everybody who counts is present!"
Loathe him as she did, he had a point; Qianli Xun let it drop.
Listening quietly till now, Matron Wei spoke up: "One question puzzles me, Young Master Chen—care to enlighten an old woman?"
"Ask away, Matron Wei—happy to oblige."
"The Zhu house isn't as wealthy as the Chens, yet hardly poor. Out of every estate in our young lady's dowry, this bamboo grove yields the least—why on earth set your sights on this patch?"
Chen Haoyu grinned smug: "Only because somebody ran her mouth boasting this grove was 'her turf'! Figured I'd teach her that anytime I snap my fingers, her turf turns into MY turf!"
Catching Matron Wei's warning glance, Qianli Xun swallowed her comeback and let the veteran handle him.
"Ah—just over wounded pride! Our young lady isn't nearly so petty. Out of regard for our families being in-laws—and calling your sister Sister-in-law—she won't hold it against you! Simply pay back the original purchase price per the contract and cover this year's village rent, and our young lady hands over the grove without a murmur."
Going this smooth left Chen Haoyu irritated—called petty to his face AND cheated out of revenge! He had hired a martial ace just to settle scores for two humiliating beatings—plus he still wanted Xiaguo! No way was this ending quiet!
"Never stingy with coin! Two chests sitting right there—one thousand taels of silver! Covers original purchase price plus village rent with change to spare!..."
Before he finished, the village head dropped into a bowing scrape: "On behalf of every bamboo villager, thank you, Young Master Chen! A living Bodhisattva! Soon we'll carve you an inscribed board reading 'Aiding the Imperiled and Assisting the Destitute'—how's that?!"
Chen Haoyu chuckled: "Carve whatever you please—for now step aside, got business with Zhu Shuzhen!"
Grinning obsequious, the village head backed off.
Cutting his eyes back to Xiaguo, Chen Haoyu told Zhu Shuzhen: "I buy back the grove—AND you hand over that maid!"
Holding down her fury, Qianli Xun snapped: "Keep dreaming! Take the grove—touch one hair on my maid and see what happens!"
"What if I insist on taking her today? What are you gonna do about it?!"
"And what if I refuse to give her up? What are YOU gonna do about it?!"
Chen Haoyu roared with laughter: "Hahaha! Think you're tough, right? Got the guts to spar my master fighter here? Beat him, and I leave the maid alone! Lose, and you hand her right over along with the grove!"
Sizing up the scarred, muscle-bound giant with mean eyes—clearly a deadly brawler—Qianli Xun refused to blink. Backing down wasn't an option.
"Done! I'll spar your fighter—and when I win, you accept three terms!"
"Let's hear 'em before I agree!"
"When I win: first, never collect rent from these bamboo villagers again! Second, drop every scheme on Xiaguo! Third, every time you see me from now on, bow polite, say hello, then scram out of my sight! Deal?"
Term one meant peanuts—this grove made pocket change anyway. Term two he had already offered. Term three boiled his blood—total public humiliation! Never in his gilded life had anyone insulted him like that!
About to explode, he glanced at his massive hired bruiser, then at slender Zhu Shuzhen—one punch would fold her flat! Fury flipped straight into a vicious grin: "Deal! You're on!"
Stepping out of the arbor into open dirt, the bruiser flexed his inner force—rip!—shredding his shirt to scraps as knotted muscles twitched like coiled iron, sending shivers through everyone watching.
No way Miss beats a monster like that! Kuqiao panicked while Xiaguo trembled in dread lest Miss get hurt on her account.
Leaning close, Matron Wei whispered: "Young lady, maybe let him have Xiaguo? From what I know, girls Chen Haoyu fancies either stay in his household as maids or get sent home with silver—brothel rumors are likely talk. Pretty as Xiaguo is, he'd keep her around as a maid; no sense risking injury over her!"
Whispering back, Qianli Xun winked: "Relax, Matron—flashy muscles don't always win fights! Besides, it isn't just for Xiaguo—Chen Haoyu swallowed our grove, time to make him spit something back out for our pride and the villagers' sake!"
Rising, she stepped out past the arbor facing the giant—the contrast between slender lady and hulking brute couldn't look starker.
Chen Haoyu jeered: "Still time to forfeit, Zhu Shuzhen!"
Ignoring him, before Qianli Xun even threw a probing jab, the bruiser surged forward like an avalanche—fierce punches and sweeping kicks packing bone-crushing power! Slipping aside with crisp footwork, Qianli Xun flowed into classic Qianli Fist deflections, turning raw force aside with supple finesse.
Shock flickered across the fighter's eyes—this frail-looking woman wielded razor-sharp skill! Dropping cockiness at once, he poured full force into his attack.
Even dodging and countering clean, colliding with mountain-heavy power taxed her arms and stamina—pure finesse alone wouldn't topple brute bulk.
Matron Wei, Kuqiao, and Xiaguo watched with hearts jammed in their throats, sweat glistening on their brows.
Seeing Zhu Shuzhen pressed back, Chen Haoyu crowed inside: Might not fight well myself, plenty of heavyweights out there who can crush you flat!
